\displaystyle=-\gamma\varepsilon^{2}\Gamma^{\gamma-1}_{+}(\left|v-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|v-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|v-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}-1\right)\left(v_{1}^{2}-\left(v_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{3}\right)},

(4.43)

where
\Gamma
is defined in (
1.6
) and
\varrho_{2}
is defined in (
A.3
). Note that (
A.2
) gives that
\varrho_{2}\left(r\right)/r^{2}
is bounded at
r=0
. Additionally, for
v\in B_{\rho}\left(q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{v_{2}}{\left(v_{1}-z_{1}\right)^{2}+v_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{v_{2}-q^{\prime}}{\left(v_{1}-z_{1}\right)^{2}+v_{2}^{2}}+\frac{q^{\prime}}{\left(v_{1}-z_{1}\right)^{2}+v_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{2}\right)}+\frac{q^{\prime}}{z_{1}^{2}-2z_{1}v_{1}+v_{1}^{2}+\left(v_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}+2q^{\prime}\left(v_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)+\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{2}}.

(4.44)

Furthermore, for
v\in B_{\rho}\left(q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)
, and
\varepsilon>0
small enough the last term on the right hand side of (
4.44
) can be written as

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\prime}}{z_{1}^{2}-2z_{1}v_{1}+v_{1}^{2}+\left(v_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}+2q^{\prime}\left(v_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)+\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{q^{\prime}}{z_{1}^{2}+\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\frac{1}{1-\frac{2z_{1}v_{1}}{z_{1}^{2}+\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{2}}+\frac{v_{1}^{2}}{z_{1}^{2}+\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{2}}+\frac{\left(v_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}}{z_{1}^{2}+\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{2}}+\frac{2q^{\prime}\left(v_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)}{z_{1}^{2}+\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{q^{\prime}}{z_{1}^{2}+\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\left(1+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon\right)}\right).

(4.45)

Therefore, for
\varepsilon>0
small enough, we can use (
4.43
)–(
4.45
) to see that

\displaystyle\frac{v_{2}}{\left(v_{1}-z_{1}\right)^{2}+v_{2}^{2}}\left(V\psi_{q^{\prime}}-E+N[\psi_{q^{\prime}}]\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{2}\right)}+\frac{q^{\prime}}{z_{1}^{2}+\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\right)\left(-\gamma\varepsilon^{2}\Gamma^{\gamma-1}_{+}(\left|v-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|v-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|v-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}-1\right)\left(v_{1}^{2}-\left(v_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{4}\right)}.

(4.46)

Then, using (
4.46
), we can write (
4.42
) as

\displaystyle\partial_{2}\psi_{q^{\prime}}\left(z_{1},0\right)=-\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{q^{\prime}}{z_{1}^{2}+\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\int_{B_{1}\left(0\right)}\gamma\varepsilon^{2}\Gamma^{\gamma-1}_{+}(\left|v\right|)\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|v\right|)}{\left|v\right|^{2}}-1\right)\left(v_{1}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}\right)dv+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{4}\right)},

(4.47)

where we have used the change of variables
v\mapsto v-q^{\prime}e_{2}
on the integral on the right hand side.

However, we can see the first term on the right hand side of (
4.47
) is 0, as we are integrating a sum of mode
2
terms on the unit ball. Thus

\displaystyle\partial_{2}\psi_{q^{\prime}}\left(z_{1},0\right)=\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{4}\right)},

which, by (
4.39
) and (
4.40
) implies

\displaystyle\left\|\beta_{1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}\leq C\varepsilon^{4}.

To bound
\beta_{2}
effectively, we first note that since

\displaystyle-\Gamma\left(\frac{2qe_{2}}{\varepsilon}\right)-cq=\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega,

we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle=\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(2\partial_{2}\Gamma\left(y+q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)+\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(c\right)\varepsilon y_{2}+\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left(\partial_{q^{\prime}}\Omega\right)+c\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle=\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(2\partial_{q^{\prime}}\Gamma\left(y+q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)-2\partial_{q^{\prime}}\Gamma\left(2q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)+\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(c\right)\varepsilon\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right).

Noting that we can write

\displaystyle 2\partial_{q^{\prime}}\Gamma\left(y+q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)-2\partial_{q^{\prime}}\Gamma\left(2q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)=\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Gamma\left(z+2q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)-\Gamma\left(2q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)\right)\bigg\rvert_{z=y-q^{\prime}e_{2}},

we can use (
4.34
)–(
4.37
) to conclude that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}=-\varepsilon\partial_{q^{\prime}}g\left(q^{\prime}\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)-\frac{m}{4\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{3}}\left(y_{1}^{2}-\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{4}\right)}.

Now, a slight adjustment to the linear theory developed in
vortex-pair-paper
to take in to account the potential
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)
instead of
V
defined in (
4.41
) implies the bound

\displaystyle\left\|\beta_{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}\leq\left\|\mathcal{E}-\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\beta_{1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}.

Next, since the operator
\Delta+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
is to main order
\Delta+\gamma\Gamma_{+}^{\gamma-1}
, we can use the Lemma
A.3
to deduce that

\displaystyle\beta_{2}\left(y_{1},y_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\partial_{q^{\prime}}g\left(q^{\prime}\right)\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)-\frac{m}{4\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{3}}\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}\left(y_{1}^{2}-\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{4}\right)},

where
\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2}
are defined in (
A.3
). Therefore, on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}\right)^{+}
we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left(\partial_{q^{\prime}}\psi_{q^{\prime}}+\partial_{2}\psi_{q^{\prime}}\right)=\varepsilon\left(\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{q^{\prime}}g\left(q^{\prime}\right)\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{m\varepsilon}{4\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{3}}\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}\left(y_{1}^{2}-\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{5}\right)}.

(4.48)

Combining (
4.38
) and (
4.48
), we have, on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mathcal{R}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{q^{\prime}}g\left(q^{\prime}\right)\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}+1\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)-\frac{m\varepsilon}{4\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{3}}\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+1\right)\left(y_{1}^{2}-\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{5}\right)}.

(4.49)

Due to symmetry, the exact same behaviour is true in coordinates
y+q^{\prime}e_{2}
for
\varepsilon\mathcal{R}_{2}
on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{-}
, as required.
∎

The calculations presented in (
4.36
)–(
4.49
) also give a bound on the variation of the vortex pair with respect to
q^{\prime}
after translation by
q^{\prime}e_{2}
. This bound will be useful later on in the construction so we record it here separately for convenience.

Lemma 4.4
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
small enough, on the support of
U_{R}
restricted to
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Gamma\left(z\right)-\Gamma\left(z+2q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)+\psi_{q^{\prime}}\left(z+q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)\right|\bigg\rvert_{z=y-q^{\prime}e_{2}}\leq C\varepsilon^{3}.

An analogous statement holds on the support of
U_{R}
restricted to the lower half plane, and on the support of
U_{L}
, in the appropriate coordinates.

With Lemma
4.4
in hand, let us define some related quantities for convenience that we will use later on. First, note that using (
2.7
), we can write, on the upper half plane,

\displaystyle U_{R}=f_{\varepsilon}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)=\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)^{\gamma}_{+}\chi_{B_{\frac{s}{\varepsilon}}\left(q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)}(y)

\displaystyle=\left(\Gamma\left(z\right)+\frac{m}{2}\log{\left(1+\frac{z_{2}}{q^{\prime}}+\frac{\left|z\right|^{2}}{4\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\right)}-c\left(q\right)\varepsilon z_{2}+\psi_{q^{\prime}}(z+q^{\prime}e_{2})\right)^{\gamma}_{+}\chi_{B_{\frac{s}{\varepsilon}}\left(0\right)}(z)\bigg\rvert_{z=y-q^{\prime}e_{2}},

(4.50)

where we write
c(q)
to emphasise its dependence on
q
. Accordingly, we define

\displaystyle\mathscr{V}\left(z,\mathcal{Q}\right)=\Gamma\left(z\right)+\frac{m}{2}\log{\left(1+\frac{z_{2}}{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}+\frac{\left|z\right|^{2}}{4\left(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\right)}-c\left(\mathcal{Q}\right)\varepsilon z_{2}+\psi_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}(z+\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}e_{2})

(4.51)

and

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,\mathcal{Q}\right)\right)=\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\right)=\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,\mathcal{Q}\right)\right)^{\gamma}_{+}\chi_{B_{\frac{s}{\varepsilon}}\left(0\right)}(z),

(4.52)

so that

\displaystyle U_{R}\left(y,t\right)=\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},q\right)\right).

We also define
\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{0}\left(z,\mathcal{Q}\right)=\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,\mathcal{Q}\right)\right)
so that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{0}\left(z,\mathcal{Q}\right)=\left(\Gamma\left(z\right)+\frac{m}{2}\log{\left(1+\frac{z_{2}}{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}+\frac{\left|z\right|^{2}}{4\left(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\right)}-c\left(\mathcal{Q}\right)\varepsilon z_{2}+\psi_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}(z+\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}e_{2})\right)^{\gamma}_{+}\chi_{B_{\frac{s}{\varepsilon}}\left(0\right)}(z),

(4.53)

We construct
\mathscr{W}_{1}
from
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
using
\mathscr{V}
completely analogously, as well as
\mathscr{W}_{2}
from

\displaystyle\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}=\gamma\left(\gamma-1\right)\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)^{\gamma-2}_{+}\chi_{B_{\frac{s}{\varepsilon}}\left(q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)}(y),

(4.54)

and so on for further derivatives of
f_{\varepsilon}
.
We similarly define
\mathscr{Z}_{1}\left(z,\mathcal{Q}\right)
and
\mathscr{Z}_{2}\left(z,\mathcal{Q}\right)
by applying the change of variables
z=y-q^{\prime}e_{2}
to
\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}
and
\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)
respectively:

\displaystyle\mathscr{Z}_{1}\left(z,\mathcal{Q}\right)

\displaystyle=\partial_{1}\Gamma\left(z\right)+\frac{my_{1}}{4\left(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\right)^{2}\left(1+\frac{z_{2}}{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}+\frac{\left|z\right|^{2}}{4\left(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\right)}+\partial_{1}\psi_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}\left(z+\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}e_{2}\right),

(4.55)

\displaystyle\mathscr{Z}_{2}\left(z,\mathcal{Q}\right)

\displaystyle=-\partial_{2}\Gamma\left(z\right)-\frac{m\left(\frac{1}{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}+\frac{2z_{2}}{4\left(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\right)}{2\left(1+\frac{z_{2}}{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}+\frac{\left|z\right|^{2}}{4\left(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\right)}-\frac{m\left(\frac{z_{2}}{\left(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}+\frac{\left|z\right|^{2}}{2\left(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\right)^{3}}\right)}{2\left(1+\frac{z_{2}}{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}+\frac{\left|z\right|^{2}}{4\left(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\right)}-\partial_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}c\left(\mathcal{Q}\right)+\partial_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}\left(z+\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}e_{2}\right).

Much like the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
, for
\xi
satisfying (
4.6
)–(
4.11
),
\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\right)
is supported on the ball
B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)
, for
\rho>0
defined in (
4.17
), with respect to the
z
coordinate. Furthermore, as we shift only in the
e_{2}
direction to define
\mathscr{W}_{0}
,
\mathscr{Z}_{1}
, and
\mathscr{Z}_{2}
, they retain the same parities with respect to
y_{1}
as
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
,
\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}
, and
\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)
. That is,
\mathscr{W}_{0}
and
\mathscr{Z}_{2}
are even in
y_{1}
and
\mathscr{Z}_{1}
is odd in
y_{1}
.

Due to (
4.6
)–(
4.9
), and Lemmas
4.1
and
4.4
, on
\left[T_{0},T\right]
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}\right)^{+}-\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\right)\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{4}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t^{2}},\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}

(4.56)

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}-\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\right)\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{4}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t^{2}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+},

(4.57)

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}-\mathscr{Z}_{1}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},q_{0}\right)\right|+\left|\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)-\mathscr{Z}_{2}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},q_{0}\right)\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{4}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t^{2}}.

(4.58)

with analogous estimates for the difference between
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}
and
\mathscr{W}_{2}
and higher derivatives of
f_{\varepsilon}
. Note that the bounds on the hand right sides of (
4.56
)–(
4.57
) also clearly hold when derivatives of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}\right)^{+}
are replaced with derivatives of
\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\right)
.

From the right hand side of (
4.49
) in the proof of Lemma
4.3
, we can see that there are mode
1
and
2
terms coming from
\varepsilon\mathcal{R}_{1}
and
\varepsilon\mathcal{R}_{2}
whose sizes are of order
\varepsilon^{4}
on the support of
\left(f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\right)^{+}
and
\left(f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\right)^{-}
respectively. The following lemma shows that from these terms, we can extract terms of the right form to put on the right hand side of our elliptic improvement, and leave terms that are small enough to be treated as genuine remainder terms in the construction of a first approximation.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
\varepsilon\mathcal{R}_{1}
be as in (
4.31
). Then for all
\varepsilon>0
small enough, on the support of
U_{R}
restricted to the upper half plane we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mathcal{R}_{1}

\displaystyle=\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\frac{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}\left(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|\right)}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{q^{\prime}}g\left(q^{\prime}\right)\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}+1\right)y_{1}\right)

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\frac{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{8\Gamma^{\prime}\left(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|\right)}m\varepsilon\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{-3}\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+1\right)y_{1}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{1},

with
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}=\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{5}\right)}
. By symmetry, an analogous statement is true for
\varepsilon\mathcal{R}_{2}
on the support of
U_{R}
restricted to the lower half plane in coordinates
y+q^{\prime}e_{2}
.

Proof.

We prove the lemma for the first term on the right hand side of (
4.31
) with the proof being analogous for the other term, and for
\varepsilon\mathcal{R}_{2}
by symmetry. On the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
, we let
y-q^{\prime}e_{2}=re^{i\theta}
, so that

\displaystyle\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Gamma\left(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|\right)\right)\cdot\grad_{y}=\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}\left(r\right)}{r}\partial_{\theta}.

Here we are emphasising that
\Gamma
is a radial function in its argument. Then we see that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}+1\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)=\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(r)}{r}+1\right)r\sin{\theta}=-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}\left(r\right)}{r}\partial_{\theta}\left(\frac{r}{\Gamma^{\prime}\left(r\right)}\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(r)}{r}+1\right)r\cos{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Gamma\left(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|\right)\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\frac{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}\left(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|\right)}\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}+1\right)y_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\frac{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}\left(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|\right)}\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}+1\right)y_{1}\right)

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\Gamma\left(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|\right)\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\frac{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}\left(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|\right)}\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}+1\right)y_{1}\right).

From Theorem
2.1
, we know that on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
,

\displaystyle\left|\grad_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\Gamma\left(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|\right)\right)\right|\leq C\varepsilon^{2}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\left|\grad_{y}\left(\frac{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}\left(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|\right)}\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}+1\right)y_{1}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}+1\right)\right|\left|\grad_{y}\left(\frac{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|y_{1}}{\Gamma^{\prime}\left(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|\right)}\right)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\grad_{y}\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}+1\right)\right|\left|\frac{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|y_{1}}{\Gamma^{\prime}\left(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C,

for some constant
C>0
on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
, where we have used Lemma
A.3
for the boundedness of
\varrho_{1}\left(r\right)/r
and its gradient, as well as the fact that
\left|r/\Gamma^{\prime}\right|
and its derivative is bounded. Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\Gamma\left(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|\right)\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\frac{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}\left(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|\right)}\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}+1\right)y_{1}\right)\right|\leq C\varepsilon^{2},

which along with the fact that

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{q^{\prime}}g\right|+\left|\frac{m\varepsilon}{\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{3}}\right|\leq C\varepsilon^{4},

gives us the desired bound.
∎

A key tool in our construction of the first approximation is an understanding of how the quantity

\displaystyle\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)-\varepsilon\dot{q}y_{1}

behaves on the support of
U_{R}
restricted to the upper half plane. The next lemma shows that due to the fact that
\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)
solves (
2.16
), this quantity expands as a sum of operators in
\left(p_{1},q_{1}\right)
and
\left(\tilde{p},\tilde{q}\right)
with good structure, and error terms that have sufficient bounds to be able to construct our first approximation. Recall the definition of
\mathcal{F}
from (
2.14
), and define for
h,k:[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}^{2}

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}\left(h\right)\left[k\right]=\begin{pmatrix}-\mathcal{F}\left(h_{2}+k_{2},h_{1}+k_{1}\right)+\mathcal{F}\left(h_{2},h_{1}\right)-g\left(h_{2}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}\right)+g\left(h_{2}^{\prime}\right)\\
\mathcal{F}\left(h_{1}+k_{1},h_{2}+k_{2}\right)-\mathcal{F}\left(h_{1},h_{2}\right)\\
\end{pmatrix}.

(4.59)

Lemma 4.6
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
small enough, and
t\geq T_{0}
as in (
4.19
), on the support of
U_{R}
restricted to the upper half plane, we have that

\displaystyle\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)-\varepsilon\dot{q}y_{1}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\left[\xi_{1}\right]+\dot{\xi}_{1}\right)\cdot\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)^{\perp}+\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\left[\tilde{\xi}\right]+\dot{\tilde{\xi}}\right)\cdot\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)^{\perp}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{E}_{R1}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p,q\right)+\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{0}\left(p,q\right),

(4.60)

where, in coordinates
y-q^{\prime}e_{2}
,
\mathcal{E}_{R1}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p,q\right)
is

•

of order
\varepsilon^{5}t^{-2}
at mode
1
,

•

of order
\varepsilon^{2}t^{-2}
at mode
2
,

•

of order
\varepsilon^{3}t^{-3}
at mode
3
,

•

order
\varepsilon^{4}t^{-4}
at mode
4
,

with no other modes appearing,

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{2}\right)\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}},

and
\mathcal{C}_{0}
a
\left(p,q\right)
dependent number. An analogous statement holds on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{-}
in coordinates
y+q^{\prime}e_{2}
, and for
\Psi_{R}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)\left(w_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)-\varepsilon\dot{q}w_{1}
on the support of
U_{L}
restricted to the upper half plane in coordinates
\varepsilon w=x+pe_{1}
.

Proof.

First we note that on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
,

\displaystyle\Psi_{L}

\displaystyle=\Gamma\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}+2p^{\prime}e_{1}\right)-\Gamma\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}+2p^{\prime}e_{1}+2q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)+\psi_{q^{\prime}}\left(y+2p^{\prime}e_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{m}{2}\log{\left(1+\frac{\varepsilon y_{1}}{p}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}{4p^{2}}\right)}+\frac{m}{2}\log{\left(1+\frac{\varepsilon\left(py_{1}+q\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)}{p^{2}+q^{2}}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}{4\left(p^{2}+q^{2}\right)}\right)}

\displaystyle-\frac{m}{2}\log{\left(\frac{p^{2}+q^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)}+\psi_{q^{\prime}}\left(y+2p^{\prime}e_{1}\right).

(4.61)

We first concentrate on the first two terms on the right hand side of (
4.61
). On the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}

\displaystyle-\frac{m}{2}\log{\left(1+\frac{\varepsilon y_{1}}{p}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}{4p^{2}}\right)}=

\displaystyle-\frac{m}{2}\left(\frac{\varepsilon y_{1}}{p}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}{4p^{2}}-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}y_{1}^{2}}{2p^{2}}-\frac{\varepsilon^{3}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}y_{1}}{4p^{3}}+\frac{\varepsilon^{3}y_{1}^{3}}{3p^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{m}{2}\left(-\frac{\varepsilon^{4}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{4}}{32p^{4}}+\frac{\varepsilon^{4}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}y_{1}^{2}}{4p^{4}}-\frac{\varepsilon^{4}y_{1}^{4}}{4p^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{R}_{3},

(4.62)

where
\mathcal{R}_{3}=\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{5}t^{-5}\right)}
. We see that (
4.62
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle-\frac{m}{2}\log{\left(1+\frac{\varepsilon y_{1}}{p}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}{4p^{2}}\right)}=

\displaystyle-\frac{m}{2}\left(\frac{\varepsilon y_{1}}{p}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\left(\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}-y_{1}^{2}\right)}{4p^{2}}-\frac{\varepsilon^{3}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}y_{1}}{4p^{3}}+\frac{\varepsilon^{3}y_{1}^{3}}{3p^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{m}{2}\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{4}\left(\left(y_{1}^{2}-\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)^{2}-4y_{1}^{2}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)}{32p^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{R}_{3}.

(4.63)

Next we have that

\displaystyle\frac{m}{2}\log{\left(1+\frac{\varepsilon\left(py_{1}+q\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)}{p^{2}+q^{2}}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}{4\left(p^{2}+q^{2}\right)}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{m}{2}\left(\frac{\varepsilon\left(py_{1}+q\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)}{p^{2}+q^{2}}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}{4\left(p^{2}+q^{2}\right)}-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\left(py_{1}+q\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)^{2}}{2\left(p^{2}+q^{2}\right)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{m}{2}\left(-\frac{\varepsilon^{3}\left(py_{1}+q\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}{4\left(p^{2}+q^{2}\right)^{2}}+\frac{\varepsilon^{3}\left(py_{1}+q\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)^{3}}{3\left(p^{2}+q^{2}\right)^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{m}{2}\left(-\frac{\varepsilon^{4}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{4}}{32\left(p^{2}+q^{2}\right)^{2}}+\frac{\varepsilon^{4}\left(py_{1}+q\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)^{2}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}{4\left(p^{2}+q^{2}\right)^{3}}-\frac{\varepsilon^{4}\left(py_{1}+q\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)^{4}}{4\left(p^{2}+q^{2}\right)^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{R}_{4},

(4.64)

where, again,
\mathcal{R}_{4}=\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{5}t^{-5}\right)}
on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
. As with (
4.62
)–(
4.63
), we can rewrite (
4.64
) as

\displaystyle\frac{m}{2}\log{\left(1+\frac{\varepsilon\left(py_{1}+q\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)}{p^{2}+q^{2}}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}{4\left(p^{2}+q^{2}\right)}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{m}{2}\left(\frac{\varepsilon\left(py_{1}+q\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)}{p^{2}+q^{2}}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\left(\left(q^{2}-p^{2}\right)\left(y_{1}^{2}-\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)-4pqy_{1}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)}{4\left(p^{2}+q^{2}\right)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{m}{2}\left(-\frac{\varepsilon^{3}\left(py_{1}+q\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}{4\left(p^{2}+q^{2}\right)^{2}}+\frac{\varepsilon^{3}\left(py_{1}+q\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)^{3}}{3\left(p^{2}+q^{2}\right)^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{m}{2}\left(-\frac{\varepsilon^{4}\left(\left(\left(y_{1}^{2}-\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)^{2}-4y_{1}^{2}\left(y-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)\left(\left(p^{2}-q^{2}\right)^{2}-4p^{2}q^{2}\right)\right)}{32\left(p^{2}+q^{2}\right)^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{m}{2}\left(-\frac{2\varepsilon^{4}pq\left(q^{2}-p^{2}\right)\left(y_{1}^{2}-\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)y_{1}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)}{32\left(p^{2}+q^{2}\right)^{4}}\right)+\mathcal{R}_{4},

For
\psi_{q^{\prime}}\left(y+2p^{\prime}e_{1}\right)
, once again using the fact that
\psi_{q^{\prime}}
solves (

), we have

\displaystyle\psi_{q^{\prime}}\left(y+2p^{\prime}e_{1}\right)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\log{\left(\frac{\left|z-\overline{\left(y+2p^{\prime}e_{1}\right)}\right|^{2}}{\left|z-\left(y+2p^{\prime}e_{1}\right)\right|^{2}}\right)}\left(V\psi_{q^{\prime}}-E+N[\psi_{q^{\prime}}]\right)dz.

(4.65)

The logarithm in the integrand can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\log{\left(\frac{\left|z-\overline{\left(y+2p^{\prime}e_{1}\right)}\right|^{2}}{\left|z-\left(y+2p^{\prime}e_{1}\right)\right|^{2}}\right)}=\log{\left(1+\frac{4\left(\left(z_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)+q^{\prime}\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)+4q^{\prime}\left(\left(z_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)+q^{\prime}\right)}{\left(\left(z_{1}-y_{1}\right)+2p^{\prime}\right)^{2}+\left(\left(z_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)-\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)^{2}}\right)}.

(4.66)

Then, as
V\psi_{q^{\prime}}+E-N[\psi_{q^{\prime}}]

B_{\rho}\left(q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)
by (

),

can be taken to be in

. The same is true for

as we are considering
\psi_{q^{\prime}}\left(y+2p^{\prime}e_{1}\right)
on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
. Thus, for small enough

, we can use (

)–(

\displaystyle\frac{4\left(\left(z_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)+q^{\prime}\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)+4q^{\prime}\left(\left(z_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)+q^{\prime}\right)}{\left(\left(z_{1}-y_{1}\right)+2p^{\prime}\right)^{2}+\left(\left(z_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)-\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\left(z_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)}{p^{2}}\frac{1}{1+\frac{\varepsilon\left(z_{1}-y_{1}\right)}{p}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\left|\left(z-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)-\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)\right|^{2}}{4p^{2}}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\varepsilon q\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)}{p^{2}}\frac{1}{1+\frac{\varepsilon\left(z_{1}-y_{1}\right)}{p}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\left|\left(z-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)-\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)\right|^{2}}{4p^{2}}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\varepsilon q\left(z_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)}{p^{2}}\frac{1}{1+\frac{\varepsilon\left(z_{1}-y_{1}\right)}{p}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\left|\left(z-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)-\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)\right|^{2}}{4p^{2}}}

\displaystyle+\frac{q^{2}}{p^{2}}\frac{1}{1+\frac{\varepsilon\left(z_{1}-y_{1}\right)}{p}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\left|\left(z-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)-\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)\right|^{2}}{4p^{2}}}.

\displaystyle\Lambda_{ij}\left(q^{\prime}\right)\coloneqq\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}z_{1}^{i}\left(z_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{j}\left(V\psi_{q^{\prime}}-E+N[\psi_{q^{\prime}}]\right)dz=\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{3}\right)},\ \ \left(i,j\right)\in\left\{\left(1,0\right),\left(0,1\right)\right\},

\displaystyle\Lambda_{ij}\left(q^{\prime}\right)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}z_{1}^{i}\left(z_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{j}\left(V\psi_{q^{\prime}}-E+N[\psi_{q^{\prime}}]\right)dz=\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{2}\right)},\ \ \left(i,j\right)\in\left\{\left(1,1\right),\left(2,0\right),\left(0,2\right)\right\}.

(4.67)

Using (

)–(

\displaystyle+\mathcal{R}_{5},

where

. Therefore, we have that on the support of

\displaystyle+\mathcal{E}_{2}+\mathcal{E}_{3}+\mathcal{E}_{4}+\mathcal{R}_{3}+\mathcal{R}_{4}+\mathcal{R}_{5}+\mathcal{C}_{0},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p,q\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{m}{2}\left(-\frac{\varepsilon^{4}\left(\left(\left(y_{1}^{2}-\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)^{2}-4y_{1}^{2}\left(y-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)\left(\left(p^{2}-q^{2}\right)^{2}-4p^{2}q^{2}\right)\right)}{32\left(p^{2}+q^{2}\right)^{4}}\right)

Now, concentrating on the first two terms on the right hand side of (

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\left(-\mathcal{F}\left(q_{0},p_{0}\right)-g\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)+\dot{p}_{0}\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)-\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{F}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)+\dot{q}_{0}\right)y_{1}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\left(-\mathcal{F}\left(q_{0}+q_{1},p_{0}+p_{1}\right)+\mathcal{F}\left(q_{0},p_{0}\right)-g\left(q_{0}^{\prime}+q_{1}^{\prime}\right)+g\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)+\dot{p}_{1}\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{F}\left(p_{0}+p_{1},q_{0}+q_{1}\right)-\mathcal{F}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)+\dot{q}_{1}\right)y_{1}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\left(-\mathcal{F}\left(q_{0}+q_{1}+\tilde{q},p_{0}+p_{1}+\tilde{p}^{\prime}\right)+\mathcal{F}\left(q_{0}+q_{1},p_{0}+p_{1}\right)-g\left(q_{0}^{\prime}+q_{1}^{\prime}+\tilde{q}^{\prime}\right)+g\left(q_{0}^{\prime}+q_{1}^{\prime}\right)+\dot{\tilde{p}}\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{F}\left(p_{0}+p_{1}+\tilde{p},q_{0}+q_{1}+\tilde{q}\right)-\mathcal{F}\left(p_{0}+p_{1},q_{0}+q_{1}\right)+\dot{\tilde{q}}\right)y_{1}.

(4.72)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left(-\mathcal{F}\left(q,p\right)-g\left(q^{\prime}\right)+\dot{p}\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)-\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{F}\left(p,q\right)+\dot{q}\right)y_{1}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\left(-\mathcal{F}\left(q_{0}+q_{1},p_{0}+p_{1}\right)+\mathcal{F}\left(q_{0},p_{0}\right)-g\left(q_{0}^{\prime}+q_{1}^{\prime}\right)+g\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)+\dot{p}_{1}\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{F}\left(p_{0}+p_{1},q_{0}+q_{1}\right)-\mathcal{F}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)+\dot{q}_{1}\right)y_{1}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\left(-\mathcal{F}\left(q_{0}+q_{1}+\tilde{q},p_{0}+p_{1}+\tilde{p}^{\prime}\right)+\mathcal{F}\left(q_{0}+q_{1},p_{0}+p_{1}\right)-g\left(q_{0}^{\prime}+q_{1}^{\prime}+\tilde{q}^{\prime}\right)+g\left(q_{0}^{\prime}+q_{1}^{\prime}\right)+\dot{\tilde{p}}\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{F}\left(p_{0}+p_{1}+\tilde{p},q_{0}+q_{1}+\tilde{q}\right)-\mathcal{F}\left(p_{0}+p_{1},q_{0}+q_{1}\right)+\dot{\tilde{q}}\right)y_{1}.

Letting

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{F}\left(p_{0}+p_{1},q_{0}+q_{1}\right)-\mathcal{F}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)+\dot{q}_{1}\right)y_{1}

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{F}\left(p_{0}+p_{1}+\tilde{p},q_{0}+q_{1}+\tilde{q}\right)-\mathcal{F}\left(p_{0}+p_{1},q_{0}+q_{1}\right)+\dot{\tilde{q}}\right)y_{1}+\mathcal{E}_{R1}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p,q\right)+\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{0},

(4.73)

and

Now we construct the first approximation. Recall the definitions of

Theorem 4.7
.

Let
t\in\left[T_{0},T\right]
as in (
4.19
)–(
4.20
). Then for all
\varepsilon>0
small enough, there exists

satisfying (

)–(

,

,

,

,

restricted to the upper half plane,

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{R1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}+\left\|\nabla\psi_{R1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}+\left\|\phi_{R1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{2}}{t^{2}},

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{R2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}+\left\|\nabla\psi_{R2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}+\left\|\phi_{R2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{4}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t^{2}},

with analogous estimates on the support of
U_{R}
restricted to the lower half plane, and for
\psi_{Lk}
,
\phi_{Lk}
,
k=1,2
on the support of

,

\displaystyle\left\|\psi^{out}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}+\left\|\nabla\psi^{out}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{4}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t^{3}},

such that if
\psi_{R}=\psi_{R1}+\psi_{R2}
,

in (

)–(
4.24
), and analogously for
\psi_{L}
and
\phi_{L}
, we have, on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}

\displaystyle E_{R1}\left(\phi_{R},\psi_{R},\psi^{out};\xi\right)

\displaystyle=\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\varepsilon\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\left[\tilde{\xi}\right]+\dot{\tilde{\xi}}\right)\cdot\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{1}\left(y,\xi\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{2}\left(y,\xi\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{3}\left(y,\xi\right),

where

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{1}\left(y,\xi\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}},

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{2}\left(y,\xi\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}},

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{3}\left(y,\xi\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}},

and analogously for

on the lower half plane, as well as for

, and finally,

\displaystyle E_{2}^{out}\left(\phi^{in},\psi^{in},\psi^{out};\xi\right)=\mathcal{O}{\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{4}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t^{3}}\right)}.

The construction will essentially proceed in five steps. We will first rewrite
E_{R1}
from its form in Lemma
4.3
to more easily complete the second step, which is constructing an elliptic improvement of size
\varepsilon^{2}t^{-2}
on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
. We will then use this first elliptic improvement to construct
\psi^{out}
. Then we will rewrite
E_{R1}
again in order to use the errors
\psi_{R1}
and
\psi^{out}
produce to both construct a second elliptic improvement of size
\varepsilon^{4}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|t^{-2}
, and solve a certain system of ODEs for
\xi_{1}
that will make sure the second elliptic improvement does not produce any errors that are too large.

Proof.

Step I: Rewriting
E_{R1}
Part A

To construct our first elliptic improvement, we must first rewrite (
4.30
) in an amenable manner using Lemmas
4.5
and
4.6
. We decompose
\phi_{R}
and
\psi_{R}
as

\displaystyle\phi_{R}

\displaystyle=\phi_{R1}+\phi_{R2},\quad\psi_{R}=\psi_{R1}+\psi_{R2},

\displaystyle-\Delta\psi_{Rk}

\displaystyle=\phi_{Rk},\ k=1,2,

where the last line is understood to mean solving in
L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)
, on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
, with boundary condition given by
\psi_{Rk}\left(y_{1},0\right)\equiv 0
. Then we can rewrite (
4.30
) as

\displaystyle E_{R1}

\displaystyle=\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\phi_{R1}-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}\right)+\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\phi_{R2}-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R1}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}+\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R1}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R2}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R2}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}\right)

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\Delta\psi_{R1}+f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\psi_{R1}\right)-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\Delta\psi_{R2}+f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\psi_{R2}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)\left(y_{2}-\sgn{\left(y_{2}\right)}q^{\prime}\right)-\varepsilon\dot{q}\sgn{\left(y_{2}\right)}y_{1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi^{out}\cdot\grad_{y}\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R1}\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi^{out}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}+\dot{q}\left(\varepsilon\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{1}+\varepsilon\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{-}\mathcal{R}_{2}\right).

As we will construct
\psi_{Rk}
,
k=1,2
, and
\psi^{out}
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
, we are also free to consider
E_{R1}
only on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
, and then Theorem
4.7
will follow by oddness in
y_{2}
. In this setting, using Lemma
4.5
, we have, on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
,

\displaystyle E_{R1}

\displaystyle=\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\phi_{R1}-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}\right)+\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\phi_{R2}-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R1}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}+\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R1}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R2}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R2}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}\right)

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\Delta\psi_{R1}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\psi_{R1}\right)-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\Delta\psi_{R2}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\psi_{R2}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)-\varepsilon\dot{q}y_{1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\dot{q}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{q^{\prime}}g\left(q^{\prime}\right)\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}+1\right)y_{1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}}\frac{m\varepsilon^{4}\dot{q}}{8q^{3}}\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+1\right)y_{1}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi^{out}\cdot\grad_{y}\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R1}\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi^{out}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}+\dot{q}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}.

(4.74)

We first note that by the definition of
q(t)
given in (
4.5
)–(
4.11
) and the bound for
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}
in Lemma
4.5
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\dot{q}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}}{t^{3}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}.

Next we use Lemma
4.6
to rewrite (
4.74
) as

\displaystyle E_{R1}

\displaystyle=\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\phi_{R1}-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}\right)+\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\phi_{R2}-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R1}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}+\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R1}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R2}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R2}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}\right)

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\Delta\psi_{R1}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\psi_{R1}-\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{E}_{2}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p,q\right)+\mathcal{E}_{3}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p,q\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\varepsilon\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\left[\xi_{1}\right]+\dot{\xi}_{1}\right)\cdot\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\varepsilon\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\left[\tilde{\xi}\right]+\dot{\tilde{\xi}}\right)\cdot\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\Delta\psi_{R2}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\psi_{R2}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{E}_{4}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p,q\right)+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\Lambda_{01}}{p^{2}}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)+\frac{\varepsilon q\Lambda_{00}}{p^{2}}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\dot{q}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{q^{\prime}}g\left(q^{\prime}\right)\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}+1\right)y_{1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}}\frac{m\varepsilon^{4}\dot{q}}{8q^{3}}\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+1\right)y_{1}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi^{out}\cdot\grad_{y}\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R1}\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi^{out}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}+\dot{q}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{2}\right),

(4.75)

where from Lemma
4.6
, we know

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{2}\right)\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}},

and we have also used the fact that
\mathcal{E}_{R1}=\mathcal{E}_{2}+\mathcal{E}_{3}+\mathcal{E}_{4}
, with the terms on the right hand side defined in (
4.69
)–(
4.71
), and the dependence of each summand on
\left(p,q\right)
emphasised.

Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{6}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}_{2}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p,q\right)-\mathcal{E}_{2}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p_{0},q_{0}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{7}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}_{3}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p,q\right)-\mathcal{E}_{3}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p_{0},q_{0}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{8}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}_{4}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p,q\right)-\mathcal{E}_{4}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p_{0},q_{0}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{9}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\Lambda_{01}\left(q^{\prime}\right)}{p^{2}}+\frac{\varepsilon q\Lambda_{00}\left(q^{\prime}\right)}{p^{2}}\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)-\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\Lambda_{01}\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)}{p_{0}^{2}}+\frac{\varepsilon q_{0}\Lambda_{00}\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)}{p_{0}^{2}}\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right).

Note that for
\Lambda_{00}
and
\Lambda_{01}
defined (
4.67
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\Lambda_{00}\left(q^{\prime}\right)-\Lambda_{00}\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)\right|+\left|\Lambda_{01}\left(q^{\prime}\right)-\Lambda_{01}\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)\right|\leq C\varepsilon^{3}\left|\frac{q-q_{0}}{\varepsilon}\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{4}}{t^{2}}.

(4.76)

Here, the first inequality comes from making a changing of variables
v=z-q^{\prime}e_{2}
in the integrals defining
\Lambda_{00}
and
\Lambda_{01}
in (
4.67
), and then using Lemma
4.4
. The second inequality comes from the bounds (
4.6
)–(
4.11
).

Using(
4.76
), (
4.69
), (
4.71
), and Lemma
4.1
, we have that

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\mathcal{R}_{6}+\mathcal{R}_{7}+\mathcal{R}_{8}+\mathcal{R}_{9}\right)\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}}.

Next we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{10}

\displaystyle=\left(\varepsilon^{2}\dot{q}\partial_{q^{\prime}}g\left(q^{\prime}\right)-\varepsilon^{2}\dot{q}_{0}\partial_{q^{\prime}}g\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)\right)\frac{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}+1\right)y_{1},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{11}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{m\varepsilon^{4}\dot{q}}{8q^{3}}-\frac{m\varepsilon^{4}\dot{q}_{0}}{8q_{0}^{3}}\right)\frac{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+1\right)y_{1}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right).

Then once again using Lemma
4.1
, as well as (
2.12
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\mathcal{R}_{10}+\mathcal{R}_{11}\right)\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}}.

Letting
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{3}=\mathcal{R}_{6}+\mathcal{R}_{7}+\mathcal{R}_{8}+\mathcal{R}_{9}+\mathcal{R}_{10}+\mathcal{R}_{11}
we can rewrite (
4.75
) as

\displaystyle E_{R1}

\displaystyle=\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\phi_{R1}-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}\right)+\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\phi_{R2}-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R1}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}+\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R1}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R2}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R2}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}\right)

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\Delta\psi_{R1}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\psi_{R1}-\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{E}_{2}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p_{0},q_{0}\right)+\mathcal{E}_{3}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p_{0},q_{0}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\Delta\psi_{R2}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\psi_{R2}-\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{E}_{4}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p_{0},q_{0}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\Lambda_{01}\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)}{p_{0}^{2}}+\frac{\varepsilon q_{0}\Lambda_{00}\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)}{p_{0}^{2}}\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\varepsilon\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\left[\xi_{1}\right]+\dot{\xi}_{1}\right)\cdot\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\dot{q}_{0}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{q^{\prime}}g\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}+1\right)y_{1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}}\frac{m\varepsilon^{4}\dot{q}_{0}}{8q_{0}^{3}}\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+1\right)y_{1}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\varepsilon\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\left[\tilde{\xi}\right]+\dot{\tilde{\xi}}\right)\cdot\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi^{out}\cdot\grad_{y}\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R1}\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi^{out}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}+\dot{q}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\right),

(4.77)

with

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{2}\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\right)\right)\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}}.

We are now ready to construct the first elliptic improvement.

Step II: Constructing
\psi_{R1}

We construct
\psi_{R1}
on the upper half plane
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
with boundary condition
\psi_{R1}\left(y_{1},0\right)=0
by solving the equation

\displaystyle\Delta\psi_{R1}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\psi_{R1}

\displaystyle=\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{E}_{2}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p_{0},q_{0}\right)+\mathcal{E}_{3}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p_{0},q_{0}\right)\right)+\alpha_{1}\left(p,q\right)\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}

\displaystyle+\alpha_{2}\left(p,q\right)\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right).

(4.78)

The linear theory for this projected problem set out can be easily adapted from
vortex-pair-paper
, and thus
\psi_{R1}
manifestly exists. By
4.6
as well as (
4.78
) it has bounds

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{R1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}+\left\|\nabla\psi_{R1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}+\left\|\phi_{R1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}+\left|\alpha_{1}\right|+\left|\alpha_{2}\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{2}}{t^{2}}.

However, we can find better bounds for
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
. Noting
\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}
and
\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)
are in the kernel of
\Delta+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
, we first integrate (
4.78
) against
\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}
and obtain

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}\left(p,q\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{E}_{2}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p_{0},q_{0}\right)\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}\ dy

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{E}_{3}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p_{0},q_{0}\right)\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}\ dy.

(4.79)

Now, as
\Psi_{R}
was constructed to be even in
y_{1}
, we have that
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}
is odd in
y_{1}
. Thus, noting the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{2}
and
\mathcal{E}_{3}
in (
4.69
)–(
4.70
), (
4.79
) becomes

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}\left(p,q\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}\right)^{2}\right)=-\frac{m\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}\frac{p_{0}q_{0}y_{1}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)}{\left(p_{0}^{2}+q_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}}dy

\displaystyle+\frac{m\varepsilon^{3}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}\left(\frac{1}{12p_{0}^{3}}+\frac{\left(p_{0}^{3}-3p_{0}q_{0}^{2}\right)}{12\left(p_{0}^{2}+q_{0}^{2}\right)^{3}}\right)\left(4y_{1}^{3}-3\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}y_{1}\right)dy.

(4.80)

Next, noting that

\displaystyle\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}=\partial_{1}\left(\Gamma\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)-\Gamma\left(y+q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)+\psi_{q^{\prime}}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right),

we know from the construction of the vortex pair in
vortex-pair-paper
that on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
in coordinates
y-q^{\prime}e_{2}
,
\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}
is mode
1
to main order, of order
\varepsilon^{3}
at mode
2
and mode
0
, with lower order terms of size
\varepsilon^{4}
. We also know that
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
is, to main order, radial, with size
\varepsilon^{2}
corrections. Combining this with (
4.6
)–(
4.7
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\alpha_{1}\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}}{t^{3}}.

(4.81)

Next, we integrate (
4.78
) against
\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)
. Similarly to (
4.79
)–(
4.80
), we use the fact that
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)
is even in
y_{1}
to obtain

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}\left(p,q\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)\right)^{2}\right)=

\displaystyle-\frac{m\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)\left(\frac{1}{4p_{0}^{2}}-\frac{\left(p_{0}^{2}-q_{0}^{2}\right)}{4\left(p_{0}^{2}+q_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}}\right)\left(y_{1}^{2}-\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)dy

\displaystyle-\frac{m\varepsilon^{3}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)\frac{\left(q_{0}^{3}-3p_{0}^{2}q_{0}\right)}{12\left(p_{0}^{2}+q_{0}^{2}\right)^{3}}\left(3\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)-4\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{3}\right)dy.

(4.82)

The modal considerations when calculating the size of
\alpha_{1}
in (
4.81
) hold for
\alpha_{2}
, and thus from (
4.82
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\left|\alpha_{2}\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}}{t^{2}}.

(4.83)

Moreover, using the change of variables
z=y-q^{\prime}e_{2}
on the integrals on both sides of (
4.82
) as well as Lemma
4.4
and bounds (
4.6
)–(
4.11
), we have that

\displaystyle\left|\alpha_{2}\left(p,q\right)-\alpha_{2}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}}{t^{3}}.

(4.84)

Let
\mathcal{R}_{12}
be defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{12}=\alpha_{1}\left(p,q\right)\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}+\left(\alpha_{2}\left(p,q\right)-\alpha_{2}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)\right)\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right).

(4.85)

Then (
4.81
)–(
4.84
) gives

\displaystyle\left|\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{12}\right)\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}},

recalling
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
is a function of
\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}
.

Next, due to (
4.69
)–(
4.70
) and (
4.81
)–(
4.83
), as well as the fact that
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
is radial at main order, we know that on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
,
\psi_{R1}
is

•

of order
\varepsilon^{2}t^{-2}
at mode
2
,

•

of order
\varepsilon^{3}t^{-3}
at mode
3
,

and of order
\varepsilon^{4}t^{-2}
otherwise. As we know that
\Delta+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
is
\Delta+\gamma\Gamma_{+}^{\gamma-1}
to main order, as well as precise behaviour of
\mathcal{E}_{2}
and
\mathcal{E}_{3}
from (
4.69
)–(
4.70
), we can use Lemma
A.3
to deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{R1}-\mathcal{E}_{2}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)-\mathcal{E}_{3}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)-\alpha_{1}\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}-\alpha_{2}\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{m\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\left(\frac{1}{4p_{0}^{2}}-\frac{\left(p_{0}^{2}-q_{0}^{2}\right)}{4\left(p_{0}^{2}+q_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}-1\right)\left(y_{1}^{2}-\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{m\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\frac{p_{0}q_{0}}{\left(p_{0}^{2}+q_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}}\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}-1\right)y_{1}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{m\varepsilon^{3}}{2}\left(\frac{1}{12p_{0}^{3}}+\frac{\left(p_{0}^{3}-3p_{0}q_{0}^{2}\right)}{12\left(p_{0}^{2}+q_{0}^{2}\right)^{3}}\right)\left(\frac{\varrho_{3}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{3}}-1\right)\left(4y_{1}^{3}-3\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}y_{1}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{m\varepsilon^{3}}{2}\frac{\left(q_{0}^{3}-3p_{0}^{2}q_{0}\right)}{12\left(p_{0}^{2}+q_{0}^{2}\right)^{3}}\left(\frac{\varrho_{3}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{3}}-1\right)\left(4\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{3}-3\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)+\mathcal{R}_{13},

(4.86)

where
\mathcal{R}_{13}=\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{4}t^{-2}\right)}
on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
, with
\varrho_{2},\varrho_{3}
being defined in (
A.3
).

Step III: Constructing
\psi^{out}

Having constructed
\psi_{R1}
with desirable properties, we can construct
\psi_{L1}
for the left-hand vortex pair in a completely symmetric manner by reflecting in
x_{1}
. Having
\psi_{R1}
and
\psi_{L1}
in hand, we construct
\psi^{out}
in the original
x
variable. Recall the definition of the cutoffs
\eta_{K}^{i}
for
i=R,L
in (
4.15
)–(
4.16
). As
E_{2}^{out}
has the form (
4.28
), we obtain
\psi^{out}
by solving the equation

\displaystyle\Delta\psi^{out}+\psi_{R1}\Delta\eta^{(R)}_{K}+2\nabla\eta^{(R)}_{K}\cdot\nabla\psi_{R1}-\psi_{L1}\Delta\eta^{(L)}_{K}-2\nabla\eta^{(L)}_{K}\cdot\nabla\psi_{L1}=0

(4.87)

on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
with boundary condition
\psi^{out}\left(x_{1},0\right)\equiv 0
. We first concentrate on the terms involving
\psi_{R1}
in (
4.87
). Note that in the
x
variable, (
4.15
) implies that both of these terms are supported on
B_{K(t+1)}^{\mathsf{c}}\left(pe_{1}\right)\cap\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
. Thus we can equivalently look at the size of
\psi_{R1}
in the
y
variable on
B_{\frac{K(t+1)}{\varepsilon}}^{\mathsf{c}}\left(0\right)\cap\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
. We note that since
K
is given in (
4.18
) and
t\geq T_{0}
, we can assume
\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|\geq C\varepsilon^{-1}
, an absolute constant
C>0
. Using (
4.78
), we can write

\displaystyle\psi_{R1}(y,t)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\log{\left(\frac{\left|z-\bar{y}\right|}{\left|z-y\right|}\right)}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\psi_{R1}+\mathcal{E}_{2}-\mathcal{E}_{3}-\alpha_{1}\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}-\alpha_{2}\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)\right)dz.

(4.88)

Due to the presence of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
in the integrand in (
4.88
), we can consider

\displaystyle\log{\left(\frac{\left|z-\bar{y}\right|}{\left|z-y\right|}\right)},\ \left|z-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|\leq\rho,\ \left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|\geq C\varepsilon^{-1},

(4.89)

for
\rho>0
defined in (
4.17
). On this region, we have

\displaystyle\log{\left(\frac{\left|z-\bar{y}\right|}{\left|z-y\right|}\right)}=\frac{1}{2}\log{\left(\frac{\left|z-\bar{y}\right|^{2}}{\left|z-y\right|^{2}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\log{\left(\left|\frac{z-q^{\prime}e_{2}}{|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}|}-\frac{\overline{y+q^{\prime}e_{2}}}{|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}|}\right|^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\log{\left(\left|\frac{z-q^{\prime}e_{2}}{|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}|}-\frac{y-q^{\prime}e_{2}}{|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}|}\right|^{2}\right)}

We can rewrite this as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\log{\left(1-\frac{\left(\overline{y-q^{\prime}e_{2}}\right)\cdot\left(z-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}-\frac{y_{1}z_{1}}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+\frac{2q^{\prime}\left(z_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+\frac{2q^{\prime}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+\frac{4\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{2}}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+\left|\frac{z-q^{\prime}e_{2}}{y-q^{\prime}e_{2}}\right|^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\log{\left(1-\frac{2\left(z-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)\cdot\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+\left|\frac{z-q^{\prime}e_{2}}{y-q^{\prime}e_{2}}\right|^{2}\right)}\coloneqq\mathscr{L}_{1}\left(z-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)-\mathscr{L}_{2}\left(z-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)

Note that on the relevant region defined in (
4.89
), we have that

\displaystyle-\mathscr{L}_{2}\left(z-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)=\frac{\left(z-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)\cdot\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{2}\right)}.

The first term on the right hand side is
\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon\right)}
, and is a sum of mode
1
terms in the variable
z-q^{\prime}e_{2}
. Therefore, using the fact that
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
is radial to main order, with a leading order correction that is
\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{2}\right)}
at mode
2
, and (
4.69
)–(
4.70
), (
4.78
)–(
4.84
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{2}\left(z-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\psi_{R1}-\mathcal{E}_{2}-\mathcal{E}_{3}-\alpha_{1}\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}-\alpha_{2}\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)\right)dz=\mathcal{O}{\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{4}}{t^{2}}\right)}.

(4.90)

We can also ensure that for small enough
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\left|-\frac{\left(\overline{y-q^{\prime}e_{2}}\right)\cdot\left(z-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}-\frac{y_{1}z_{1}}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+\frac{2q^{\prime}\left(z_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+\frac{2q^{\prime}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+\frac{4\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{2}}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+\left|\frac{z-q^{\prime}e_{2}}{y-q^{\prime}e_{2}}\right|^{2}\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}.

We therefore have

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{1}\left(z-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{2q^{\prime}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+\frac{4\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{2}}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}\right)\left(-\frac{\left(\overline{y-q^{\prime}e_{2}}\right)\cdot\left(z-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}-\frac{y_{1}z_{1}}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+\frac{2q^{\prime}\left(z_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\log{\left(1+\frac{2q^{\prime}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+\frac{4\left(q^{\prime}\right)^{2}}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}\right)}+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{2}\right)}.

(4.91)

With respect to
z-q^{\prime}e_{2}
, the first term on the right hand side of (
4.91
) is a mode
1
term of order
\varepsilon
, and the second is a constant term of order
1
. Thus, applying similar modal considerations as for (
4.90
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{2}\left(z-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\psi_{R1}-\mathcal{E}_{2}-\mathcal{E}_{3}-\alpha_{1}\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}-\alpha_{2}\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)\right)dz=\mathcal{O}{\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{4}}{t^{2}}\right)}.

(4.92)

We can estimate the gradient of
\psi_{R1}
on the region specified in (
4.89
) similarly.

Next we note that (
4.15
) gives

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\eta^{(R)}_{K}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)}+\left\|\Delta\eta^{(R)}_{K}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)}\leq\frac{C}{t}.

(4.93)

With (
4.90
),(
4.92
), and (
4.93
), as well as analogous estimates for
\psi_{L1}
and
\eta^{(L)}_{K}
given by symmetry in
x_{1}
and (
4.16
), we obtain the bound

\displaystyle\left\|\psi^{out}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}+\left\|\nabla\psi^{out}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{4}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t^{3}}.

(4.94)

Recalling (
4.29
), the dependence of
\psi^{out}
on
\xi
in the
y
variable can be estimated using Lemmas
4.1
and
4.4
, and we obtain that on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
,

\displaystyle\left|\psi^{out}\left(y,\xi\right)-\psi^{out}\left(y,\xi_{0}\right)\right|+\left|\nabla\left(\psi^{out}\left(y,\xi\right)-\psi^{out}\left(y,\xi_{0}\right)\right)\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t^{3}}.

(4.95)

Step IV: Rewriting
E_{R1}
Part B

We now seek to construct
\psi_{R2}
. We will first once again rewrite
E_{R1}
in an amenable form to do so. From (
4.78
), we can rewrite (
4.77
) as

\displaystyle E_{R1}

\displaystyle=\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\phi_{R1}-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}\right)+\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\phi_{R2}-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R1}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}+\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R1}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R2}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R2}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}\right)

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\Delta\psi_{R2}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\psi_{R2}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{E}_{4}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p_{0},q_{0}\right)+\alpha_{2}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\Lambda_{01}\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)}{p_{0}^{2}}+\frac{\varepsilon q_{0}\Lambda_{00}\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)}{p_{0}^{2}}\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\varepsilon\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\left[\xi_{1}\right]+\dot{\xi}_{1}\right)\cdot\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\dot{q}_{0}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{q^{\prime}}g\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}+1\right)y_{1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}}\frac{m\varepsilon^{4}\dot{q}_{0}}{8q_{0}^{3}}\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+1\right)y_{1}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\varepsilon\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\left[\tilde{\xi}\right]+\dot{\tilde{\xi}}\right)\cdot\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi^{out}\cdot\grad_{y}\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R1}\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi^{out}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}+\dot{q}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\mathcal{R}_{12}\right),

with
\mathcal{R}_{12}
being defined in (
4.85
). From this expression we first concentrate on

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\phi_{R1}-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1},

(4.96)

which we rewrite as

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\right).

(4.97)

We deal with the second term on the right hand side of (
4.97
) first. Following the same procedure as in the proof of Lemma
4.3
, we have that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\right)

\displaystyle=-\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)-\varepsilon\dot{q}y_{1}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\mathcal{R}_{14},

(4.98)

where
\mathcal{R}_{14}=\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{4}t^{-3}\right)}
on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}
, which has the same support as
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
. Using (
4.86
) and Lemma
4.6
, we can rewrite on the right hand side of (
4.98
) multiplying
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}
as

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{m\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\left(\frac{1}{4p_{0}^{2}}-\frac{\left(p_{0}^{2}-q_{0}^{2}\right)}{4\left|\xi_{0}\right|^{4}}\right)\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}-1\right)\left(y_{1}^{2}-\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)\right)\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\mathcal{E}_{2}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\Gamma\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{m\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\frac{p_{0}q_{0}}{\left|\xi_{0}\right|^{4}}\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}-1\right)y_{1}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\mathcal{E}_{2}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\Gamma\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)\right)+\mathcal{R}_{15},

(4.99)

where

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{15}\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}}.

To simplify notation, we define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}_{1}(\xi_{0})=\frac{1}{4p_{0}^{2}}-\frac{\left(p_{0}^{2}-q_{0}^{2}\right)}{4\left(p_{0}^{2}+q_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}},\quad\mathfrak{d}_{2}(\xi_{0})=\frac{p_{0}q_{0}}{\left(p_{0}^{2}+q_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}}.

Then, using the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{2}
from (
4.69
), we see that the first two terms of (
4.99
) can be written as

\displaystyle+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\frac{m\varepsilon^{4}}{4}\mathfrak{d}_{1}(\xi_{0})\mathfrak{d}_{2}(\xi_{0})\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}-1\right)\frac{\Gamma\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}\left(\left(y_{1}^{2}-\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)^{2}-4y_{1}^{2}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle-\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\frac{m\varepsilon^{4}}{4}\left(\mathfrak{d}_{2}(\xi_{0})^{2}-4\mathfrak{d}_{1}(\xi_{0})^{2}\right)\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}-1\right)\frac{\Gamma\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}\left(y_{1}^{2}-\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)^{2}\right)y_{1}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right),

(4.100)

which, modulo multiplication by
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}
, is readily seen to be a sum of mode
4
terms that are of order
\varepsilon^{4}t^{-4}
. Letting the expression in (
4.100
) be
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{E}_{4,1}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p_{0},q_{0}\right)
, we have that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\right)=\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{E}_{4,1}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p_{0},q_{0}\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{15}.

As
\mathcal{E}_{4,1}
contains no mode
0
terms with respect to
y-q^{\prime}e_{2}
, we can proceed analogously to Lemma
4.5
, and obtain that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\right)

\displaystyle=\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\nabla_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{E}_{4,2}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{15}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{16},

where, with respect to
y-q^{\prime}e_{2}
,
\mathcal{E}_{4,2}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)
is again a sum of mode
4
terms of order
\varepsilon^{4}t^{-4}
, and

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{16}\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}}.

For the first term on the right hand side of (
4.97
), we recall from (
4.86
) that on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}=\mathcal{E}_{2,1}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p_{0},q_{0}\right)+\mathcal{E}_{3,1}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p_{0},q_{0}\right)+\mathcal{R}_{13},

(4.101)

where
\mathcal{E}_{2,1}
and
