\mathcal{E}_{3,1}
are shorthand for the sum of the mode
2
and mode
3
terms respectively on the right hand side of (
4.86
), and where
\mathcal{R}_{13}
is of order
\varepsilon^{4}t^{-2}
. Then, noting that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)=-\frac{\dot{q}}{\varepsilon}\partial_{2}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right),

once we substitute (
4.101
) in to the first term on the right hand side of (
4.97
), we obtain

\displaystyle-\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)-\varepsilon\dot{q}y_{1}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\mathcal{E}_{2,1}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p_{0},q_{0}\right)+\mathcal{E}_{3,1}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p_{0},q_{0}\right)\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{17},

(4.102)

where

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{17}\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}}.

Recalling from Lemma
4.6
that on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
,

\displaystyle\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)-\varepsilon\dot{q}y_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{t^{2}}\right),

and from (
4.86
) that
\mathcal{E}_{3,1}
is of order
\varepsilon^{3}t^{-3}
, we can rewrite (
4.102
) as

\displaystyle\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\mathcal{E}_{2}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{y}\left(\mathcal{E}_{2,1}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{17}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{18},

where

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{18}\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}}.

Similarly to (
4.99
)–(
4.100
), we can see that on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
,

\displaystyle\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\mathcal{E}_{2}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{y}\left(\mathcal{E}_{2,1}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)\right)=\mathcal{E}_{4,3}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},p_{0},q_{0}\right),

where, with respect to
y-q^{\prime}e_{2}
,
\mathcal{E}_{4,3}
is a sum of mode
4
terms of order
\varepsilon^{4}t^{-4}
. Once again following the strategy set out in Lemma
4.5
, we can write the first term on the right hand side of (
4.97
) as

\displaystyle\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\nabla_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{E}_{4,4}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{R}_{17}+\mathcal{R}_{18}+\mathcal{R}_{19}\right),

(4.103)

where, with respect to
y-q^{\prime}e_{2}
,
\mathcal{E}_{4,4}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)
is again a sum of mode
4
terms of order
\varepsilon^{4}t^{-4}
on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
, and

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{19}\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}}.

Next we look at

\displaystyle\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R1}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}.

Note once again that on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
, with respect to
y-q^{\prime}e_{2}
, both
\psi_{R1}
and
\phi_{R1}/\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
are mode
2
of order
\varepsilon^{2}t^{-2}
and of order
\varepsilon^{3}t^{-2}
otherwise. Therefore, we can proceed analogously to (
4.96
)–(
4.103
) and obtain

\displaystyle\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R1}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}=\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\nabla_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{E}_{4,5}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{E}_{4,6}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{20}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{21},

with
\mathcal{E}_{4,5}
,
\mathcal{E}_{4,6}
being a sum of mode
4
terms and of order
\varepsilon^{4}t^{-4}
on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
(and therefore on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}
). Moreover,

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{20}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}},

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{21}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}}.

Next, using (
4.86
), (
4.94
)–(
4.95
), we have

\displaystyle\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi^{out}\cdot\grad_{y}\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R1}\right)=-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\nabla_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\psi^{out}\left(y,\xi_{0}\right)\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{22}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{23},

with

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{22}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}},

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{23}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}}.

Let

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{4}

\displaystyle=\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\mathcal{R}_{12}\right)+\mathcal{R}_{17}+\mathcal{R}_{18}+\mathcal{R}_{19}+\mathcal{R}_{21}+\mathcal{R}_{23},

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{5}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{16}+\mathcal{R}_{17}+\mathcal{R}_{20}+\mathcal{R}_{22}.

Then we can rewrite
E_{R1}
as

\displaystyle E_{R1}

\displaystyle=\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\phi_{R2}-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R1}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R2}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R2}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}\right)

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\Delta\psi_{R2}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\psi_{R2}-\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{E}_{4}\left(\xi_{0}\right)+\alpha_{2}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\Lambda_{01}\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)}{p_{0}^{2}}+\frac{\varepsilon q_{0}\Lambda_{00}\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)}{p_{0}^{2}}\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\varepsilon\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\left[\xi_{1}\right]+\dot{\xi}_{1}\right)\cdot\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\dot{q}_{0}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{q^{\prime}}g\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}+1\right)y_{1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}}\frac{m\varepsilon^{4}\dot{q}_{0}}{8q_{0}^{3}}\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+1\right)y_{1}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\nabla_{y}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{E}_{4,2}\left(\xi_{0}\right)+\mathcal{E}_{4,5}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{E}_{4,4}\left(\xi_{0}\right)+\mathcal{E}_{4,6}\left(\xi_{0}\right)+\psi^{out}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\varepsilon\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\left[\tilde{\xi}\right]+\dot{\tilde{\xi}}\right)\cdot\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi^{out}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}+\dot{q}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{4}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{5}.

Step V: Constructing
\psi_{R2}
and
\xi_{1}

In the final step of the construction of the first approximation, we construct
\psi_{R2}
, and subsequently construct
\xi_{1}
as the solution to a system of ODEs that ensures the construction of
\psi_{R2}
only produces error terms that are small enough for our purposes.

We define
\psi_{R2}
as the unique solution to

\displaystyle\Delta\psi_{R2}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\psi_{R2}=\varepsilon\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\left[\xi_{1}\right]+\dot{\xi}_{1}\right)\cdot\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)^{\perp}

\displaystyle+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{E}_{4}\left(\xi_{0}\right)+\alpha_{2}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\Lambda_{01}\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)}{p_{0}^{2}}+\frac{\varepsilon q_{0}\Lambda_{00}\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)}{p_{0}^{2}}\right)\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\dot{q}_{0}\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{q^{\prime}}g\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}+1\right)y_{1}\right)

\displaystyle-\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}}\frac{m\varepsilon^{4}\dot{q}_{0}}{8q_{0}^{3}}\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|)}{\left|y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right|^{2}}+1\right)y_{1}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{E}_{4,4}\left(\xi_{0}\right)+\mathcal{E}_{4,6}\left(\xi_{0}\right)+\psi^{out}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{E}_{4,2}\left(\xi_{0}\right)+\mathcal{E}_{4,5}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\alpha_{3}\left(\xi\right)\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}+\alpha_{4}\left(\xi\right)\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)\right)

(4.104)

on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
with boundary condition
\psi_{R2}\left(y_{1},0\right)\equiv 0
. As for the equation for
\psi_{R1}
given in (
4.78
), the linear theory for the vortex pair implies that this solution exists, and has bounds

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{R2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}+\left\|\nabla\psi_{R2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}+\left\|\phi_{R2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{4}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t^{2}},

(4.105)

which we obtain from (
4.6
)–(
4.11
), Lemma
4.1
, (
4.67
), (
4.79
)–(
4.84
), (
4.94
), and the fact that we constructed the
\mathcal{E}_{4}
,
\mathcal{E}_{4,k}
,
k=2,4,5,6
to be order
\varepsilon^{4}t^{-4}
on the support of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
.

Given (
4.105
), we get that

\displaystyle\left(\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\phi_{R2}-\left[\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}-c\right)e_{1}\right]\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}\right)+\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R1}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R2}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R1}+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi_{R2}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\psi^{out}\cdot\grad_{y}\phi_{R2}=\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{24}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{25}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{26},

where

\displaystyle\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)^{+}=\gamma\left(\gamma-1\right)\left(\gamma-2\right)\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)^{\gamma-3}_{+}\chi_{B_{\frac{s}{\varepsilon}}\left(q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)}(y),

and

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{24}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}},

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{25}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}},

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{26}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}}.

Then
E_{R1}
becomes

\displaystyle E_{R1}

\displaystyle=-\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\alpha_{3}\left(\xi\right)\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}+\alpha_{4}\left(\xi\right)\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\varepsilon\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\left[\tilde{\xi}\right]+\dot{\tilde{\xi}}\right)\cdot\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle+\dot{q}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{2}\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{24}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{25}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}_{26}

\displaystyle+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{4}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{5}.

Thus we are left with the task of ensuring that
\alpha_{3}
and
\alpha_{4}
are small enough, which, as discussed previously, we will do by constructing an appropriate
\xi_{1}=\left(p_{1},q_{1}\right)
that solves a system of ODEs.

First, we need the following estimate on
\psi^{out}
upon the change of variables
z=y-q^{\prime}e_{2}
:

\displaystyle\left|\psi^{out}\left(z+q^{\prime}e_{2},\xi_{0}\right)-\psi^{out}\left(z+q_{0}^{\prime}e_{2},\xi_{0}\right)\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t^{5}}.

(4.106)

Next we note that the system of ODEs derived from integrating (
4.104
) against elements of the kernel of
\Delta+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
will have terms that depend on
\tilde{\xi}
due to the presence of
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
and the aforementioned elements of the kernel in the integrands. Hence, we instead let
\xi_{1}
solve a different system with no dependence on
\tilde{\xi}
which, by (
4.56
)–(
4.58
),(
4.106
) approximates the system with dependence on
\tilde{\xi}
well enough to give us the smallness on
\alpha_{3}
and
\alpha_{4}
that we need.

Recall the definitions of
\mathscr{W}_{0}
,
\mathscr{W}_{1}
,
\mathscr{Z}_{1}
, and
\mathscr{Z}_{2}
from (
4.53
)–(
4.55
), including the fact that
\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\right)
and
\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\right)
are supported on the ball
B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)
, for
\rho>0
defined in (
4.17
). Then,
\xi_{1}
will solve the system given by

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left(\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\left[\xi_{1}\right]\right)_{1}+\dot{p}_{1}+\left(\frac{\varepsilon\Lambda_{01}\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)}{p_{0}^{2}}+\frac{q_{0}\Lambda_{00}\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)}{p_{0}^{2}}\right)\right)\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\right)\mathscr{Z}_{2}\left(z,q_{0}\right)z_{2}\ dz

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\right)\mathscr{Z}_{2}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\left(\mathcal{E}_{4}\left(z,\xi_{0}\right)+\alpha_{2}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\mathscr{Z}_{2}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\right)dz

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\right)\mathscr{Z}_{2}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\left(\mathcal{E}_{4,4}\left(z,\xi_{0}\right)+\mathcal{E}_{4,6}\left(z,\xi_{0}\right)+\psi^{out}\left(z+q_{0}^{\prime}e_{2},\xi_{0}\right)\right)dz

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\right)\mathscr{Z}_{2}\left(\mathcal{E}_{4,2}\left(z,\xi_{0}\right)+\mathcal{E}_{4,5}\left(z,\xi_{0}\right)\right)dz=0,

(4.107)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\left(\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\left[\xi_{1}\right]\right)_{2}+\dot{q}_{1}\right)\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\right)\mathscr{Z}_{1}\left(z,q_{0}\right)z_{1}\ dz+\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\right)\mathscr{Z}_{1}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\mathcal{E}_{4}\left(z,\xi_{0}\right)dz

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\right)\mathscr{Z}_{1}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\left(\frac{\dot{q}_{0}\left|z\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}(z)}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{q^{\prime}}g\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)\left(\frac{\varrho_{1}(\left|z\right|)}{\left|z\right|}+1\right)z_{1}\right)dz

\displaystyle-\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\right)\mathscr{Z}_{1}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\left(\frac{\left|z\right|}{\Gamma^{\prime}(z)}\frac{m\varepsilon^{4}\dot{q}_{0}}{8q_{0}^{3}}\left(\frac{\varrho_{2}(\left|z\right|)}{\left|z\right|^{2}}+1\right)z_{1}z_{2}\right)dz

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\right)\mathscr{Z}_{1}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\left(\mathcal{E}_{4,4}\left(z,\xi_{0}\right)+\mathcal{E}_{4,6}\left(z,\xi_{0}\right)+\psi^{out}\left(z+q_{0}^{\prime}e_{2},\xi_{0}\right)\right)dz

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\right)\mathscr{Z}_{1}\left(z,q_{0}\right)\left(\mathcal{E}_{4,2}\left(z,\xi_{0}\right)+\mathcal{E}_{4,5}\left(z,\xi_{0}\right)\right)dz=0.

(4.108)

We need to check that the solution to this system on
\left[T_{0},\infty\right)
satisfies (
4.8
)–(
4.9
). To do this we will proceed similarly to the construction of
\xi_{0}
from
\xi_{*{}}
in Section
3
. To begin with, the system (
4.107
)–(
4.108
) can be written more compactly as

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\dot{p}_{1}\\
\dot{q}_{1}\\
\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}-\mathcal{F}\left(q_{0}+q_{1},p_{0}+p_{1}\right)+\mathcal{F}\left(q_{0},p_{0}\right)-g\left(q_{0}^{\prime}+q_{1}^{\prime}\right)+g\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)\\
\mathcal{F}\left(p_{0}+p_{1},q_{0}+q_{1}\right)-\mathcal{F}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)\\
\end{pmatrix}=\mathscr{R}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\coloneqq\begin{pmatrix}\mathscr{R}\left(\xi_{0}\right)_{1}\\
\mathscr{R}\left(\xi_{0}\right)_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}

(4.109)

where
\mathscr{R}\left(\xi_{0}\right)
has bounds

\displaystyle\left|\mathscr{R}\left(\xi_{0}\right)_{1}\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{3}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t^{2}},\ \left|\mathscr{R}\left(\xi_{0}\right)_{2}\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{3}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t^{3}}.

The system (
4.109
) can be further rewritten as

\displaystyle\left(\frac{d}{dt}+\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\right)\xi_{1}=\mathscr{R}\left(\xi_{0}\right)+\mathscr{M}_{g}\xi_{1}+\mathscr{N}\left(\xi_{1}\right),

where
\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{0}\right)
is analogously define to (
3.6
):

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{0}\right)=\frac{m}{2}

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}-\frac{2p_{0}q_{0}}{\left(p_{0}^{2}+q_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}}&\frac{1}{q_{0}^{2}}+\frac{\left(p_{0}^{2}-q_{0}^{2}\right)}{\left(p_{0}^{2}+q_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}}\\
-\frac{1}{p_{0}^{2}}+\frac{\left(p_{0}^{2}-q_{0}^{2}\right)}{\left(p_{0}^{2}+q_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}}&\frac{2p_{0}q_{0}}{\left(p_{0}^{2}+q_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}}\end{pmatrix},

(4.110)

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}_{g}=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}0&\frac{\partial_{q^{\prime}}g\left(q_{0}^{\prime}\right)}{\varepsilon}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix},

and
\mathscr{N}
is the nonlinear term given by

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}\left(\xi_{1}\right)_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{q_{0}^{\prime}}^{q_{0}^{\prime}+q_{1}^{\prime}}\partial^{2}_{Q}g(Q)\left(q_{0}^{\prime}+q_{1}^{\prime}-Q\right)dQ+\mathcal{F}\left(q_{0}+q_{1},p_{0}+p_{1}\right)-\mathcal{F}\left(q_{0},p_{0}\right)+\left(\mathscr{C}\xi_{1}\right)_{1},

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}\left(\xi_{1}\right)_{2}

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{F}\left(p_{0}+p_{1},q_{0}+q_{1}\right)+\mathcal{F}\left(p_{0},q_{0}\right)+\left(\mathscr{C}\xi_{1}\right)_{2}.

Recalling
\xi_{0}=\xi_{*{}}+\overline{\xi}
, we can compare
\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{0}\right)
to
\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{*{}}\right)
defined in (
3.6
), and use (
3.4
) as well as the bounds (
3.13
)–(
3.14
) to deduce that the matrix
\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{0}\right)-\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{*{}}\right)
has bounds

\displaystyle\left|\left(\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{0}\right)-\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{*{}}\right)\right)_{ij}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{2}}{t^{2}},\ \left(i,j\right)\neq\left(1,2\right),

\displaystyle\left|\left(\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{0}\right)-\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{*{}}\right)\right)_{12}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2}.

Thus we can once again rewrite (
4.109
) as

\displaystyle\left(\frac{d}{dt}+\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{*{}}\right)\right)\xi_{1}=\mathscr{R}\left(\xi_{0}\right)+\mathscr{L}_{\varepsilon}\xi_{1}+\mathscr{N}\left(\xi_{1}\right),

(4.111)

where
\mathscr{L}_{\varepsilon}=\mathscr{M}_{g}+\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{*{}}\right)-\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{0}\right)
has bounds

\displaystyle\left|\left(\mathscr{L}_{\varepsilon}\right)_{ij}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{2}}{t^{2}},\ \left(i,j\right)\neq\left(1,2\right),\quad\left|\left(\mathscr{L}_{\varepsilon}\right)_{12}\right|\leq C\varepsilon.

The system (
4.111
) is now framed in such a way that we can adapt the machinery developed in Section
3
to construct
\xi_{0}
. Recall that formally, we can write

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}+\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{*{}}\right)=e^{-\mathscr{B}}\frac{d}{dt}\left(e^{\mathscr{B}}\cdot\right),

with
\mathscr{B}
defined in (
3.8
). Define the operator
\mathscr{K}_{\left(\infty,\infty\right)}
by

\displaystyle\mathscr{K}_{\left(\infty,\infty\right)}\xi=\mathscr{K}_{\left(\infty,\infty\right)}\begin{pmatrix}\xi_{1}\\
\xi_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}=e^{-\mathscr{B}}\begin{pmatrix}\int_{t}^{\infty}\left(e^{\mathscr{B}}\xi\right)_{1}\\
\int_{t}^{\infty}\left(e^{\mathscr{B}}\xi\right)_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

Next, we define
\mathscr{X}_{\left(1,2\right)}
to be the space of functions
\xi=\left(\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\right):\left[T_{0},\infty\right)^{2}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
satisfying

\displaystyle\left\|\xi\right\|_{\mathscr{X}_{\left(1,2\right)}}\coloneqq\left\|t\xi_{1}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},\infty\right)}+\left\|t^{2}\xi_{2}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},\infty\right)}<\infty.

Now, let
B
be the ball radius
\varepsilon^{3}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}
in
\mathscr{X}_{\left(1,2\right)}
:

\displaystyle B=\left\{\xi\in\mathscr{X}_{\left(1,2\right)}:\left\|\xi\right\|_{\mathscr{X}_{\left(1,2\right)}}\leq\varepsilon^{3}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\right\}.

Finally we define the operator
\mathscr{T}
on
B
by

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}\xi\coloneqq\mathscr{K}_{\left(\infty,\infty\right)}\left(\mathscr{R}\left(\xi_{0}\right)+\mathscr{L}_{\varepsilon}\xi_{1}+\mathscr{N}\left(\xi_{1}\right)\right).

Then just as in Section
3
, one can check directly that
\mathscr{T}:B\to B
and is a contraction on
B
for small enough
\varepsilon>0
. Then the fixed point
\mathscr{T}\xi_{1}=\xi_{1}
solves (
4.107
)–(
4.108
) and satisfies bounds (
4.8
)–(
4.9
).

With our
\xi_{1}
in hand, we can compare the equations they solve in (
4.107
)–(
4.108
) to the identities obtained by integrating (
4.104
) against
\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)
and
\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}
respectively. Then the bounds on the right hand side of (
4.104
) as well as (
4.56
)–(
4.58
),(
4.106
) give that

\displaystyle\left|\alpha_{3}\right|+\left|\alpha_{4}\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}}{t^{3}}.

Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{*}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\alpha_{3}\left(\xi\right)\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}+\alpha_{4}\left(\xi\right)\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)\right)+\dot{q}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{2}\right)+\mathcal{R}_{24}+\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\right)+\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{4},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{*}_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{25}+\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{5},\quad\mathcal{R}^{*}_{3}=\mathcal{R}_{26}.

Then

\displaystyle E_{R1}

\displaystyle=\nabla^{\perp}_{y}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right)\cdot\grad_{y}\left(\varepsilon\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\left[\tilde{\xi}\right]+\dot{\tilde{\xi}}\right)\cdot\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{1}\left(y,\xi\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{2}\left(y,\xi\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{3}\left(y,\xi\right),

where

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{1}\left(y,\xi\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}},

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{2}\left(y,\xi\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}},

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{3}\left(y,\xi\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}}.

Finally, due to (
4.105
) and analogous bounds for
\psi_{L2}
, we have

\displaystyle E_{2}^{out}=\left(\psi_{R2}\Delta\eta^{(R)}_{K}+2\nabla\eta^{(R)}_{K}\cdot\nabla\psi_{R2}\right)-\left(\psi_{L2}\Delta\eta^{(L)}_{K}+2\nabla\eta^{(L)}_{K}\cdot\nabla\psi_{L2}\right)=\mathcal{O}{\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{4}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t^{3}}\right)},

(4.112)

as required.
∎

5
Constructing Solutions on
\left[T_{0},T\right]

Recalling the statement of Theorem
1.1
, we explicitly state here that for the rest of the paper we have
\gamma\geq 19
.

Having constructed the first approximation
\left(\omega_{*},\Psi_{*}\right)
towards a full solution of (
1.1
) on
\left[T_{0},T\right]
in Theorem
4.7
, we now look for a solution on the same time interval to (
1.1
) of the form

\displaystyle\omega\left(x,t\right)

\displaystyle=\omega_{*}\left(x,t\right)+\phi_{*}\left(x,t\right),

(5.1)

\displaystyle\Psi\left(x,t\right)

\displaystyle=\Psi_{*}\left(x,t\right)+\psi_{*}\left(x,t\right).

(5.2)

We set

\displaystyle\phi_{*}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-2}\left(\phi_{*R}-\phi_{*L}\right),

\displaystyle\psi_{*}

\displaystyle=\eta^{(R)}_{K}\psi_{*R}-\eta^{(L)}_{K}\psi_{*L}+\psi_{*}^{out},

with
\eta^{(R)}_{K}
and
\eta^{(L)}_{K}
defined in (
4.15
)–(
4.16
). We define

\displaystyle\phi_{*}^{in}

\displaystyle=\left(\phi_{*R},\phi_{*L}\right),

\displaystyle\psi_{*}^{in}

\displaystyle=\left(\psi_{*R},\psi_{*L}\right).

Finally we again have
\xi(t)=\left(p(t),q(t)\right)
as in (
4.5
):

\displaystyle\xi(t)=\xi_{0}(t)+\xi_{1}(t)+\tilde{\xi}(t),

for
\xi_{0}
solving (
2.16
),
\xi_{1}
solving (
4.107
)–(
4.108
), and
\xi_{0},\xi_{1}
, and
\tilde{\xi}
satisfying (
4.6
)–(
4.11
).

A delicate issue in this construction is the choice of coordinates. Recall that
\left(\psi_{R},\phi_{R}\right)
built in Theorem
4.7
when constructing the first approximation, has the form

\displaystyle\psi_{R}

\displaystyle=\psi_{R}\left(\frac{x-pe_{1}}{\varepsilon},t\right),

\displaystyle\phi_{R}

\displaystyle=\phi_{R}\left(\frac{x-pe_{1}}{\varepsilon},t\right),

with an analogous centring around
\varepsilon^{-1}\left(x+pe_{1}\right)
for
\left(\psi_{L},\phi_{L}\right)
. These choices privilege the symmetry of the two vortex pairs around the line
x_{1}=0
. Moreover, it was natural to solve the equations that gave us
\psi_{R}
on the upper half plane with
0
boundary condition on
x_{2}=0
. Accordingly, in this section we again define

\displaystyle y=\frac{x-pe_{1}}{\varepsilon},\ p^{\prime}=\frac{p}{e_{1}},\ q^{\prime}=\frac{q}{e_{1}},

(5.3)

as in (
4.29
).

However, it will turn out that in order to reveal important structure of the full linearised Euler operator, it is more useful to work in coordinates

\displaystyle z=\frac{x-\xi\left(t\right)}{\varepsilon}=\frac{x-pe_{1}-qe_{2}}{\varepsilon}

(5.4)

for
\left(\phi_{*R},\psi_{*R}\right)
, and analogously for
\left(\phi_{*L},\psi_{*L}\right)
. The majority of the construction of the full solution in this section will be done in
z
coordinates. However, when emphasising the relationship between
\phi_{*}
and
\psi_{*}
, that is

\displaystyle-\Delta\psi_{*j}=\phi_{*j},\ \ \ j=R,L,

(5.5)

we will usually work in
y
coordinates and take (
5.5
) to mean satisfying the stated equation on the upper half plane with
\psi_{*R}\left(y_{1},0\right)\equiv 0
, and analogously in the appropriate coordinates for
\psi_{*L}
. These instances will be clarified when they occur.

To work in
z
coordinates effectively, we will construct
\left(\phi_{*},\psi_{*}\right)
on a translate of the upper half plane, after which we will translate back to the upper half plane and obtain the full solution by using the desired odd symmetry in the
x_{2}
variable to reflect the solution across the line
x_{2}=0
. Concretely, we define the set
\mathbb{H}_{w}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{w}=\left\{\left(v_{1},v_{2}\right):v_{2}\geq w\right\}.

(5.6)

Then
\mathbb{H}_{0}=\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
, and we will construct
\left(\phi_{*R}\left(z,t\right),\psi_{*R}\left(z,t\right)\right)
on
\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}
such that
\supp\phi_{*R}\subset B_{3\rho}\left(0\right)
,
\rho>0
defined in (
4.17
),
\psi_{*R}\left(z_{1},-q^{\prime},t\right)=0
, and analogously for
\left(\phi_{*L},\psi_{*L}\right)
.

We now concentrate on
\left(\phi_{*R}\left(z,t\right),\psi_{*R}\left(z,t\right)\right)
with the understanding that everything for
\left(\phi_{*L},\psi_{*L}\right)
is obtained after reflection in an odd manner around
x_{1}=0
in the original variables. We make a further decomposition of
\left(\phi_{*R}\left(z,t\right),\psi_{*R}\left(z,t\right)\right)
of the form

\displaystyle\phi_{*R}\left(z,t\right)=\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\left(z,t\right)+\alpha_{R}(t)U_{R}\left(z,t\right),

(5.7)

with
U_{R}
defined in (
4.14
), and
\alpha_{R}
will solve a certain ODE defined in (
5.32
), and will satisfy

\displaystyle\left\|t^{2}\alpha_{R}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}+\left\|t^{3}\dot{\alpha}_{R}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}\leq\varepsilon^{3-\sigma},\ \ \alpha_{R}\left(T\right)=0,

(5.8)

for fixed, arbitrarily small
\sigma
. We also impose three orthogonality conditions on
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
related to its mass. The first is the condition of
0
total mass:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\ dz=0.

(5.9)

The next two are centre of mass conditions:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}z_{1}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\ dz

\displaystyle=0,

(5.10)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}z_{2}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\ dz

\displaystyle=0.

(5.11)

Remark 5.1
.

The time dependent parameter
\alpha_{R}
appearing in (
5.7
) should be thought of as a way of adjusting the mass, and will be used to enforce the condition (
5.9
), just as
\left(\tilde{p},\tilde{q}\right)
will be used to enforce (
5.10
)–(
5.11
).

Having imposed the ansatz (
5.7
) for
\phi_{*R}
, we have, in
z
coordinates,

\displaystyle\psi_{*R}\left(z,t\right)=\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\left(z,t\right)+\alpha_{R}\left(t\right)\left(\Psi_{R}\left(z,t\right)-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)\right),

(5.12)

with
\Psi_{R}
defined in (
4.13
). In
y
coordinates this can be restated as

\displaystyle\psi_{*R}\left(y,t\right)=\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\left(y,t\right)+\alpha_{R}\left(t\right)\left(\Psi_{R}\left(y,t\right)-c\varepsilon y_{2}\right),

(5.13)

where, in the upper half plane in
y
variables,
-\Delta\tilde{\psi}_{*R}=\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
with
\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\in L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}\right)
and
\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\left(y_{1},0\right)\equiv 0
. That is,

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\left(y,t\right)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\log{\left(\frac{\left|v-\bar{y}\right|}{\left|v-y\right|}\right)}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\left(v,t\right)dv.

(5.14)

Thus, given (
5.7
) and (
5.12
)–(
5.13
), we have
-\Delta\psi_{*R}=\phi_{*R}
. We let

\tilde{\phi}_{*}=\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\tilde{\phi}_{*L}\right),\quad\tilde{\psi}_{*}=\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\tilde{\psi}_{*L}\right).

(5.15)

We finally note that by symmetry, we can decompose
\tilde{\phi}_{*L}
as

\displaystyle\phi_{*L}=\tilde{\phi}_{*L}-\alpha_{R}(t)U_{L},

in the appropriate coordinates, with
\tilde{\phi}_{*L}
satisfying the odd symmetry condition in
x_{1}
with respect to
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
. Thus, we can drop the subscript on
\alpha_{R}
, and we shall simply work with

\alpha(t)=\alpha_{R}(t).

5.1
Homotopic Operators

In this section we define, with motivation, operators depending on a homotopy parameter
\lambda\in\left[0,1\right]
. At
\lambda=0
, these operators will be linear, and at
\lambda=1
, their annihilation will imply a solution to the Euler system (
1.1
). To begin with, we insert
\left(\omega,\Psi\right)
, defined in (
5.1
)–(
5.2
) into
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
defined in (
4.1
)–(
4.2
):

\displaystyle E_{1}\left[\omega,\Psi\right]

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\left(\omega_{*}+\phi_{*}\right)+\nabla^{\perp}\left(\Psi_{*}+\psi_{*}\right)\cdot\nabla\left(\omega_{*}+\phi_{*}\right),

\displaystyle E_{2}\left[\omega,\Psi\right]

\displaystyle=\Delta\left(\Psi_{*}+\psi_{*}\right)+\left(\omega_{*}+\phi_{*}\right).

Similarly to (
4.25
)–(
4.28
), we have

\displaystyle E_{1}\left[\omega,\Psi\right]

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-4}\left(E_{*R}\left(\phi_{R},\phi_{*R},\psi_{R},\psi_{*R},\psi^{out},\psi_{*}^{out};\xi\right)-E_{*L}\left(\phi_{L},\phi_{*L},\psi_{L},\psi_{*L},\psi^{out},\psi_{*}^{out};\xi\right)\right),

and

\displaystyle E_{2}\left[\omega,\Psi\right]=E_{*}^{out}\left(\phi^{in},\phi^{in}_{*},\psi^{in},\psi^{in}_{*},\psi^{out},\psi_{*}^{out};\xi\right),

where

\displaystyle E_{*R}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R}+\phi_{*R}\right)+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla^{\perp}\left(\Psi_{0}+\psi_{R}+\psi_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\cdot\nabla\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R}+\phi_{*R}\right),

(5.16)

\displaystyle E_{*L}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\left(U_{L}+\phi_{L}+\phi_{*L}\right)+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla^{\perp}\left(\Psi_{0}-\psi_{L}-\psi_{*L}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\cdot\nabla\left(U_{L}+\phi_{L}+\phi_{*L}\right),

\displaystyle E_{*}^{out}=\Delta\psi_{*}^{out}

\displaystyle+\left(\psi_{*R}\Delta\eta^{(R)}_{K}+2\nabla\eta^{(R)}_{K}\cdot\nabla\psi_{*R}\right)-\left(\psi_{*L}\Delta\eta^{(L)}_{K}+2\nabla\eta^{(L)}_{K}\cdot\nabla\psi_{*L}\right)+E_{2}^{out}\left(\phi^{in},\psi^{in},\psi^{out};\xi\right),

(5.17)

with
\psi_{j}
,
\phi_{j}
,
j=R,L
, and
\psi^{out}
constructed in the proof of Theorem
4.7
, and
E_{2}^{out}
given in (
4.112
). We recall that the specific forms of
E_{R1}
,
E_{L1}
, and
E_{2}^{out}
given in (
4.26
)–(
4.28
) are due to the choice of
K
and
T_{0}
in (
4.18
)–(
4.19
) implying
\eta^{(j)}_{K}\equiv 1
on
\supp{\phi_{j}}
for
j=R,L
,
\eta^{(R)}_{K}\equiv 0
on
\supp{\phi_{L}}
, and vice versa. Due to (
4.10
), (
4.11
), and (
5.8
), the supports of
\phi_{*R}
and
\phi_{*L}
will be close enough to the supports of
U_{R}
and
U_{L}
respectively to imply the same behaviour, hence the specific forms of
E_{*R}
,
E_{*L}
, and
E_{*}^{out}
given in (
5.16
)–(
5.17
).

Now we consider

\displaystyle E_{*R}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R}+\phi_{*R}\right)+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla^{\perp}\left(\Psi_{0}+\psi_{R}+\psi_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\cdot\nabla\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R}+\phi_{*R}\right).

Regrouping terms, this becomes

\displaystyle E_{*R}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R}\right)+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla^{\perp}\left(\Psi_{0}+\psi_{R}+\psi^{out}\right)\cdot\nabla\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R}\right)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\phi_{*R}+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla^{\perp}\left(\Psi_{0}+\psi_{R}+\psi_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\cdot\nabla\phi_{*R}+\varepsilon^{2}\nabla^{\perp}\left(\psi_{*R}+\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\cdot\nabla\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R}\right).

(5.18)

Now, to define our homotopy parameter dependent operators, we introduce a change of coordinates
z_{\lambda}
that itself depends on the homotopy parameter
\lambda
:

\displaystyle z_{\lambda}=\frac{x-\xi_{0}\left(t\right)-\xi_{1}\left(t\right)-\lambda\tilde{\xi}\left(t\right)}{\varepsilon},

(5.19)

where
\xi_{0}
and
\xi_{1}
were constructed in Section
3
and Theorem
4.7
, and
\tilde{\xi}
satisfies (
4.10
)–(
4.11
). We denote
\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}\coloneqq\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}+\lambda\tilde{\xi}
, and in general any quantities that depend on
\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}
will be written with a
\lambda
subscript. In
z_{\lambda}
coordinates, (
5.18
) becomes

\displaystyle E_{*R}=-\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}U_{R}\cdot\left(\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\left[\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right]+\lambda\dot{\tilde{\xi}}\right)\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{1}\left(z_{\lambda},\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{2}\left(z_{\lambda},\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{3}\left(z_{\lambda},\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}\right)+\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\phi_{*R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\psi_{*R}+\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\Psi_{0}-\varepsilon\dot{p}_{\lambda}z_{\lambda,2}+\varepsilon\dot{q}_{\lambda}z_{\lambda,1}+\psi_{R}+\psi_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\phi_{*R},

(5.20)

where, in
y
coordinates,

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{1}\left(y,\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}},

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{2}\left(y,\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}},

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{3}\left(y,\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{5}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)^{+}}{t^{3}}.

Clearly the first
4
terms on the right hand side of (
5.20
) correspond to the first two terms on the right hand side of (
5.18
) by Theorem
4.7
. We can further rewrite (
5.20
) as

\displaystyle E_{*R}=-\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}U_{R}\cdot\left(\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\left[\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right]+\lambda\dot{\tilde{\xi}}-\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{1}\left(z_{\lambda},\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{2}\left(z_{\lambda},\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{3}\left(z_{\lambda},\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}\right)+\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\phi_{*R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\psi_{*R}\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\psi^{out}_{*}\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\phi_{R}+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\Psi_{0}-\varepsilon\dot{p}_{\lambda}z_{\lambda,2}+\varepsilon\dot{q}_{\lambda}z_{\lambda,1}+\psi_{R}+\psi_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\phi_{*R}.

(5.21)

We now concentrate on the last
4
terms of (
5.21
). Firstly, as
\phi_{*R}
and
\psi_{*R}
are of the form (
5.7
) and (
5.12
) respectively, the last
4
terms of (
5.21
) can be written as

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R}+\alpha U_{R}\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R}+\alpha\left(\Psi_{R}-c_{\lambda}\varepsilon\left(z_{\lambda,2}+q^{\prime}_{\lambda}\right)\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z,\lambda}\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R}\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\psi^{out}_{*}\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\phi_{R}

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\left(1+\alpha\right)\left(\Psi_{R}-c_{\lambda}\varepsilon\left(z_{\lambda,2}+q^{\prime}_{\lambda}\right)\right)-\left(\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}_{\lambda}-c_{\lambda}\right)z_{\lambda,2}-\varepsilon\dot{q}_{\lambda}z_{\lambda,1}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R}+\alpha U_{R}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\psi_{R}+\tilde{\psi}_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R}+\alpha U_{R}\right),

(5.22)

where we have used the definition of
\Psi_{0}
from (
4.12
). As
U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)
is a function of
\Psi_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)-c_{\lambda}\varepsilon\left(z_{\lambda,2}+q^{\prime}_{\lambda}\right)
in our chosen coordinates, (
5.22
) becomes

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\Psi_{0}-\varepsilon\dot{p}_{\lambda}z_{\lambda,2}+\varepsilon\dot{q}_{\lambda}z_{\lambda,1}+\psi_{R}+\psi_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}\left(\dot{\alpha}\left(t\right)U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)+\alpha\left(t\right)\partial_{t}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\right)+\alpha\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\Psi_{R}-c_{\lambda}\varepsilon\left(z_{\lambda,2}+q^{\prime}_{\lambda}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\phi_{R}

\displaystyle+\alpha\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\left(-\left(\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}_{\lambda}-c\right)z_{\lambda,2}-\varepsilon\dot{q}_{\lambda}z_{\lambda,1}\right)+\psi_{R}+\tilde{\psi}_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}U_{R}+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\psi^{out}_{*}\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\phi_{R}.

(5.23)

Thus we can write
E_{*R}
as

\displaystyle E_{*R}=\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\Psi_{0}-\varepsilon\dot{p}_{\lambda}z_{\lambda,2}+\varepsilon\dot{q}_{\lambda}z_{\lambda,1}+\psi_{R}+\psi_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}\left(\dot{\alpha}\left(t\right)U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)+\alpha\left(t\right)\partial_{t}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\right)+\alpha\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c_{\lambda}\varepsilon\left(z_{\lambda,2}+q^{\prime}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\phi_{R}

\displaystyle+\alpha\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\left(-\left(\Psi_{L}+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}_{\lambda}-c_{\lambda}\right)z_{\lambda,2}-\varepsilon\dot{q}_{\lambda}z_{\lambda,1}\right)+\psi_{R}+\tilde{\psi}_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}U_{R}+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\psi^{out}_{*}\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\phi_{R}

\displaystyle-\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}U_{R}\cdot\left(\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\left[\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right]+\lambda\dot{\tilde{\xi}}-\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{1}\left(z_{\lambda},\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{2}\left(z_{\lambda},\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{3}\left(z_{\lambda},\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}\right).

(5.24)

We note that similarly to (
5.22
) and (
5.23
), we can write

\displaystyle\Psi_{0}-\varepsilon\dot{p}_{\lambda}z_{\lambda,2}+\varepsilon\dot{q}_{\lambda}z_{\lambda,1}+\psi_{R}+\psi_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\Psi_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)-c_{\lambda}\varepsilon\left(z_{\lambda,2}+q^{\prime}_{\lambda}\right)-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega_{\lambda}-\left(\Psi_{L}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}_{\lambda}-c_{\lambda}\right)z_{\lambda,2}-\varepsilon\dot{q}_{\lambda}z_{\lambda,1}\right)

\displaystyle+\psi_{R}+\psi_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*},

wherein we note that whilst the main order term
\Psi_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)-c_{\lambda}\varepsilon\left(z_{\lambda,2}+q^{\prime}_{\lambda}\right)-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega_{\lambda}
has a nonlinear dependence on
\lambda\tilde{\xi}
for
\lambda>0
, this dependence disappears at
\lambda=0
. Let

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}\coloneqq-\left(\Psi_{L}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}_{\lambda}-c_{\lambda}\right)z_{\lambda,2}-\varepsilon\dot{q}_{\lambda}z_{\lambda,1}\right)+\psi_{R}+\psi_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*},

(5.25)

and define
\mathscr{E}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)
by

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\coloneqq\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\left(U_{R}+\lambda\phi_{R}\right)\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\Psi_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)-c_{\lambda}\varepsilon\left(z_{\lambda,2}+q^{\prime}_{\lambda}\right)-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega_{\lambda}+\lambda\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}\dot{\alpha}\left(t\right)U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)-\lambda\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\cdot\left(\varepsilon\left(\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\tilde{\xi}-\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\right)+\lambda\tilde{\mathscr{E}}_{R}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)

\displaystyle-\lambda\varepsilon\dot{\tilde{\xi}}\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\left(U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\cdot\grad_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\left[\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right]-\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\left[\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right]\right)\cdot z_{\lambda}^{\perp}\right),

(5.26)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{E}}_{R}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)=\alpha\left(t\right)\partial_{t}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)+\alpha\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c_{\lambda}\varepsilon\left(z_{\lambda,2}+q^{\prime}_{\lambda}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\phi_{R}

\displaystyle+\alpha\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\left(-\left(\Psi_{L}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}_{\lambda}-c_{\lambda}\right)z_{\lambda,2}-\varepsilon\dot{q}_{\lambda}z_{\lambda,1}\right)+\psi_{R}+\tilde{\psi}_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\psi^{out}_{*}\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\phi_{R}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{1}\left(z_{\lambda},\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{2}\left(z_{\lambda},\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{3}\left(z_{\lambda},\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}\right).

(5.27)

Here
\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)
is defined analogously to (
3.6
) and (
4.110
),
\phi_{R}
and
\psi_{R}
were constructed in the proof of Theorem
4.7
, and
\tilde{\psi}_{*R}
is related to
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
via (
5.14
).

We next define

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{\lambda}^{out}\left(\tilde{\psi},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)

\displaystyle=\Delta\psi_{*}^{out}+\lambda\left(\psi_{*R}\Delta\eta^{(R)}_{K}+2\nabla\eta^{(R)}_{K}\cdot\nabla\psi_{*R}\right)-\lambda\left(\psi_{*L}\Delta\eta^{(L)}_{K}+2\nabla\eta^{(L)}_{K}\cdot\nabla\psi_{*L}\right)

\displaystyle+\lambda E_{2}^{out}\left(\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right),

(5.28)

with
\tilde{\psi}_{*}
being defined in (
5.15
), and
E_{2}^{out}
being given in (
4.112
).

We note that at
\lambda=1
, we recover (
5.24
) and (
5.17
) respectively:

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{R,1}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)

\displaystyle=E_{*R}\left(\phi_{R},\phi_{*R},\psi_{R},\psi_{*R},\psi^{out},\psi_{*}^{out};\xi\right),

(5.29)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{1}^{out}\left(\tilde{\psi},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)

\displaystyle=E_{*}^{out}\left(\phi^{in},\phi^{in}_{*},\psi^{in},\psi^{in}_{*},\psi^{out},\psi_{*}^{out};\xi\right).

(5.30)

Thus a solution to the system (
1.1
) is constructed if we can find
\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\tilde{\xi}\right)
that make the quantities stated in (
5.29
)–(
5.30
) equal to
0
. We will do this by a continuation argument that involves finding a priori estimates along the deformation parameter
\lambda
for the equations one obtains by setting (
5.26
) and (
5.28
) equal to
0
, in addition imposing suitable bounds for the parameter functions for
t\in\left[T_{0},T\right]
.

Then, given
\left(\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)
, we require that
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
satisfies the terminal value problem

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)=c_{R0}\left(t\right)U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)+c_{R1}\left(t\right)\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\partial_{z_{\lambda,1}}\Psi_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)

\displaystyle+c_{R2}\left(t\right)\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\partial_{q^{\prime}_{\lambda}}\left(\Psi_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)-c_{\lambda}\varepsilon\left(z_{\lambda,2}+q^{\prime}_{\lambda}\right)-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega_{\lambda}\right)\ \ \text{in}\ \mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{\lambda}}\times\left[T_{0},T\right],

(5.31a)

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{*R}(\mathrel{\;\cdot\;},T)=0\ \ \text{in}\ \mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{\lambda}},

(5.31b)

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)=0\ \ \text{on}\ \partial\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}\times\left[T_{0},T\right],

(5.31c)

where
\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{\lambda}}
is defined in (
5.6
), and we once again recall that
T_{0}
and
T
are defined in (
4.19
)–(
4.20
). Upon integrating against
1
,
z_{\lambda,1}
, and
z_{\lambda,2}
in turn, we can see that for
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
satisfying (
5.31
), we can derive explicit formulae for the
c_{Rj}
that depend linearly on
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
.

Remark 5.2
.

It will be necessary later to make sure that we can express
c_{Rj}
in such a way that they do not depend on derivatives of
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
. This will be achieved via integration by parts.

Given a solution to (
5.31
),
\mathscr{E}_{R,\lambda}
will be annihilated once we impose, for
j=0,1,2
, the terminal value problem

\displaystyle c_{Rj}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi},\lambda\right)=0\ \ \textrm{for all}\ t\in\left[T_{0},T\right],

(5.32)

with
\tilde{\xi}
satisfying

\displaystyle\left\|t\tilde{p}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}+\left\|t^{2}\dot{\tilde{p}}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{4-\sigma},\ \ \tilde{p}\left(T\right)=0,

(5.33a)

\displaystyle\left\|t^{2}\tilde{q}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}+\left\|t^{3}\dot{\tilde{q}}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{4-\sigma},\ \ \tilde{q}\left(T\right)=0,

(5.33b)

and
\alpha
satisfying

\displaystyle\left\|t^{2}\alpha\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}+\left\|t^{3}\dot{\alpha}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}\leq\varepsilon^{3-\sigma},\ \ \alpha\left(T\right)=0,

for some small
\sigma>0
. We require that
\tilde{\phi}_{*L}
and
c_{Lj}
solve analogous terminal value problems to (
5.31
) and (
5.32
).
We also impose that
\psi^{out}_{*}
, in
x
variables, solves the boundary value problem

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{\lambda}^{out}\left(\tilde{\psi},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)

\displaystyle=0\ \ \text{in}\ \mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\times\left[T_{0},T\right],

(5.34a)

\displaystyle\psi^{out}_{*}\left(x_{1},0,t\right)

\displaystyle=0\ \ \text{for all}\ t\in\left[T_{0},T\right],

(5.34b)

To construct a solution to (
1.1
) via the above scheme, we consider the vector of parameter functions
\mathfrak{P}=\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)
, belonging to a Banach space
\left(\mathfrak{X},\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\mathfrak{X}}\right)
and reformulate the equations (
5.14
), (
5.31
)–(
5.34
) as a fixed point problem of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\left(\mathfrak{P},\lambda\right)=\mathfrak{P},\ \ \mathfrak{P}\in\mathscr{O},

(5.35)

where
\mathscr{O}
is a bounded open set in
\mathfrak{X}
, and
\mathcal{T}\left(\cdot,\lambda\right)
is a homotopy of nonlinear compact operators with
\mathcal{T}\left(\cdot,0\right)
being linear.

For a suitable choice of
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathscr{O}
, we will show that for all
\lambda\in[0,1]
, no solution
\mathcal{Z}\in\partial\mathscr{O}
exists. Existence of a solution to (
5.35
) at
\lambda=1
will then follow by standard degree theory, which, as stated, will give a solution to (
1.1
). The choices of
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathscr{O}
will also give the desired properties we claimed the solution would possess in Theorem
1.1
.

We now move on to a priori estimates for the system (
5.14
), (
5.31
)–(
5.34
).

5.2
Weighted
L^{2}
Function Space

We must first define the appropriate function spaces in which we will work. Much like
[
10
]
, when deriving a priori estimates, it is natural to formally consider the weighted
L^{2}
space for
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
given by the norm

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}.

(5.36)

However, unlike the Liouville nonlinearity considered in
[
10
]
, our nonlinearity has compact support, and whilst we will see that
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
also has compact support, it will not be true in general that
\supp{\tilde{\phi}_{*R}}\subset\supp{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}
, meaning that (
5.36
) is not a well defined quantity. Hence we must take care in defining the analogous weighted
L^{2}
space to (
5.36
) that we will use in our context.

This motivates the following definitions. Firstly, recall the definitions of
\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{i}
,
i=0,1,2
, and
\mathscr{Z}_{j}
,
j=1,2
from (
4.51
)–(
4.55
). We adopt the shorthand

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{i}\left(\mathcal{Q}\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{i}\left(z,\mathcal{Q}\right),\ \ i=0,1,2,

\displaystyle\mathscr{Z}_{j}\left(\mathcal{Q}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathscr{Z}_{j}\left(z,\mathcal{Q}\right),\ \ j=1,2.

(5.37)

From (
4.51
)–(
4.55
) we can infer that in
z
coordinates defined by (
5.4
), we have

\displaystyle U_{R}=\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{0}\left(q\right),\ \ \ \left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}=\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{1}\left(q\right)

\displaystyle\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}=\mathscr{Z}_{1}\left(q\right),\ \ \ \partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)=\mathscr{Z}_{2}\left(q\right).

(5.38)

With (
5.37
)–(
5.38
) in hand, let
\eta_{0}(r)
once again be the smooth cutoff that is identically
1
for
r\leq 1
and identically
0
for
r\geq 2
. Define the interior cutoff,
\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}
, by

\displaystyle\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)=1-\eta_{0}\left(\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{1}\left(z,q_{0}+q_{1}\right)\right),

(5.39)

where we recall
\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{1}\left(z,q_{0}+q_{1}\right)
was defined in (
4.51
)-(
4.55
). Then
\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}
is supported on the region

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{1}\left(z,q_{0}+q_{1}\right)\geq 2\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}},

(5.40)

which corresponds to the region

\displaystyle\mathscr{V}\left(z,q_{0}+q_{1}\right)\geq\frac{2}{\gamma}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(5.41)

For future convenience we also define the set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}=\left\{\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{1}\left(z,q_{0}+q_{1}\right)\geq\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\right\}.

(5.42)

We next define
\eta_{1}(r)
to be the smooth cutoff function that is identically
1
for
r\leq 3
and identically
0
for
r\geq 4
. We also recall from (
4.55
)–(
4.56
) that
\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{1}\left(z,q_{0}+q_{1}\right)
is supported on
B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)
,
\rho>0
defined in (
4.17
), in
z
coordinates. We let the exterior cutoff,
\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}
, be defined by

\displaystyle\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\left(z,t\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\eta_{1}\left(\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{1}\left(z,q_{0}+q_{1}\right)\right)&\text{if }z\in\supp\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{1}\left(z,q_{0}+q_{1}\right)\\
\eta_{0}\left(\frac{\left|z\right|}{4\rho}\right)&\text{if }z\in\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\setminus\supp\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{1}\left(z,q_{0}+q_{1}\right)\end{array}\right..

We note the two functions that are glued to construct
\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}
match at the boundary of their domains of definition as they are both identically
1
on a region around said boundary. We note that
\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}
is supported on the region

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{1}\left(z,q_{0}+q_{1}\right)\leq 4\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}},

which corresponds to the region

\displaystyle 0\leq\mathscr{V}\left(z,q_{0}+q_{1}\right)\leq\frac{4}{\gamma}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(5.45)

It is clear that

\displaystyle 1\leq\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}+\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\ \ \mathrm{on}\ B_{4\rho}\left(0\right),

(5.46)

\displaystyle\left|\grad_{z}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right|+\left|\grad_{z}\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

some absolute constant
C>0
. Moreover, using the definitions (
5.39
) and (
5.2
), as well as the bounds (
4.6
)–(
4.9
), we have that the
\supp{\partial_{t}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}}\cup\supp{\nabla_{z}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}}\subset\supp{\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}}
, and that on the supports of
\partial_{t}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}
and
\nabla_{z}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}
,

\displaystyle\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{\mathfrak{e}},

(5.47)

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{t}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}}{t},

(5.48)

\displaystyle\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}+\left|\nabla_{z}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}.

(5.49)

With the definitions (
5.39
) and (
5.2
) in hand we define, for
1\leq r\leq\infty
, the space
\mathscr{Y}_{r}
as all functions on
\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}
with

\displaystyle\left\|\Phi\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{r}}\coloneqq\left\|\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{1}\left(q_{0}+q_{1}\right)^{\frac{1}{r}-1}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\Phi\right\|_{L^{r}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}+\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{1}{r}\right)}\left\|\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\Phi\right\|_{L^{r}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}<\infty.

(5.50)

We have that if
\supp\Phi\subset B_{4\rho}\left(0\right)
, then by (
5.46
) we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Phi\right\|_{L^{r}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\leq C\left\|\Phi\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{r}}.

(5.51)

Remark 5.3
.

As
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}=\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{1}\left(q\right)
, for
r=2
we see a similar structure in the first term on the right hand side of (
5.50
) when comparing to (
5.36
). This first term on the right hand side of (
5.50
) will require most care when deriving a priori estimates, whereas sufficient bounds for the second term can be derived using the transport structure of the linearised operator.

Now we record some inequalities related to the Poisson equation
-\Delta\tilde{\psi}_{*R}=\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
that we will need later on.

5.3
The Poisson Equation

In this section,
5.3
, we work in
y
coordinates defined in (
5.3
). We recall from (
5.14
) that
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}

\tilde{\psi}_{*R}
are related by

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\left(y,t\right)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\log{\left(\frac{\left|v-\bar{y}\right|}{\left|v-y\right|}\right)}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\left(v,t\right)dv,

with
\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\left(y_{1},0\right)=0
. Accordingly we would like to prove effective bounds on
\bar{\psi}
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
satisfying
\psi\left(y_{1},0\right)=0
and related to
\bar{\phi}
by
-\Delta\bar{\psi}=\bar{\phi}
, and thus by

\displaystyle\bar{\psi}\left(y\right)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\log{\left(\frac{\left|v-\bar{y}\right|}{\left|v-y\right|}\right)}\bar{\phi}\left(v\right)dv

(5.52)

We make the assumptions

\displaystyle\supp\bar{\phi}\subset B_{3\rho}\left(q^{\prime}e_{2}\right),\ \ \ \left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}<\infty,\ \ \ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\bar{\phi}\left(v\right)dv=0,

(5.53)

where the definition of the
\mathscr{Y}_{2}
norm from (
5.50
) has been shifted to the upper half plane using the relationship
z=y-q^{\prime}e_{2}
, and
\rho>0
is defined in (
4.17
). Note that (
5.53
) implies for
1\leq r\leq\infty

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{r}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}\leq C\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{r}}.

First we use the
0
mass condition in (
5.53
) to obtain estimates on
\bar{\psi}
and
\nabla\bar{\psi}
that will be crucial in obtaining a priori estimates for solutions to the system (
5.31
). For convenience we recall the definition of the Hölder seminorm of order
\beta
for some
\beta\in\left(0,1\right)
:

\displaystyle\left[F\right]_{\beta}\left(y\right)\coloneqq\sup_{y_{1},y_{2}\in B_{1}\left(y\right)}\frac{\left|F\left(y_{1}\right)-F\left(y_{2}\right)\right|}{\left|y_{1}-y_{2}\right|^{\beta}}.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
\bar{\phi}
satisfy (
5.53
), and let
\bar{\psi}
depend on
\bar{\phi}
by (
5.52
). Suppose further that
\bar{\phi}\in L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{r_{0}}_{+}\right)
, some
r_{0}>2
. Then for all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
and
\infty>r_{1}>2
, and some
\beta\in\left(0,1\right)
we have

\displaystyle\left|\bar{\psi}\left(y\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)},

(5.54)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\bar{\psi}\left(y\right)\right\|_{L^{r_{1}}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)},

(5.55)

\displaystyle\left|\nabla\bar{\psi}\left(y\right)\right|+\left[\nabla\bar{\psi}\right]_{\beta}\left(y\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{r_{0}}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)},

(5.56)

where
C>0
is an absolute constant.

Remark 5.5
.

The
0
mass condition in (
5.53
) allows us to obtain the bounds (
5.54
)–(
5.56
) with
C
an absolute constant independent of
\varepsilon
. Without the mass condition, due to the fact that
\supp\bar{\phi}\subset B_{2\rho}\left(q^{\prime}e_{2}\right)
in
y
coordinates,
C
would in general grow like
\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|
as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

The proof for the inequalities in Lemma
5.4
are standard elliptic estimates and we omit them. If estimates (
5.54
), (
5.55
), and (
5.56
) hold, and moreover
\bar{\phi}\in L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)
, we have the following interpolation estimate.

Lemma 5.6
.

Suppose
\bar{\phi}
satisfies (
5.53
), and in addition,
\bar{\phi}\in L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)
. Then given
\sigma\in\left(0,1\right)
, there exist numbers
C_{\sigma}>0
and
\beta\in\left(0,1\right)
such that

\displaystyle\left|\bar{\psi}\left(y\right)\right|+\left|\nabla\bar{\psi}\left(y\right)\right|+\left[\nabla\bar{\psi}\right]_{\beta}\left(y\right)\leq C_{\sigma}\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}^{\sigma}\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}^{1-\sigma}.

(5.57)

Proof.

Since
\bar{\phi}\in L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)
, the compact support of
\bar{\phi}
means that we have
\bar{\phi}\in L^{r_{0}}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)
, any
r_{0}>2
. Moreoever, we know

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}\leq C\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{r_{0}}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}.

Thus, using Lemma
5.4
, we can infer that

\displaystyle\left|\bar{\psi}\left(y\right)\right|+\left|\nabla\bar{\psi}\left(y\right)\right|+\left[\nabla\bar{\psi}\right]_{\beta}\left(y\right)\leq C\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{r_{0}}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}.

From here, interpolation gives us (
5.57
).
∎

5.4
Lower Bound on a Quadratic Form

We now consider functions
\bar{\phi}
on the upper half plane
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
once again satisfying the assumptions

\displaystyle\supp\bar{\phi}\subset B_{3\rho}\left(q^{\prime}e_{2}\right),\ \ \ \left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}<\infty,\ \ \ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\bar{\phi}\left(y\right)dy=0,

(5.58)

for
\rho>0
defined in (
4.17
), as well as the centre of mass conditions

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}y_{1}\bar{\phi}\left(y\right)dy=0,\ \ \ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(y_{2}-q^{\prime}\right)\bar{\phi}\left(y\right)dy=0.

(5.59)

Note that the centre of mass conditions for
\bar{\phi}
match the conditions (
5.10
)–(
5.11
) upon a change of variables to move from
\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}
to
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
.

If
\bar{\phi}
satisfies the above conditions, we gain the following crucial estimate. Recall the definitions of
\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}
,
\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}
, and
\mathfrak{W}
from (
5.39
)–(
5.2
).

Lemma 5.7
.

Suppose
\bar{\phi}
satisfies (
5.58
)–(
5.59
), and
\xi=\left(p,q\right)
satisfies (
4.6
)–(
4.11
). Then for all
t\in\left[T_{0},T\right]
there exists an absolute constant
C_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}-\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}\right)\right)\geq C_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\left|\bar{\phi}\right|^{2}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}+\mathcal{R}_{1},

(5.60)

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\left\|\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}}{t^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}}\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}^{2}\right).

Proof.

Since
\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{2}}<\infty
, due to (
5.58
), we also have
\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}<\infty
. Next, due to the fact that
\gamma\geq 19
, as well as (
4.6
)–(
4.11
), Lemma
4.4
, and (
5.40
), we have

\displaystyle\inf_{y\in\supp\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\geq C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}},

some constant
C>0
, for all
t\in\left[T_{0},T\right]
, where we recall the definition of
\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}
from (
5.39
). Thus

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\left[T_{0},T\right]}\left\|\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}<\infty.

Thus we have that
\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\right)^{-1}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}\in\mathcal{H}=\left\{h\in L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+},\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}dx\right):h\left(y_{1},0\right)=0\right\}
defined in Section
B
, and we can expand in the eigenbasis
\left(e_{j}\right)_{j\geq 0}
related to the operator
\mathfrak{T}\left(h\right)=\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}h\right)
also defined in Section
B
. Let

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bar{\phi}_{j}e_{j}.

(5.61)

Then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}-\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}\right)\right)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}-\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}-\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathfrak{T}\left(\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\right)=\left(\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}},\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)_{\mathcal{H}}-\left(\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}},\mathfrak{T}\left(\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\right)_{\mathcal{H}},

where
\left(\mathrel{\;\cdot\;},\mathrel{\;\cdot\;}\right)_{\mathcal{H}}
is the inner product associated to
\mathcal{H}
. Then in the eigenbasis
\left(e_{j}\right)
with associated eigenvalues
\left(\mu_{j}\left(\varepsilon\right)\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}-\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{2}\left(1-\mu_{j}\right)\left|\bar{\phi}_{j}\right|^{2}+\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}\left(1-\mu_{j}\right)\left|\bar{\phi}_{j}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\mu_{0}\right)\left|\bar{\phi}_{0}\right|^{2}+\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}\left(1-\mu_{j}\right)\left|\bar{\phi}_{j}\right|^{2},

(5.62)

where we have used the fact that
\mu_{1}=\mu_{2}=1
for all
\varepsilon>0
. From Section
B
we also have that
e_{0}
has eigenvalue
\mu_{0}\left(\varepsilon\right)\to\infty
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. From Lemma
B.1
we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}\left(1-\mu_{j}\right)\left|\bar{\phi}_{j}\right|^{2}\geq\left(1-\delta\right)\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}\left|\bar{\phi}_{j}\right|^{2},

(5.63)

some absolute constant
\delta\in\left(0,1\right)
. For
j=0
, using the orthogonality of the eigenbasis, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{0}\bar{\phi}_{0}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)e_{0}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}\right)e_{0}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\bar{\phi}\right)e_{0}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}\right)-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\bar{\phi}\right)\right)e_{0}.

(5.64)

The first term on the right hand side of (
5.64
) has the bound

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\bar{\phi}\right)e_{0}\right|\leq C\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}\leq C\left(\left\|\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}}{\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}+\left\|\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}\right),

where
C>0
can be taken as an absolute constant due to the
0
mass condition for
\bar{\phi}
and Lemma
5.4
, and we have used (
5.46
) in the second inequality. For the second term on the right hand side of (
5.64
), on the region
\mathfrak{W}=\left\{\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{1}\left(y-q^{\prime}e_{2},q_{0}+q_{1}\right)\geq\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\right\}
defined in (
5.42
),
\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\equiv 1
, so we have

\displaystyle\left|\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}\right)-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\bar{\phi}\right)\right|\leq C\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left\|\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathfrak{W}^{\mathsf{c}}\right)}\leq C\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left\|\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)},

(5.65)

where the factor of
\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}
on the right hand side of (
5.65
) comes from the fact that
\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\equiv 1
on the intersection of the support of
\bar{\phi}
and
\mathfrak{W}^{\mathsf{c}}
, due to the definition (
5.2
) and (
5.58
).

On
\mathfrak{W}^{\mathsf{c}}
we instead have the bound

\displaystyle\left|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}\right)-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\bar{\phi}\right)\right)\right|\leq C\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left\|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathfrak{W}^{\mathsf{c}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}.

Once again using
\gamma\geq 19
, as well as (
4.6
)–(
4.11
), Lemma
4.4
, and (
5.40
), we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathfrak{W}^{\mathsf{c}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-1}{4}}}{t^{\frac{\gamma-1}{4}}}.

Thus

\displaystyle\mu_{0}\left|\bar{\phi}_{0}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\mu_{0}}\left\|\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}}{\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}^{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\left\|\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}^{2}+\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\left\|\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathfrak{W}^{\mathsf{c}}\right)}\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\mu_{0}}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\left|\bar{\phi}_{j}\right|^{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\left\|\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}}{t^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}}\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}^{2}\right),

(5.66)

where we have used the fact that
\mu_{0}\to\infty
as
\varepsilon\to 0
in the first inequality, and (
5.61
) in the second inequality.

Next, we use the centre of mass condition with respect to
y_{1}
given in (
5.59
) to get

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}y_{1}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\ dy+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(1-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)y_{1}\bar{\phi}\ dy

\displaystyle=\bar{\phi}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}y_{1}e_{1}\ dy+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}y_{1}\left(\sum_{j\neq 1}\bar{\phi}_{j}e_{j}\right)\ dy+\mathcal{O}\left(\left\|\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathfrak{W}^{\mathsf{c}}\right)}\right),

\displaystyle=\bar{\phi}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}y_{1}e_{1}\ dy+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}y_{1}\left(\sum_{j\neq 1,2}\bar{\phi}_{j}e_{j}\right)\ dy+\mathcal{O}\left(\left\|\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathfrak{W}^{\mathsf{c}}\right)}\right),

where we have again used (
5.61
), and the final equality is due to the fact that
e_{2}
is even in
y_{1}
. Since
e_{1}
is, up to normalization constant, equal to
\partial_{1}\Psi_{R}
, we have that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}y_{1}e_{1}\ dy\geq c_{0}>0,

some absolute constant
c_{0}
. Thus, we have
