\displaystyle c_{1}\left(\left|\bar{\phi}_{1}\right|^{2}+\left|\bar{\phi}_{2}\right|^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\left\|\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}^{2}\right)\leq\sum_{j\neq 1,2}\left|\bar{\phi}_{j}\right|^{2},

(5.67)

as the argument for
\bar{\phi}_{2}
using the centre of mass condition with respect to
y_{2}-q^{\prime}
in (
5.59
) is completely analogous.

Using (
5.63
), (
5.66
), and (
5.67
), we obtain for (
5.62
)

\displaystyle\left(1-\mu_{0}\right)\left|\bar{\phi}_{0}\right|^{2}+\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}\left(1-\mu_{j}\right)\left|\bar{\phi}_{j}\right|^{2}\geq\left(1-\frac{C}{\mu_{0}}\right)\left|\bar{\phi}_{0}\right|^{2}+\left(1-\delta-\frac{C}{\mu_{0}}\right)\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}\left|\bar{\phi}_{j}\right|^{2}-\frac{C}{\mu_{0}}\left(\left|\bar{\phi}_{1}\right|^{2}+\left|\bar{\phi}_{2}\right|^{2}\right)+\mathcal{R}_{0}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{C}{\mu_{0}}\right)\left|\bar{\phi}_{0}\right|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\delta-\frac{C}{\mu_{0}}\right)\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}\left|\bar{\phi}_{j}\right|^{2}+\frac{c_{1}}{2}\left(1-\delta-\left(1+\frac{2}{c_{1}}\right)\frac{C}{\mu_{0}}\right)\left(\left|\bar{\phi}_{1}\right|^{2}+\left|\bar{\phi}_{2}\right|^{2}\right)+\mathcal{R}_{1},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\left\|\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}}{t^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}}\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\right)}^{2}\right).

Then, once again using that
\mu_{0}\to\infty
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, as well as (
5.61
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}-\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\bar{\phi}\right)\right)\geq C_{0}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\left|\bar{\phi}\right|^{2}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)+\mathcal{R}_{1},

for some absolute constant
C_{0}>0
, as required.
∎

5.5
Weighted
L^{2}
A Priori Estimates

With Lemma
5.7
in hand, in this section we obtain a priori estimates in a weighted
L^{2}
space for solutions to a certain linear transport-like operator. Before we introduce this operator, we first give some motivation. We want to deal with an operator that has structure compatible with the integrand on the left hand side of the inequality (
5.60
) that we showed in Lemma
5.7
. To this end, we consider the first three terms on the right hand side of (
5.24
) given by

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\left(z,t\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R}\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\Psi_{0}-\varepsilon\dot{p}z_{2}+\varepsilon\dot{q}z_{1}+\psi_{R}+\psi_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}.

(5.68)

Here we have suppressed any dependence on
\lambda
temporarily. Recalling that
\phi_{R}=\phi_{R1}+\phi_{R2}
from Theorem
4.7
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(U_{R}+\phi_{R}\right)=

\displaystyle-\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}+\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\cdot\nabla_{z}\phi_{R2}.

(5.69)

We can rewrite this as

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)+\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\cdot\nabla_{z}\phi_{R2}

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\right)-\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}.

(5.70)

The last two terms of (
5.70
) give

\displaystyle\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}-\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\nabla^{\perp}{z}\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\right),

whence the first two terms cancel, and thus (
5.69
) can be written as

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)+\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\right)

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\cdot\nabla_{z}\phi_{R2}.

(5.71)

Now we go back to (
5.68
). Using (
5.69
)–(
5.71
), we can write (
5.68
) as

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\left(z,t\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\Psi_{0}-\varepsilon\dot{p}z_{2}+\varepsilon\dot{q}z_{1}+\psi_{R}+\psi_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R}-\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\right)\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\Psi_{0}-\varepsilon\dot{p}z_{2}+\varepsilon\dot{q}z_{1}+\psi_{R}+\psi_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}-\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)+\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\Psi_{0}-\varepsilon\dot{p}z_{2}+\varepsilon\dot{q}z_{1}+\psi_{R}+\psi_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}-\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\cdot\nabla_{z}\phi_{R2}.

(5.72)

The last three terms of (
5.72
) can be written as a sum of terms that are either quadratic in the unknowns
\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)
or linear in these unknowns with coefficients that are small enough so that they can also be treated as error terms in the fixed point formulation alongside the quadratic terms. For the term on the second line, we note that the term

\displaystyle\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\Psi_{0}-\varepsilon\dot{p}z_{2}+\varepsilon\dot{q}z_{1}+\psi_{R}+\psi_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}-\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)+\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\right)

is small enough due to the presence of the term
-\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)+\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)
inducing some cancellations of higher order terms; see Lemmas
4.6
,
4.7
, and (
4.86
). The term on the third line of (
5.72
) is similar, with the very last term having this property due to the size of
\phi_{R2}
.

On
\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}
, we also have

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)+\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\right)=\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\left|\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right|^{2}\right),

and recalling the structure of
\phi_{R1}/\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
from (
4.86
), on
\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}
we can write (
5.68
) schematically as

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\left(z,t\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R}-f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)+a_{*}\right)\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\right)+E,

(5.73)

where
a_{*}
is the collection of terms not depending on the unknowns,
a
is the collection of all other terms, and
E
are all the quadratic and small linear terms with respect to the unknowns that can be treated as errors in a fixed point formulation. Having obtained (
5.73
), we see the term under
\nabla_{z}
has similar structure to one of the factors in the integrand on the left hand side of (
5.60
), which is what we desired.

Recalling that the homotopic operators (
5.26
) and (
5.28
) were defined to have no nonlinear dependence on the unknowns
\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)
at
\lambda=0
, we first wish to study solutions to a suitable linear (in
\tilde{\phi}_{*}
) problem. With the discussion (
5.68
)–(
5.73
) in hand, we will derive a priori estimates for the system

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{t}+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{b}}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{b}}\left(\phi-\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q_{b}\right)+a_{*}\right)\psi\right)+E=0\ \ \text{in}\ \mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{b}}\times\left[T_{0},T\right],

(5.74a)

\displaystyle\phi(\mathrel{\;\cdot\;},T)=0\ \ \text{in}\ \mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{b}},

(5.74b)

\displaystyle\phi\left(z_{b},t\right)=0\ \ \text{on}\ \partial\mathbb{H}_{-q_{b}^{\prime}}\times\left[T_{0},T\right],

(5.74c)

where we have used the coordinate transformation

\displaystyle z_{b}=\frac{x-\xi_{0}\left(t\right)-\xi_{1}\left(t\right)-b\left(t\right)}{\varepsilon},

(5.75)

with
b\left(t\right)
,
a\left(z_{b},t\right)
, and
a_{*}\left(z_{b},t\right)
are some given functions, and we define
\xi^{\left(b\right)}
analogously to
\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}
in (
5.19
):

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(b\right)}=\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}+b.

(5.76)

Moreover,
E\left(z,t\right)
is some error, and we recall the definition of
\mathscr{V}
and
\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathrel{\;\cdot\;}\right)=\gamma\left(\mathrel{\;\cdot\;}\right)^{\gamma-1}_{+}\chi_{B_{\frac{s}{\varepsilon}}\left(0\right)}(z_{b})
from (
4.51
) and (
4.52
). In particular, in our chosen coordinate system (
5.75
), we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{V}\left(q_{b}\right)=\Psi_{R}\left(z_{b},t\right)-c_{b}\varepsilon\left(z_{b,2}+q^{\prime}_{b}\right)-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega_{b}.

Note the analogous structures to the operators in (
5.73
) and (
5.74a
).

Here
\psi
is given by

\displaystyle\psi\left(z,t\right)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{b}}}\log{\left(\frac{\left|v-\overline{\left(z_{b}+q^{\prime}_{b}e_{2}\right)}\right|}{\left|v-\left(z_{b}+q^{\prime}_{b}e_{2}\right)\right|}\right)}\phi\left(v,t\right)dv,

(5.77)

and we impose that for all
t\in\left[T_{0},T\right]
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{b}}}\phi\left(z_{b},t\right)\ dz_{b}=0,\ \ \ \int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{b}}}z_{b,1}\phi\left(z_{b},t\right)\ dz_{b}=0,\ \ \ \int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{b}}}z_{b,2}\phi\left(z,t\right)\ dz_{b}=0.

(5.78)

On the functions
a_{*}\left(z_{b},t\right)
and
a\left(z_{b},t\right)
, we assume

\displaystyle\Delta\left(a_{*}+a\right)\in L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q_{b}^{\prime}}\times\left[T_{0},T\right]\right),

(5.79)

We note that this implies
\nabla_{z_{b}}a
has a uniform Log-Lipschitz bound with respect to its space variable. That is, for all
t\in\left[T_{0},T\right]
, and
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{b}}
,

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{z_{b}}a\left(z_{1},t\right)-\nabla_{z_{b}}a\left(z_{2},t\right)\right|\leq C\left|z_{1}-z_{2}\right|\left|\log{\left|z_{1}-z_{2}\right|}\right|,

(5.80)

for an absolute constant
C>0
.

We also assume that for some constants
C>0
and
\nu>3/4
, we have, for all
t\in\left[T_{0},T\right]
,

\displaystyle\left|a_{*}\right|+\left|\nabla_{z_{b}}a_{*}\right|+\left|D^{2}_{z_{b}}a_{*}\right|+\left|\partial_{t}a_{*}\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{2}}{t^{2}},

(5.81)

\displaystyle\left|a\right|+\left|\nabla_{z_{b}}a\right|\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2+\nu}}{t^{1+\nu}},

(5.82)

On
b=(b_{1},b_{2})
we assume that

\displaystyle\left\|tb_{1}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}+\left\|t^{2}\dot{b}_{1}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{3+\nu},

(5.83)

\displaystyle\left\|t^{2}b_{2}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}+\left\|t^{3}\dot{b}_{2}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{3+\nu}.

(5.84)

Finally, due to the definitions of
\mathscr{W}_{1}
and
\mathscr{V}
, as well as the assumption on
a_{*}
in (
5.81
), we know that
\supp\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\subset B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)
,
\rho>0
defined in (
4.17
), for all
t\in\left[T_{0},T\right]
. We make the same assumption on
E
, and only study solutions to (
5.74
) that enjoy a similar property:

\displaystyle\supp E\left(t\right)\subset B_{2\rho}\left(0\right),\quad\supp\phi\left(t\right)\subset B_{3\rho}\left(0\right)\quad t\in\left[T_{0},T\right],

(5.85)

for
\rho>0
defined in (
4.17
). With these assumptions in mind, we now prove a priori estimates for solutions to (
5.74
) satisfying (
5.77
)–(
5.85
) over the next two lemmas. We will once again suppress dependence on
b
in the statement and proof, but we will point out whenever we use (
5.83
)–(
5.84
).

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
E\left(z,t\right)\in\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}
, defined in (
5.50
). Suppose
\phi
solves (
5.74
) with assumptions (
5.78
)–(
5.85
) holding, and suppose
\xi=\left(p,q\right)
is of the form (
5.76
), with (
4.6
)–(
4.9
), and (
5.83
)–(
5.84
) satisfied. Then for all
\varepsilon>0
small enough, and all
t\in\left[T_{0},T\right]
,

\displaystyle\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathfrak{W}^{\mathsf{c}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma}{2}-3}}{t^{\frac{\gamma}{2}-1}}\int_{t}^{T}\left(\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}+\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\right)d\tau,

(5.86)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{4}\right)}\left\|\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-11}{4}}}{t^{\frac{\gamma-3}{4}}}\int_{t}^{T}\left(\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}+\left\|\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\right)d\tau.

(5.87)

Proof.

Owing to the transport-like structure of (
5.74a
), let
t\in\left[T_{0},T\right]
, and define characteristics
\bar{z}\left(\tau,t,z\right)
by the ODE problem

\displaystyle\frac{d\bar{z}}{d\tau}=\varepsilon^{-2}\nabla^{\perp}_{\bar{z}}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau,t,z\right),\tau\right),\ \ \ \tau\in\left[t,T\right],

(5.88a)

\displaystyle\bar{z}\left(t,t,z\right)=z.

(5.88b)

The first thing to note is that due to (
5.80
)–(
5.81
), we have definiteness of the characteristics. We also have a good estimate on
\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau\right),\tau\right)
as
\tau
varies. Let
t<\tau_{1}
and note

\displaystyle\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau_{1}\right),\tau_{1}\right)-\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(z,t\right)=\int_{t}^{\tau_{1}}\partial_{\tau}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)d\tau.

Then we note that due to (
5.88a
)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)=\varepsilon^{-2}\grad_{\bar{z}}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\cdot\nabla^{\perp}_{\bar{z}}\left(a\right)\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau\right),\tau\right)+\partial_{\tau}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(Z,\tau\right)\rvert_{Z=\bar{z}\left(\tau\right)}.

Then, because of (
4.6
)–(
4.9
), (
5.83
)–(
5.84
), (
4.51
), and (
5.81
)–(
5.82
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{\tau}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\right|\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{\nu}}{t^{1+\nu}},

some absolute constants
C>0
and
\nu>3/4
. Thus, for all
\tau_{1}\in\left[t,T\right]
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau_{1}\right),\tau_{1}\right)-\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(z,t\right)\right|\leq C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\frac{3}{4}}.

(5.89)

Then, using the characteristics defined by the system (
5.88
), we can write a solution to (
5.74
),
\phi
, as

\displaystyle\phi\left(z,t\right)=\varepsilon^{-2}\int_{t}^{T}\left(-E\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau\right),\tau\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{\bar{z}}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\cdot\nabla_{\bar{z}}\left(\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\psi\right)\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau\right),\tau\right)\right)d\tau.

Now, because of (
5.85
),
E\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau\right),\tau\right)
is only non zero when
\bar{z}\left(\tau\right)\in B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)
. Thus, using (
5.51
), we have, for all
\tau\in\left[t,T\right]
,

\displaystyle\left|E\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau\right),\tau\right)\right|\leq C\left(\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q_{0}+q_{1}\right)\right)\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau\right),\tau\right)+\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{2}\right)}\right)\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}.

We note that since
z\in\mathfrak{W}^{\mathsf{c}}
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathscr{V}\left(q_{0}+q_{1}\right)\left(z,t\right)\right|\leq\frac{1}{\gamma}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(5.90)

Then (
4.52
), (
5.81
), (
5.89
), and (
5.90
) give, for all
\tau\in\left[t,T\right]

\displaystyle\left|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q_{0}+q_{1}\right)\right)\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau\right),\tau\right)\right|\leq C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{2}\right)},

(5.91)

which in turn gives, for
z\in\mathfrak{W}^{\mathsf{c}}
and all
\tau\in\left[t,T\right]
,

\displaystyle\left|E\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau,t,z\right),\tau\right)\right|\leq C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{2}\right)}\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}\left(\tau\right).

Thus we have that

\displaystyle\left|\phi\left(z,t\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{-2}\int_{t}^{T}\left(\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\tau}\right)^{\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{2}\right)}\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}+\left|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\right|\left|\nabla_{\bar{z}}\psi\right|\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau\right),\tau\right)\right)d\tau

\displaystyle+C\varepsilon^{-2}\int_{t}^{T}\left|\mathscr{W}_{2}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\right|\left|\psi\right|\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau\right),\tau\right)d\tau,

where we have bounded
\nabla^{\perp}_{\bar{z}}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}+a\right)
in
L^{\infty}
. We note that the inequality (
5.91
) also applies to
\mathscr{W}_{2}
with exponent
\left(\gamma-2\right)/2
. Then, using Lemma
4.4
, (
5.83
)–(
5.84
), and (
5.81
), we have that for
z\in\mathfrak{W}^{c}
and all
\tau\in\left[t,T\right]
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau\right),\tau\right)\right|\leq C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{2}\right)},

and once again analogously for
\mathscr{W}_{2}
with exponent
\left(\gamma-2\right)/2
. Therefore, using (
5.78
) to apply Lemma
5.4
and (
5.85
) to apply (
5.51
) as well as the Minkowski integral inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathfrak{W}^{\mathsf{c}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{-2}\left(\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{2}\right)}\int_{t}^{T}\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}d\tau+\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\left(\frac{\gamma-2}{2}\right)}\int_{t}^{T}\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}d\tau\right)

\displaystyle+C\varepsilon^{-2}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{2}\right)}\int_{t}^{T}\left(\int_{\supp{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau,z\right),\tau\right)}}\left|\nabla_{\bar{z}}\psi\right|^{2}\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau\right),\tau\right)dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d\tau.

(5.92)

Let us focus on the space integral in the last term of (
5.92
),

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\supp{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau,z\right),\tau\right)}}\left|\nabla_{\bar{z}}\psi\right|^{2}\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau,z\right),\tau\right)dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(5.93)

where we emphasize the dependence of
\bar{z}
on
z
. Now, we note that the system of differential equations that
\bar{z}
satisfies in (
5.88a
) abstractly takes the form

\displaystyle\dot{\bar{z}}=F\left(\bar{z},\tau\right),

where
F
is divergence free. We have

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\left(\nabla_{z}\bar{z}\right)=\nabla_{\bar{z}}F\left(\bar{z},\tau\right)\nabla_{z}\bar{z}.

(5.94)

Then using Jacobi’s formula we have

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\left(\det{\nabla_{z}\bar{z}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\det{\nabla_{z}\bar{z}}\right)\tr{\left(\left(\nabla_{z}\bar{z}\right)^{-1}\partial_{\tau}\left(\nabla_{z}\bar{z}\right)\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(\det{\nabla_{z}\bar{z}}\right)\tr{\left(\nabla_{\bar{z}}F\left(\bar{z},\tau\right)\right)}

\displaystyle=0,

where the second equality is due to (
5.94
), and the third equality is due to
F
being divergence free. Thus we have that the Jacobian of the transformation sending
z
to
\bar{z}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathscr{J}\left(z,\bar{z}\right)=1

(5.95)

for all
\tau\in\left[t,T\right]
. Next we note that in the integral (
5.93
)

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\supp{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau,z\right),\tau\right)}}\left|\nabla_{\bar{z}}\psi\right|^{2}\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau,z\right),\tau\right)dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

the region of integration on the right hand side is given as the support of
\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau,z\right),\tau\right)
as a function of
z
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\supp{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau,z\right),\tau\right)}}\left|\nabla_{\bar{z}}\psi\right|^{2}\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau,z\right),\tau\right)dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\supp{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(\bar{z},\tau\right)}}\left|\nabla_{\bar{z}}\psi\right|^{2}\left(\bar{z},\tau\right)\mathscr{J}\left(z,\bar{z}\right)d\bar{z}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\left(\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\left|\nabla_{\bar{z}}\psi\right|^{2}\left(\bar{z},\tau\right)d\bar{z}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(5.96)

where the last inequality comes from the fact that
\supp\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\subset B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)
, for
\rho>0
defined in (
4.17
), for all
t\in\left[T_{0},T\right]
, and (
5.95
). Thus, using (
5.96
), we have

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\supp{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau,z\right),\tau\right)}}\left|\nabla_{\bar{z}}\psi\right|^{2}\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau,z\right),\tau\right)dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla\psi\right\|_{L^{r_{1}}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\leq C\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)},

where we have applied Hölder’s inequality to the left hand side for
r_{1}>2
, using the compact support of the region of integration on the right hand side of (
5.96
), then Lemma
5.4
to bound the
L^{r_{1}}
norm of
\nabla\psi
.

\displaystyle\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathfrak{W}^{\mathsf{c}}\right)}\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma}{2}-3}}{t^{\frac{\gamma}{2}-1}}\int_{t}^{T}\left(\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}+\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\right)d\tau,

giving us (
5.86
).

A very similar calculation also gives

\displaystyle\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{4}\right)}\left\|\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-11}{4}}}{t^{\frac{\gamma-3}{4}}}\int_{t}^{T}\left(\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}+\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\right)d\tau.

(5.97)

Let

\displaystyle\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{4}\right)}\left\|\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}=\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}\left(t\right).

(5.98)

Then, applying (
5.46
) due to assumption on the support of
\phi
(
5.85
), from (
5.97
), we obtain

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-11}{4}}}{t^{\frac{\gamma-3}{4}}}\int_{t}^{T}\left(\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}+\left\|\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\right)d\tau

\displaystyle+\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-11}{4}}}{t^{\frac{\gamma-3}{4}}}\int_{t}^{T}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\tau}\right)^{\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{4}\right)}\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}\left(\tau\right)d\tau,

having used the fact that
\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{1}\left(q_{0}+q_{1}\right)
is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}
on
\left[t,T\right]
. Then applying Grönwall’s inequality gives us

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}\left(t\right)\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-11}{4}}}{t^{\frac{\gamma-3}{4}}}\int_{t}^{T}\left(\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}+\left\|\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\right)d\tau,

as claimed in (
5.87
).
∎

Now we move on to the a priori estimate on the interior region where
\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}
is supported.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
E\left(z,t\right)\in\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}
, defined in (
5.50
), and suppose
E
has the bound

\displaystyle\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}<\frac{\varepsilon^{5-\sigma_{0}}}{t^{3}},

(5.99)

for some small
\sigma_{0}>0
.

Suppose further that
\phi
solves (
5.74
) with assumptions (
5.78
)–(
5.85
) holding, and suppose
\xi=\left(p,q\right)
is of the form (
5.76
), with (
4.6
)–(
4.9
), and (
5.83
)–(
5.84
) satisfied.. Then for all
\varepsilon>0
small enough, and all
T_{0}>0
large enough, we have for all
t\in\left[T_{0},T\right]
,

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{3-\sigma_{0}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t^{2-\frac{\sigma_{0}}{2}}},

(5.100)

for some absolute constant
C>0
.

Remark 5.10
.

As mentioned in Remark
4.2
, we can enlarge
T_{0}
if necessary as long as we keep it independent of
\varepsilon
.

Proof.

Take the equality (
5.74a
), multiply by

\displaystyle\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}G\coloneqq\left(\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)\right),

(5.101)

and integrate over
\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}
to obtain

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\partial_{t}\phi\left(\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)\right)dz

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)G\left(z,t\right)\ \nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\phi-\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\psi\right)dz

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\left(\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)\right)E\ dz=0,

(5.102)

where the region of integration is due to the support of
\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}
given in (
5.40
) and the fact that
\tilde{\mathscr{W}}_{1}\left(z,q_{0}+q_{1}\right)
is a nonnegative function supported on
B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)
,
\rho>0
defined in (
4.17
).

For the first term on the left hand side of (
5.102
), we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi\ \partial_{t}\phi\ dz}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left(\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi^{2}\ dz}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\partial_{t}\left(\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}\right)\phi^{2}\ dz.

(5.103)

In order to bound the last term on the right hand side of (
5.103
), we first note that on
B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)
, (
5.39
)–(
5.2
) as well as the bounds (
4.56
)–(
4.57
), (
5.83
)–(
5.84
) and (
5.81
) give that on the support of
\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}
,

\displaystyle\left(1+C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}\right)\mathscr{V}\left(q_{0}+q_{1}\right)\geq\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)\geq\left(1-C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}\right)\mathscr{V}\left(q_{0}+q_{1}\right),

(5.104)

for some constant
C>0
. Then (
5.104
), (
5.41
), (
5.47
), and (
5.48
) give

\displaystyle\left|\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\partial_{t}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right|\left|\frac{1}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}\right|\leq\frac{C\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}^{2}}{t}\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}.

(5.105)

Next, we note that due to (
4.6
)-(
4.9
),(
5.83
)–(
5.84
), (
5.81
), and (
5.41
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\partial_{t}\left(\frac{1}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}}{\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}},

where we also recall the definition of
\mathscr{W}_{0}
and
\mathscr{W}_{1}
from (
4.53
)–(
4.54
). Thus

\displaystyle\left|\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\partial_{t}\left(\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}\right)\phi^{2}\ dz\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}\left\|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}^{2}+\frac{C\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}^{2}\left(t\right)}{t},

(5.106)

where
\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}
is defined in (
5.98
).

Next we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\partial_{t}\phi\right)\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)dz=\varepsilon^{2}\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\partial_{t}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)dz-\varepsilon^{2}\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\left(\phi\partial_{t}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)dz

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left(\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)dz\right)-\varepsilon^{2}\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\left(\phi\partial_{t}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)dz.

(5.107)

Similarly to (
5.105
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\left(\phi\partial_{t}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)dz\right|\leq\frac{C}{t}\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\left|\phi\right|\left|\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)\right|dz.

(5.108)

Bounding
\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)
in
L^{\infty}
, using the representation formula (
5.77
), and then using the compact support of the integral on the right hand side of (
5.108
) to bound
\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}
in
L^{2}
gives us

\displaystyle\left|\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\left(\phi\partial_{t}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)dz\right|\leq\frac{C\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{4}\right)}\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}\left(t\right)\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}.

Note the factor of
\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|
on the right hand side due to the fact that
\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi
is not mass free and hence Lemma
5.4
does not apply. Then we use (
5.51
) for
\phi
and obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\left(\phi\partial_{t}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)dz\right|\leq\frac{C\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{4}\right)}\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}\left(t\right)\left(\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}\left(t\right)+\left\|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{4}\right)}\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}\left(t\right)^{2}+C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{4}\right)}\left\|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}^{2},

(5.109)

where, for second bound in (
5.109
) we have applied Young’s Inequality.

For the term on the second line on the left hand side of (
5.102
), we first note that since
\psi=\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\phi\right)
,

\displaystyle\nabla_{z}\left(\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\psi\right)=\nabla_{z}\left(\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\left(1-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)\phi\right)\right)+\nabla_{z}\left(\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\mathscr{W}_{2}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\left(1-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)\phi\right)

\displaystyle+\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\nabla_{z}\left(\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\left(1-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)\phi\right)\right)+\nabla_{z}\left(\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)\right),

(5.110)

where we recall the definition of
\mathscr{W}_{2}
from (
4.53
)–(
4.54
). Substituting (
5.110
) in to the integral on the second line of (
5.102
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)G\left(z,t\right)\ \nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\phi-\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\psi\right)dz

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)G\left(z,t\right)\ \nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)G\left(z,t\right)\right)dz

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mathcal{I}_{i},

(5.111)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}=\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}\mathscr{W}_{2}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\left(1-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)\phi\right)\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)dz,

(5.112)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}=-\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)\mathscr{W}_{2}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\left(1-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)\phi\right)\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)dz,

(5.113)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3}=\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\left(1-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)\phi\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)dz,

(5.114)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{4}=-\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\left(1-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)\phi\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)dz.

(5.115)

We begin to estimate (
5.111
) with the first term on the right hand side, and note

\displaystyle\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)G\left(z,t\right)\ \nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)G\left(z,t\right)\right)dz

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\ \nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(G^{2}\left(z,t\right)\right)dz

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)\mathscr{W}_{2}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)G^{2}\left(z,t\right)\ \nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)dz

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)G^{2}\left(z,t\right)\ \nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)\right)dz

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)\mathscr{W}_{2}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)G^{2}\left(z,t\right)\ \nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)dz,

(5.116)

where the last inequality comes from integration by parts. For the first term on the right hand side of (
5.116
), recalling the definition of
G
in (
5.101
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)G^{2}\left(z,t\right)\ \nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)\right)dz\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi^{2}}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}\left|\nabla_{z}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)\right)\right|dz+\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\left|\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)\right|^{2}\left|\nabla_{z}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)\right)\right|dz\right).

(5.117)

For the first term on the right hand side, we note that the integrand is supported on
\supp{\nabla_{z}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}}
, where (
5.49
) gives that
\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\leq C\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}
. For the second term, we can once again use the fact that the integrand is supported on
\supp{\nabla_{z}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}}\subset\supp{\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}}
, whence applying (
5.104
), and (
5.45
) gives the necessary bound on
\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)
to provide the powers of
\varepsilon
and
t+1
appearing in front of the integral. Finally, we can bound the
L^{2}
norm of
\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)
in terms of the
L^{2}
norm of
\eta_{i}\phi
for the price of a factor of
\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|
. Taking all this together gives us

\displaystyle\left|\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)G^{2}\left(z,t\right)\ \nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)\right)dz\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}\left(t\right)^{2}+\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\left\|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}^{2}\right).

(5.118)

For the second term on the right hand side of (
5.116
), using (
5.82
), (
5.104
), and (
5.41
), we have the bound

\displaystyle\left|\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)\mathscr{W}_{2}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(a\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{11}{4}}}{t^{\frac{7}{4}}}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}{\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{11}{4}}}{t^{\frac{7}{4}}}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}{\left(1-C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}\right)\mathscr{V}\left(q_{0}+q_{1}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{9}{4}}}{t^{\frac{5}{4}}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right).

(5.119)

Then, analogously to (
5.117
)–(
5.118
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)\mathscr{W}_{2}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)G^{2}\left(z,t\right)\ \nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)dz\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{9}{4}}}{t^{\frac{5}{4}}}\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(z,t\right)\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)G^{2}\left(z,t\right)dz

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{9}{4}}}{t^{\frac{5}{4}}}\left(\left\|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}^{2}+\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}\left(t\right)^{2}\right).

(5.120)

For
\mathcal{I}_{1}
defined in (
5.112
), we can first write

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{I}_{1}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\left|\frac{\nabla_{z}\left(a\right)}{\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}}\right|\left|\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right|\left|\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\left(1-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)\phi\right)\right|dz

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{9}{4}}}{t^{\frac{5}{4}}}\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\left|\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right|\left|\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\left(1-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)\phi\right)\right|dz,

(5.121)

where the last bound in (
5.121
) is obtained in an analogous way to (
5.119
) using (
5.82
). Now we apply Young’s inequality to the integrand in (
5.121
), and bound
\left|\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\left(1-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)\phi\right)\right|
using a strategy analogous to (
5.65
) along with (
5.46
), and obtain

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{I}_{1}\right|\leq\frac{C\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{9}{4}}}{t^{\frac{5}{4}}}\left(\left\|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}^{2}+\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}\left(t\right)^{2}\right).

(5.122)

Similarly for
\mathcal{I}_{2}
defined in (
5.113
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{I}_{2}\right|\leq\frac{C\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{9}{4}}}{t^{\frac{5}{4}}}\left(\left\|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}^{2}+\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}\left(t\right)^{2}\right).

For
\mathcal{I}_{3}
defined in (
5.114
), we use the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and obtain

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{I}_{3}\right|\leq C\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left\|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\left\|\left(1-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)},

where for the second term in the product of norms on the right hand side, we have used

\displaystyle\int\left|\nabla\left(\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\Phi\right)\right|^{2}=\int\Phi\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\Phi

to bound the
\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\left(1-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)\phi\right)\right)
term in the integrand in
\mathcal{I}_{3}
.

Then, once again using a strategy analogous to (
5.65
) and (
5.122
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{I}_{3}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\left\|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\left\|\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-1}{4}}}{t^{\frac{\gamma-1}{4}}}\left(\left\|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}^{2}+\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}\left(t\right)^{2}\right).

Analogously, for
\mathcal{I}_{4}
defined in (
5.115
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{I}_{4}\right|\leq\frac{C\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{3}\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-1}{4}}}{t^{\frac{\gamma-1}{4}}}\left(\left\|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}^{2}+\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}\left(t\right)^{2}\right).

Finally, we estimate the last term on the left hand side of (
5.102
) given by

\displaystyle\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\left(\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}-\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)\right)E\ dz.

To begin with, we again use (
5.104
) to obtain that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi E}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}dz\right|\leq C\left\|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{2}}{t^{1+\sigma_{0}}}\left\|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}^{2}+\frac{Ct^{1+\sigma_{0}}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}^{2}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}E\right)\ dz\right|\leq C\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\left\|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{2}}{t^{1+\sigma_{0}}}\left\|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}^{2}+\frac{Ct^{1+\sigma_{0}}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}^{2},

(5.123)

for
\sigma_{0}>0
taken to be the same as in (
5.99
). Then, using the identities (
5.103
), (
5.107
), (
5.111
)–(
5.115
), as well as bounds (
5.106
), (
5.109
), (
5.117
), and (
5.120
)–(
5.123
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left(\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\left(\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi^{2}}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}-\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)\right)dz\right)

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{C\varepsilon^{2}}{t^{1+\sigma_{0}}}\left\|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}^{2}-\frac{Ct^{1+\sigma_{0}}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}^{2}-C\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}\left(t\right)^{2},

Then integrating on
\left[t,T\right]
, and noting that
\phi\left(T\right)=0
from (
5.74b
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\left(\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi^{2}}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}-\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right)\right)dz

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t}^{T}\frac{C}{\tau^{1+\sigma_{0}}}\left\|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}^{2}d\tau+\int_{t}^{T}\frac{C\tau^{1+\sigma_{0}}}{\varepsilon^{4}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}^{2}d\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{t}^{T}C\varepsilon^{-2}\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}\left(\tau\right)^{2}d\tau.

(5.124)

For the left hand side of (
5.124
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi^{2}}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}dz

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi^{2}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}dz+

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}-\frac{1}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)dz.

(5.125)

Using (
4.56
)–(
4.58
) and (
5.104
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)}-\frac{1}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)dz\right|\leq C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{t}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi^{2}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}dz.

(5.126)

Then applying Lemma
5.7
and (
5.125
)–(
5.126
) to (
5.124
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi^{2}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}dz+\mathcal{R}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t}^{T}\frac{C}{\tau^{1+\sigma_{0}}}\left\|\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}^{2}d\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{t}^{T}\frac{C\tau^{1+\sigma_{0}}}{\varepsilon^{4}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}^{2}ds+\int_{t}^{T}C\varepsilon^{-2}\alpha_{\mathfrak{e}}\left(\tau\right)^{2}d\tau.

(5.127)

where we recall from Lemma
5.7
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\left\|\eta_{\mathfrak{e}}\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}}{t^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}}\left\|\bar{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}^{2}\right).

Now define
\alpha_{\mathfrak{i}}
by

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(t\right)^{2}=\int_{B_{2\rho}\left(0\right)}\frac{\eta_{\mathfrak{i}}^{2}\phi^{2}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}dz.

Using (
5.127
), (
4.56
)–(
4.58
), (
5.104
), Lemma
5.8
, (
5.51
), and recalling the definition (
5.98
), we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(t\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t}^{T}\frac{C\tau^{1+\sigma_{0}}}{\varepsilon^{4}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}^{2}d\tau+\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}}{t^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}}\alpha_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(t\right)^{2}+\int_{t}^{T}\frac{C}{\tau^{1+\sigma_{0}}}\alpha_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(\tau\right)^{2}d\tau

\displaystyle+\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-11}{2}}}{t^{\frac{\gamma-3}{2}}}\left(\int_{t}^{T}\left(\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}+\alpha_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(\tau\right)\right)d\tau\right)^{2}+\int_{t}^{T}\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-19}{2}}}{\tau^{\frac{\gamma-3}{2}}}\left(\int_{\tau}^{T}\left(\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}+\alpha_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(\tau^{\prime}\right)\right)d\tau^{\prime}\right)^{2}d\tau.

Absorbing the second term on the right side into the left hand side gives us

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(t\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t}^{T}\frac{C\tau^{1+\sigma_{0}}}{\varepsilon^{4}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}^{2}d\tau+\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-11}{2}}}{t^{\frac{\gamma-3}{2}}}\left(\int_{t}^{T}\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}d\tau\right)^{2}+\int_{t}^{T}\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-19}{2}}}{\tau^{\frac{\gamma-3}{2}}}\left(\int_{\tau}^{T}\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}d\tau^{\prime}\right)^{2}d\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{t}^{T}\frac{C}{\tau^{1+\sigma_{0}}}\alpha_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(\tau\right)^{2}d\tau+\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-11}{2}}}{t^{\frac{\gamma-3}{2}}}\left(\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(\tau\right)d\tau\right)^{2}+\int_{t}^{T}\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-19}{2}}}{\tau^{\frac{\gamma-3}{2}}}\left(\int_{\tau}^{T}\alpha_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(\tau^{\prime}\right)d\tau^{\prime}\right)^{2}d\tau.

(5.128)

Now, using the bound (
5.99
), we have the first line on the right hand side of (
5.128
) has the bound

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{T}\frac{C\tau^{1+\sigma_{0}}}{\varepsilon^{4}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}^{2}d\tau+\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-11}{2}}}{t^{\frac{\gamma-3}{2}}}\left(\int_{t}^{T}\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}d\tau\right)^{2}+\int_{t}^{T}\frac{C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-19}{2}}}{\tau^{\frac{\gamma-3}{2}}}\left(\int_{\tau}^{T}\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}d\tau^{\prime}\right)^{2}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-3}{2}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{-2}}{T_{0}^{\frac{\gamma-11}{2}+\sigma_{0}}}+\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-11}{2}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{-2}}{T_{0}^{\frac{\gamma-5}{2}+\sigma_{0}}}\right)\frac{C\varepsilon^{6-2\sigma_{0}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}}{t^{4-\sigma_{0}}}\leq\frac{2C\varepsilon^{6-2\sigma_{0}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}}{t^{4-\sigma_{0}}}

(5.129)

for
\varepsilon>0
small enough. We claim that for
T_{0}>0
large enough,

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(t\right)^{2}<\frac{3C\varepsilon^{6-2\sigma_{0}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}}{t^{4-\sigma_{0}}}

(5.130)

on
\left[T_{0},T\right]
.

Clearly (
5.130
) is true for all
t
close enough to
T
as we know
\alpha_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(T\right)=0
. Then, for the sake of contradiction, let
t_{*}
be the first time in
\left[T_{0},T\right]
, moving backwards from
T
, that this bound fails to hold. By continuity we know that at
t_{*}
,

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(t_{*}\right)^{2}=\frac{3C\varepsilon^{6-2\sigma_{0}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}}{t_{*}^{4-\sigma_{0}}}.

(5.131)

Plugging this into the right hand side of (
5.128
), we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(t_{*}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C\varepsilon^{6-2\sigma_{0}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}}{t_{*}^{4-\sigma_{0}}}+\frac{3C^{2}\varepsilon^{6-2\sigma_{0}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}}{t_{*}^{4}}+\left(\frac{3C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-7}{2}}}{\left(1-\frac{\sigma_{0}}{2}\right)^{2}T_{0}^{\frac{\gamma-11}{2}}}\right)\frac{C\varepsilon^{6-2\sigma_{0}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}}{t_{*}^{4-\sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{3C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-19}{2}}}{\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{2}-\sigma_{0}\right)\left(1-\frac{\sigma_{0}}{2}\right)^{2}T_{0}^{\frac{\gamma-9}{2}}}\right)\frac{C\varepsilon^{6-2\sigma_{0}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}}{t_{*}^{4-\sigma_{0}}},

(5.132)

where we have used (
5.129
) to bound the first three terms of (
5.128
), and (
5.130
)–(
5.131
) to bound the last three.

Then collecting like terms in (
5.132
), we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathfrak{i}}\left(t_{*}\right)^{2}\leq\left(2+\frac{3C}{T_{0}^{\sigma_{0}}}+\left(\frac{3C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-7}{2}}}{\left(1-\frac{\sigma_{0}}{2}\right)^{2}T_{0}^{\frac{\gamma-11}{2}}}\right)+\left(\frac{3C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma-19}{2}}}{\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{2}-\sigma_{0}\right)\left(1-\frac{\sigma_{0}}{2}\right)^{2}T_{0}^{\frac{\gamma-9}{2}}}\right)\right)\frac{C\varepsilon^{6-2\sigma_{0}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}}{t_{*}^{4-\sigma_{0}}},

(5.133)

whence, as long as
\gamma\geq 19
, we can clearly enlarge
T_{0}
, whilst still keeping it a constant independent of
\varepsilon
, so that the term in the parentheses on the right hand side of (
5.133
) is
<3
, contradicting (
5.131
), and giving us (
5.130
), and therefore (
5.100
), as required.
∎

Using Lemmas
5.8
and
5.9
, we can get a direct estimate on solutions to the
L^{2}
norm of solutions to (
5.74
) satisfying assumptions (
5.78
)–(
5.85
).

Corollary 5.11
.

Suppose the assumptions of Lemmas
5.8
and
5.9
hold. Then for all
\varepsilon>0
small enough, and all
T_{0}>0
large enough, we have for all
t\in\left[T_{0},T\right]
,

\displaystyle\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{3-\sigma_{0}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t^{2-\frac{\sigma_{0}}{2}}},

for some absolute constant
C>0
and
\sigma_{0}>0
defined in (
5.99
).

We can also use
5.4
alongside Lemmas
5.8
and
5.9
to gain
L^{r}
estimates on
\phi
, any
2<r<\infty
,
L^{\infty}
estimates on
\phi
and
\psi
, and Hölder estimates on
\nabla\psi
, for
\phi
solving (
5.74
) and satisfying assumptions (
5.78
)–(
5.85
). We recall that here
\psi=\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\phi\right)
in the sense of (
5.77
).

Corollary 5.12
.

Suppose the assumptions of Lemmas
5.8
and
5.9
hold. Then for all
\varepsilon>0
small enough, and all
T_{0}>0
large enough, we have for all
t\in\left[T_{0},T\right]
,

\displaystyle\left|\psi\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{3-\sigma_{0}}\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|}{t^{2-\frac{\sigma_{0}}{2}}},

(5.134)

\displaystyle\left|\nabla\psi\right|+\left[\nabla\psi\right]_{\beta}\left(y\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{3-\sigma_{1}}}{t^{2-\sigma_{1}}},

(5.135)

\displaystyle\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{r_{*}}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{3-\sigma_{1}}}{t^{2-\sigma_{1}}},

(5.136)

\displaystyle\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{1-\sigma_{1}}}{t^{1-\sigma_{1}}},

(5.137)

for some
2<r_{*}<\infty
, some absolute constant
C>0
, some small
\sigma_{1}>0
, and
\sigma_{0}>0
defined in (
5.99
).

Proof.

We proceed as in Lemma
5.8
, and use characteristics to represent
\phi
as

\displaystyle\phi\left(z,t\right)=\varepsilon^{-2}\int_{t}^{T}\left(-E\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau\right),\tau\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{\bar{z}}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\cdot\nabla_{\bar{z}}\left(\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\psi\right)\left(\bar{z}\left(\tau\right),\tau\right)\right)d\tau.

To begin with, Corollary
5.11
and Lemma
5.4
give (
5.134
).

Then taking the
L^{r_{1}}
norm of
\phi
, some
2<r_{1}<\infty
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{r_{1}}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{-2}\int_{t}^{T}\left(\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}+\left\|\psi\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}+\left\|\nabla\psi\right\|_{L^{r_{1}}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\right)d\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{-2}\int_{t}^{T}\left(\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}+\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\right)d\tau,

where the second inequality comes from Lemma
5.4
.

Then we interpolate for some
2<r_{2}<r_{1}
, and obtain, for some arbitrarily small
\sigma_{2}>0
,

\displaystyle\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{r_{2}}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\leq C\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}^{1-\sigma_{2}}\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{r_{1}}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}^{\sigma_{2}}.

(5.138)

Then (
5.138
) and Lemma
5.4
give (
5.135
) and (
5.136
).

Finally using (
5.138
) alongside Lemma
5.4
once again, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{-2}\int_{t}^{T}\left(\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}+\left\|\psi\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}+\left\|\nabla\psi\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\right)d\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{-2}\int_{t}^{T}\left(\left\|E\right\|_{\mathscr{Y}_{\infty}}+\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}+\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{r_{2}}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\right)d\tau.

which gives (
5.137
), as required.
∎

5.6
A Priori Estimates on a Projected Linear Problem

In view of (
5.31
), we want to show a priori estimates on the coefficients of projection in the following linear problem

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\phi_{t}+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{b}}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{b}}\left(\phi-\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\psi\right)+E=c_{R0}\left(t\right)U_{R}\left(z_{b},t\right)

\displaystyle+c_{R1}\left(t\right)\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(z_{b},t\right)\partial_{z_{b,1}}\Psi_{R}\left(z_{b},t\right)

\displaystyle+c_{R2}\left(t\right)\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(z_{b},t\right)\partial_{q^{\prime}_{b}}\left(\Psi_{R}\left(z_{b},t\right)-c_{b}\varepsilon\left(z_{b,2}+q^{\prime}_{b}\right)-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega_{b}\right)\ \ \text{in}\ \mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{b}}\times\left[T_{0},T\right],

(5.139a)

\displaystyle\phi(\mathrel{\;\cdot\;},T)=0\ \ \text{in}\ \mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{b}},

(5.139b)

\displaystyle\phi\left(z_{b},t\right)=0\ \ \text{on}\ \partial\mathbb{H}_{-q_{b}^{\prime}}\times\left[T_{0},T\right],

(5.139c)

with the same assumptions on
\phi,a,a_{*},b
, and
E
as in (
5.75
)–(
5.85
).

To obtain a priori estimates for the coefficients of projection in (
5.139
), we also need an extra assumption on
a_{*}
that we justify here.

To begin with, recall when defining our homotopy-dependent operators in Section
5.1
, we defined
\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}
in (
5.25
) in such a way that it will take the place of
a_{*}+a
when we move from the linear problem to the nonlinear problem. Moreover
a_{*}
will correspond to the part of
\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}
not dependent on any unknowns, and
a
will correspond to the remainder.

From Lemmas
4.4
,
4.6
and
4.7
, we know that every term in
\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}
that does not depend on an unknown will have a bound in time of at least
t^{-\left(1+\nu\right)}
, some
\nu>3/4
.

Furthermore, we know that in terms of
\varepsilon
, the largest term from
\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}
will be

\displaystyle-\mathcal{E}_{2}\left(z,\xi\right)+\psi_{R1}\left(z,\xi\right)=\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{2}\right)},

(5.140)

with
\mathcal{E}_{2}
and
\psi_{R1}
defined in (
4.69
) and (
4.78
) respectively. We can decompose the left hand side (
5.140
) into a part of
a_{*}
and a part of
a
. The former is given by

\displaystyle-\mathcal{E}_{2}\left(z,\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)+\psi_{R1}\left(z,\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right).

Noting from (
4.69
) that
\mathcal{E}_{2}
is a harmonic polynomial in
z
, and also noting from (
4.78
) that the right hand side in the equation for
\psi_{R1}
is
\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{E}_{2}+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{3}t^{-2}\right)}
, we compute that

\displaystyle\Delta_{z}\left(-\mathcal{E}_{2}\left(z,\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)+\psi_{R1}\left(z,\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\right)+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(-\mathcal{E}_{2}\left(z,\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)+\psi_{R1}\left(z,\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\right)=\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{3}t^{-2}\right)}.

(5.141)

With (
5.141
) in hand, and recalling Lemma
4.4
and subsequently (
4.50
)–(
4.58
), our additional assumption for
a_{*}
is that

\displaystyle\left|\Delta_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)+\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2+\nu}}{t^{1+\nu}},

(5.142)

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{z}\left(\Delta_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)+\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2+\nu}}{t^{1+\nu}},

(5.143)

for some
\nu>3/4
.

Now we state and prove a priori estimates on
\left(c_{R0},c_{R1},c_{R2}\right)
. We once again suppress dependence of
z
on
b
as in Lemmas
5.8
and
5.9
.

Lemma 5.13
.

Suppose
\phi
solves (
5.139
) with assumptions (
5.75
)–(
5.85
), as well as the size assumption (
5.99
) on
E
from Lemma
5.9
, and the extra assumptions on
a_{*}
(
5.142
)–(
5.143
). Then for all
\varepsilon>0
small enough, and all
T_{0}>0
large enough, we have that for all
t\in\left[T_{0},T\right]
that
c_{R}=\left(c_{R0},c_{R1},c_{R2}\right)
satisfies an equation of the form

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}c_{R}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{F}_{0}\\
\mathfrak{F}_{1}\\
\mathfrak{F}_{2}\end{pmatrix}.

(5.144)

where
\mathfrak{M}
takes the form

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}M+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon\right)}&0&\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon\right)}\\
0&-M+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon\right)}&0\\
\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon\right)}&0&M+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon\right)}\end{pmatrix}

and the right hand side has the form

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{0}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}E\ dz,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}z_{1}E\ dz+\mathcal{O}{\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2+\nu}}{t^{1+\nu}}\right)}\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}

(5.145)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}z_{2}E\ dz+\mathcal{O}{\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2+\nu}}{t^{1+\nu}}\right)}\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)},

where
M>0
is an absolute constant defined in (
1.7
), and
\nu>3/4
.

Proof.

To begin with, we test (
5.139a
) with 1, and integrate on
\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}
. The first two terms on the left hand side disappear due to assumption (
5.78
) and integration by parts respectively. The second term on the right hand side also disappears due to oddness in
z_{1}
. We obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}E\ dz=c_{R0}\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}U_{R}\ dz\right)+c_{R2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(z,t\right)\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}\left(z,t\right)-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)dz\right).

(5.146)

We know from Theorem
2.1
that the coefficient
c_{R0}
is multiplied by on the right hand side of (
5.146
) is of the form
M+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{2}\right)}
for
M
defined in (
1.7
). For the coefficient of
c_{R2}
, from (
4.50
)–(
4.58
), we know that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(z,t\right)\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}\left(z,t\right)-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)dz

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}\partial_{2}\left(\left(\Gamma\right)^{\gamma}_{+}\right)dz+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{2}\right)},

(5.147)

with the last equality being due to the compact support of the integrand on the right hand side of the first line of (
5.147
). Next, we test (
5.139a
) with
z_{1}
, and integrate on
\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}
. The first term on the left hand side will again disappear due to (
5.78
). The first and third terms on the right hand side will disappear because multiplying by
z_{1}
will make the integrands odd in
z_{1}
. We are left with

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}z_{1}E\ dz+\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}z_{1}\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\phi-\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\psi\right)dz

\displaystyle=c_{R1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}z_{1}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(z,t\right)\partial_{z_{1}}\Psi_{R}\left(z,t\right)dz\right).

(5.148)

Similarly to (
5.147
), we can write the factor multiplying
c_{R1}
on the right hand side of (
5.148
) as

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}z_{1}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(z,t\right)\partial_{z_{1}}\Psi_{R}\left(z,t\right)dz

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}z_{1}\partial_{1}\left(\left(\Gamma\right)^{\gamma}_{+}\right)+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle=-M+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{2}\right)},

(5.149)

where we have once again used compact support of the integrand to see that any boundary terms when integrating by parts are
0
, and also used (
2.4
) to get to the last equality.

For the second term on the left hand side of (
5.148
), we use integration by parts to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}z_{1}\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\phi-\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\psi\right)dz

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}\partial_{z_{2}}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(\Delta_{z}\psi+\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\psi\right)dz+\mathcal{O}{\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2+\nu}}{t^{1+\nu}}\right)}\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}.

(5.150)

Here we have once again used the compact support of the integrand to see that all boundary terms are
0
. Then we used that fact that
-\Delta_{z}=\phi
to obtain the first term on the right hand side of (
5.150
). Finally, we used assumption (
5.82
), and Lemma
5.4
on
a
to obtain the last term on the right hand side of (
5.150
).

Concentrating on the first term on the right hand side of (
5.150
), and once again using integration by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}\partial_{z_{2}}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\left(\Delta_{z}\psi+\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\psi\right)dz

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}\psi\partial_{z_{2}}\left(\Delta_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)+\mathscr{W}_{0}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\right)dz=\mathcal{O}{\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2+\nu}}{t^{1+\nu}}\right)}\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)},

(5.151)

where we have used Lemma
5.4
and (
5.143
) to obtain the right hand side of (
5.151
).

Finally, we test (
5.139a
) against
z_{2}
and integrate. Once again the first term on the left hand side disappears due to assumption (
5.78
). The second term on the right hand side disappears because the integrand preserves its oddness in
z_{1}
after multiplication by
z_{2}
. We obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}z_{2}E\ dz+\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}z_{2}\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\phi-\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\psi\right)dz

\displaystyle=c_{R0}\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}z_{2}U_{R}\ dz\right)+c_{R2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}z_{2}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(z,t\right)\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}\left(z,t\right)-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)dz\right).

(5.152)

For the first term on the right hand side of (
5.152
), analogously to (
5.147
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}z_{2}U_{R}\ dz=\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}z_{2}\left(\Gamma\right)^{\gamma}_{+}dz+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon\right)}=\int_{B_{1}\left(0\right)}z_{2}\left(\Gamma\right)^{\gamma}_{+}dz+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon\right)}=\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon\right)}.

(5.153)

To get from the first equality to the second equality, we have used the support of
\left(\Gamma\right)^{\gamma}_{+}
to change integration domains. To get from the second equality to the third equality, we have used the fact that
z_{2}\left(\Gamma\right)^{\gamma}_{+}
is a mode
1
term being integrated over the unit ball, and therefore its integral is
0
.

For the second term on the right hand side of (
5.152
), completely analogously to (
5.149
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}z_{2}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(z,t\right)\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}\left(z,t\right)-c\varepsilon\left(z_{2}+q^{\prime}\right)-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right)dz=M+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon\right)}.

(5.154)

Analogously to (
5.150
)–(
5.151
), we have for the second term on the left hand side of (
5.152
) that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}}z_{2}\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\phi-\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q\right)+a_{*}\right)\psi\right)dz=\mathcal{O}{\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2+\nu}}{t^{1+\nu}}\right)}\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}.

(5.155)

Noting that
\left(c_{R0},c_{R1},c_{R2}\right)
satisfy the linear system given by (
5.146
), (
5.148
), and (
5.152
), and we have estimates on the coefficients and errors for the linear system given by (
5.147
), (
5.149
), (
5.153
), and (
5.154
), and (
5.150
), (
5.151
), and (
5.155
) respectively, we obtain a linear system for
\left(c_{R0},c_{R1},c_{R2}\right)
of the form (
5.144
)–(
5.145
), as required.
∎

5.7
A Priori Estimates on Solutions to the Homotopic Operators

We now obtain a priori estimates to solutions
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
solving (
5.31
) given by

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)=c_{R0}\left(t\right)U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)+c_{R1}\left(t\right)\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\partial_{z_{\lambda,1}}\Psi_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)

\displaystyle+c_{R2}\left(t\right)\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\partial_{q^{\prime}_{\lambda}}\left(\Psi_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)-c_{\lambda}\varepsilon\left(z_{\lambda,2}+q^{\prime}_{\lambda}\right)-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega_{\lambda}\right)\ \ \text{in}\ \mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{\lambda}}\times\left[T_{0},T\right],

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{*R}(\mathrel{\;\cdot\;},T)=0\ \ \text{in}\ \mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{\lambda}},

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)=0\ \ \text{on}\ \partial\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}\times\left[T_{0},T\right],

with
\left(\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)
given, and sufficiently small, and
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
satisfying the orthogonality conditions (
5.9
)–(
5.11
), which correspond to satisfying assumptions (
5.78
) for solutions to our linearized operator.

From the computations (
5.68
)–(
5.73
), we know that we can write (
5.26
) as

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)=\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q_{\lambda}\right)+\lambda\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R}-\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q_{\lambda}\right)+\lambda\mathfrak{A}_{R,\lambda}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\right)\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{R}_{1}+\varepsilon^{2}\dot{\alpha}\left(t\right)U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)-\lambda\varepsilon\dot{\tilde{\xi}}\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\left(U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\right)-\lambda\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\cdot\left(\varepsilon\left(\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\tilde{\xi}-\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\cdot\grad_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{N}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\left[\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right]-\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\left[\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right]\right)\cdot z_{\lambda}^{\perp}\right)+\lambda\tilde{\mathscr{E}}_{R}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)

(5.156)

where, since

\displaystyle\mathscr{V}\left(q_{\lambda}\right)=\Psi_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)-c_{\lambda}\varepsilon\left(z_{\lambda,2}+q^{\prime}_{\lambda}\right)-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega_{\lambda},

from (
5.25
), we have

\displaystyle\lambda\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)=-\lambda\left(\left(\Psi_{L}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)+\varepsilon\left(\dot{p}_{\lambda}-c_{\lambda}\right)z_{\lambda,2}-\varepsilon\dot{q}_{\lambda}z_{\lambda,1}\right)+\psi_{R}+\psi_{*R}+\psi^{out}+\psi^{out}_{*}\right).

(5.157)

We obtain
\mathfrak{A}_{R,\lambda}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)
from collecting all terms from in the sum (
5.157
) that were constructed during the first approximation in Theorem
4.7
and freezing their dependence on
\xi^{\left(\lambda\right)}
at
\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}
. We note that
\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)
can be seen as the part of
\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)
that does not depend on any of
\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)
, and
\mathfrak{A}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)
as the remainder so that
