\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)=\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)-\mathfrak{A}_{R,\lambda}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right).

(5.158)

We can also use (
5.68
)–(
5.73
) to compare the first two terms of (
5.156
) to the first three terms on the right hand side of (
5.26
) and obtain that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{1}=\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q_{\lambda}\right)+\lambda\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\left(\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q_{\lambda}\right)+\lambda\mathfrak{A}_{R,\lambda}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\right)\right)\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\right)

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q_{\lambda}\right)+\lambda\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\lambda\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\right)\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\right)

\displaystyle+\lambda\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)-\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\right)

\displaystyle+\lambda\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\left(\frac{\phi_{R1}}{\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}}\right)\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\right)+\lambda\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\cdot\nabla_{z}\phi_{R2}.

(5.159)

It is important to note that upon Taylor expanding the term multiplying
\tilde{\psi}_{*R}
under the gradient on the first term of the right hand side of (
5.159
), we get something of order
\lambda
. Thus we can write

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{1}=\lambda\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1}.

(5.160)

Having already satisfied the corresponding mass conditions for
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
via the assumptions (
5.9
)–(
5.11
), our choice of
E
means that both
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
and
\phi
satisfy the support conditions (
5.85
). Moreover, for

\displaystyle a_{*}

\displaystyle=\lambda\mathfrak{A}_{R,\lambda}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right),

(5.161)

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle=\lambda\mathfrak{A}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right),

if we work in the parameter space where
a
satisfies (
5.82
) and (
5.80
), we have that
\left(a,a_{*}\right)
satisfy (
5.79
)–(
5.80
) as well (
5.142
)-(
5.143
) via the computations (
5.140
)–(
5.141
). Moreover, with
b=\lambda\tilde{\xi}
with
\tilde{\xi}
satisfying (
5.33a
)–(
5.33b
), we have that
b
satisfies (
5.83
)–(
5.84
).

If we define the error
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}
as being

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\lambda\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1}+\varepsilon^{2}\dot{\alpha}\left(t\right)U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)-\lambda\varepsilon\dot{\tilde{\xi}}\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\left(U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\right)-\lambda\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\cdot\left(\varepsilon\left(\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\tilde{\xi}-\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\lambda\tilde{\mathscr{E}}_{R}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right),

(5.162)

then
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}
satisfies the assumption on its support (
5.85
), as well as the bound (
5.99
) for
\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)
sufficiently small. Then from the information on the support of
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}
defined in (
5.162
), we also obtain that the solution
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
satisfies (
5.85
).

Thus, with the assumptions on
\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)
we have made, (
5.31a
) can be written in the form

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q_{\lambda}\right)+a_{*}+a\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R}-\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q_{\lambda}\right)+a_{*}\right)\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}=c_{R0}\left(t\right)U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)+c_{R1}\left(t\right)\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\partial_{z_{\lambda,1}}\Psi_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)

\displaystyle+c_{R2}\left(t\right)\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\partial_{q^{\prime}_{\lambda}}\left(\Psi_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)-c_{\lambda}\varepsilon\left(z_{\lambda,2}+q^{\prime}_{\lambda}\right)-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega_{\lambda}\right)

with all the assumptions of Lemma
5.13
, and thus we have the following estimates

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{\lambda}}\right)}\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{3-\sigma_{3}}}{t^{2-\sigma_{3}}},\quad\left\|\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{\lambda}}\right)}\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{1-\sigma_{3}}}{t^{1-\sigma_{3}}},

for all
t\in\left[T_{0},T\right]
and some small
\sigma_{3}>0
.

5.8
Fixed Point Formulation and Solution on
\left[T_{0},T\right]

We reformulate the system (
5.14
), (
5.31
), (
5.32
), (
5.34
) as a fixed point problem of the form (
5.35
) for a well chosen Banach space
\mathfrak{X}
. Recall that obtaining
\tilde{\phi}_{*L}
is a matter of reflecting
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
in an odd manner in the original
x_{1}
variable, and thus it is enough to solve the above problem.

Fix a small number
\beta\in\left(0,1\right)
. For
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)
defined on
\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{\lambda}}\times\left[T_{0},T\right]
, we consider the set of functions with support on
B_{3\rho}\left(0\right)
in the
z_{\lambda}
variables defined in (
5.19
) for
\rho>0
defined in (
4.17
) with finite
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{i}
norm, where

\displaystyle\left\|\phi\right\|_{i}\coloneqq\sup_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}\left(t^{2-\beta}\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(B_{3\rho}\left(0\right)\right)}\right)+\sup_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}\left(\varepsilon^{2}t^{1-\beta}\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(B_{3\rho}\left(0\right)\right)}\right).

Next we define the
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{o}
norm for
\psi_{*}^{out}\left(x,t\right)
defined on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\left[T_{0},T\right]
:

\displaystyle\left\|\psi\right\|_{o}\coloneqq\sup_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}\left(t^{3-\beta}\left\|\psi\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)}\right)+\sup_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}\left(t^{3-\beta}\left\|\nabla\psi\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)}\right).

For
\tilde{\psi}_{*R}
related to
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
via (
5.14
), we define
\mathfrak{X}
as the Banach space of all points
\mathfrak{P}=\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)
such that
\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}
,
\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\psi_{*}^{out}
,
\dot{\alpha}
,
\dot{\tilde{\xi}}
all exist and are continuous, with
\tilde{\xi}=\left(\tilde{p},\tilde{q}\right)
, satisfying

\displaystyle\left\|\mathfrak{P}\right\|_{\mathfrak{X}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\|\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\right\|_{i}+\left\|\psi_{*}^{out}\right\|_{o}+\left\|t\tilde{p}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}+\left\|t^{2}\dot{\tilde{p}}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}+\left\|t^{2}\tilde{q}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}+\left\|t^{3}\dot{\tilde{q}}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}

\displaystyle+\left\|t^{2}\alpha\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}+\left\|t^{3}\dot{\alpha}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}<\infty,

with
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
also satisfying
\supp{\tilde{\phi}_{*R}}\subset B_{3\rho}\left(0\right)
and the orthogonality conditions (
5.9
)–(
5.11
).

Then in
\mathfrak{X}
, we define the open set
\mathscr{O}
as the “deformed ball” around
0
in
\mathscr{X}
, that is all
\mathfrak{P}
satisfying

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\right\|_{i}<\varepsilon^{3-3\beta},\quad\left\|\psi_{*}^{out}\right\|_{o}<\varepsilon^{3-3\beta},\quad\left\|t^{2}\alpha\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}+\left\|t^{3}\dot{\alpha}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}<\varepsilon^{3-3\beta},

\displaystyle\left\|t^{2}\dot{\tilde{p}}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}+\left\|t^{2}\tilde{q}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}<\varepsilon^{4-3\beta},\quad\left\|t^{2}\tilde{q}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}+\left\|t^{3}\dot{\tilde{q}}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},T\right]}<\varepsilon^{4-3\beta}.

(5.163)

Remark 5.14
.

We note that by the representation formula,
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
solving (
5.31
) has support depending only on
U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)
and
\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q_{\lambda}\right)+a_{*}\right)
for
a_{*}
defined in (
5.161
). In coordinates
z_{\lambda}
, this means that the only unknown
\supp{\tilde{\phi}_{*R}}
depends on is
\lambda\tilde{\xi}
, and due to (
5.163
), this support is uniformly contained in
B_{3\rho}\left(0\right)
for any
\tilde{\xi}
satisfying (
5.163
), and any
\lambda\in\left[0,1\right]
.

Now we rewrite the system (
5.14
), (
5.31
), (
5.32
), (
5.34
) as a fixed point problem by finding an operator
\mathcal{T}
such that solving the system is equivalent to solving
\mathcal{T}\left(\mathfrak{P},\lambda\right)=\mathfrak{P}
for
\mathfrak{P}\in\mathscr{O}
, and for all
\lambda\in\left[0,1\right]
.

We begin with (
5.31
). For
\hat{a}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)
satisfying
\Delta_{z_{\lambda}}\hat{a}\in L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}\times\left[T_{0},T\right]\right)
, and a bounded function
E\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)
, consider the system

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\phi+\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q_{\lambda}\right)+\hat{a}\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\phi+E=0\ \ \text{in}\ \mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{\lambda}}\times\left[T_{0},T\right]

\displaystyle\phi(\mathrel{\;\cdot\;},T)=0\ \ \text{in}\ \mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}_{\lambda}},

\displaystyle\phi\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)=0\ \ \text{on}\ \partial\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}\times\left[T_{0},T\right].

The representation formula gives us a unique solution
\phi=\mathcal{L}^{-1}\left(\hat{a}\right)\left[E\right]
, where
\mathcal{L}\left(\hat{a}\right)
is the transport operator defined by the first two terms on the left hand side on the first line above, along with the terminal condition at
T
and the boundary condition at
\partial\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}
.

Then (
5.31
) can be written in the form

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{*R}=\mathcal{T}^{in}_{\lambda}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right),

(5.165)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{in}_{\lambda}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\coloneqq\mathcal{L}^{-1}\left(\lambda\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}\right)\left[\hat{\mathscr{E}}_{R}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\right],

and
\hat{\mathscr{E}}_{R}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\tilde{\xi}\right)
is given by

\displaystyle\hat{\mathscr{E}}_{R}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)

\displaystyle=\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\left(U_{R}+\lambda\phi_{R}\right)\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)+\varepsilon^{2}\dot{\alpha}\left(t\right)U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)

\displaystyle-\lambda\varepsilon\dot{\tilde{\xi}}\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\left(U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\right)-\lambda\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\cdot\left(\varepsilon\left(\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\tilde{\xi}-\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\psi^{out}_{*}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\lambda\tilde{\mathscr{E}}_{R}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)-c_{R0}\left(t\right)U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)-c_{R1}\left(t\right)\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\partial_{z_{\lambda,1}}\Psi_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)

\displaystyle-c_{R2}\left(t\right)\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)\partial_{q^{\prime}_{\lambda}}\left(\Psi_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},t\right)-c_{\lambda}\varepsilon\left(z_{\lambda,2}+q^{\prime}_{\lambda}\right)-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega_{\lambda}\right),

where we recall the definition of
\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}
from (
5.25
), and of
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}_{R}
from (

). We note that the only dependence of
\hat{\mathscr{E}}_{R}
on
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
comes through
\tilde{\psi}_{*R}
, via the relationship
(

)
.

Next we recall that (
5.34
) consists of (
5.34a
)–(

) given by

\displaystyle\Delta\psi_{*}^{out}+\lambda\left(\psi_{*R}\Delta\eta^{(R)}_{K}+2\nabla\eta^{(R)}_{K}\cdot\nabla\psi_{*R}-\psi_{*L}\Delta\eta^{(L)}_{K}+2\nabla\eta^{(L)}_{K}\cdot\nabla\psi_{*L}-E_{2}^{out}\left(\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-0\ \ \text{in}\ \mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\times\left[T_{0},T\right],

\displaystyle\psi^{out}_{*}\left(x_{1},0,t\right)

\displaystyle=0,\ \ t\in\left[T_{0},T\right],

where is defined in

(

), and we note that
\psi_{*L}

of

. Then due to the compact support (for fixed

) and oddness in

of all terms on the left hand side apart from

, as well as the boundary condition for

, (

) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\psi^{out}_{*}=\mathcal{T}^{out}_{\lambda}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right),

(5.166)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{out}_{\lambda}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\lambda\left(\psi_{*R}\Delta\eta^{(R)}_{K}+2\nabla\eta^{(R)}_{K}\cdot\nabla\psi_{*R}-\psi_{*L}\Delta\eta^{(L)}_{K}+2\nabla\eta^{(L)}_{K}\cdot\nabla\psi_{*L}-E_{2}^{out}\right)\right),

where the inverse laplacian is understood to be the standard one on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
with
0
boundary conditions at
\left\{x_{2}=0\right\}
.

For the equations (
5.32
) we first integrate (

) on
\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}
. If (

) is satisfied, then we obtain

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)dz_{\lambda}\right)\dot{\alpha}

\displaystyle+\lambda\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{1}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{2}+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)^{+}\mathcal{R}^{*}_{3}\right)\left(z_{\lambda},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)dz_{\lambda},

.

Next, we integrate (

against

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)dz_{\lambda}\right)\left(\lambda\dot{\tilde{p}}+\left[\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{*}\right)\left(\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\right]_{1}\right)=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)dz_{\lambda}\right)\left[\left(\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)-\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{*}\right)\right)\left(\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\right]_{1}

\displaystyle-\lambda\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}z_{\lambda,1}\left(\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\cdot\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\psi^{out}_{*}\right)dz_{\lambda}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}z_{\lambda,1}\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q_{\lambda}\right)+\lambda\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\right)\right)dz_{\lambda}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}z_{\lambda,1}\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q_{\lambda}\right)+\lambda\mathfrak{a}_{R,\lambda}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\right)\cdot\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}\left(\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q_{\lambda}\right)+\lambda\mathfrak{A}_{R,\lambda}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)\right)\tilde{\psi}_{*R}\right)\right)dz_{\lambda}

\displaystyle-\lambda\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}z_{\lambda,1}\left(\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1}+\tilde{\mathscr{E}}_{R}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\right)dz_{\lambda},

(5.168)

where we recall the construction of

from Section

from (

. Thus we can rewrite

)
as

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)dz_{\lambda}\right)\left(\lambda\dot{\tilde{p}}+\left[\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{*}\right)\left(\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\right]_{1}\right)=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)dz_{\lambda}\right)\left[\left(\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)-\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{*}\right)\right)\left(\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\right]_{1}

\displaystyle-\lambda\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}z_{\lambda,1}\left(\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\cdot\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\psi^{out}_{*}\right)dz_{\lambda}

\displaystyle-\lambda\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}z_{\lambda,1}\mathfrak{R}_{2}\ dz_{\lambda}-\lambda\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}z_{\lambda,1}\left(\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1}+\tilde{\mathscr{E}}_{R}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\right)dz_{\lambda},

(5.169)

for
\mathfrak{R}_{2}
satisfying the same estimates as the integral on the third line of (
5.169
) inferred from Lemma

.

We similarly integrate (
5.156
) on
\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}
against
z_{\lambda,2}

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}z_{\lambda,2}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)dz_{\lambda}\right)\dot{\alpha}+\varepsilon\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)dz_{\lambda}\right)\left(\lambda\dot{\tilde{q}}+\left[\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{*}\right)\left(\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\right]_{2}\right)=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\left(\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)dz_{\lambda}\right)\left[\left(\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{0}+\xi_{1}\right)-\mathscr{A}\left(\xi_{*}\right)\right)\left(\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\right]_{2}

\displaystyle-\lambda\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}z_{\lambda,2}\left(\nabla_{z_{\lambda}}U_{R}\left(z_{\lambda},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\cdot\nabla^{\perp}_{z_{\lambda}}\psi^{out}_{*}\right)dz_{\lambda}

\displaystyle-\lambda\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}z_{\lambda,2}\mathfrak{R}_{2}\ dz_{\lambda}-\lambda\int_{\mathbb{H}_{-q_{\lambda}^{\prime}}}z_{\lambda,2}\left(\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1}+\tilde{\mathscr{E}}_{R}\left(\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi^{out}_{*},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\right)dz_{\lambda},

(5.170)

where once again the integral on the third line should be thought of as in Section
5.6
. Then, similarly to Lemma
5.13
, the system of ODEs defined by (
5.167
), (
5.169
), and (
5.170
) can be written as

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathfrak{M}}\mathfrak{B}^{-1}\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathfrak{B}\begin{pmatrix}\alpha\\
\lambda\tilde{p}\\
\lambda\tilde{q}\end{pmatrix}\right)=\hat{\mathfrak{R}}_{3}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)+\lambda\hat{\mathfrak{R}}_{4}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right),

(5.171)

where
\hat{\mathfrak{R}}_{3}
is identically
0
at
\lambda=0
.

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathfrak{M}}=\begin{pmatrix}M+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{2}\right)}&0&0\\
0&M+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{2}\right)}&0\\
\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{3}\right)}&0&M+\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{2}\right)}\end{pmatrix},

and, in block matrix form,
\mathfrak{B}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}=\begin{pmatrix}1&\textbf{0}\\
\textbf{0}&e^{\mathscr{B}},\end{pmatrix}

and
\mathscr{B}
along with its exponential are defined in (

) and (

) respectively. Moreover,
\hat{\mathfrak{R}}
on the right hand side of (

) is the right hand side of (
5.167
)–(
5.170
) in vector form, and consist of terms that are either linear in the unknown
\left(\alpha,\tilde{p},\tilde{q}\right)
with small coefficient, or nonlinear in these unknowns, or small error terms.

Thus (
5.32
) is equivalent to the fixed point problem

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\alpha\\
\lambda\tilde{p}\\
\lambda\tilde{q}\end{pmatrix}=\mathcal{T}^{ode}_{\lambda}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right),

(5.172)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{ode}_{\lambda}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)\coloneqq\mathfrak{B}^{-1}\int_{t}^{T}\mathfrak{B}\tilde{\mathfrak{M}}^{-1}\left(\hat{\mathfrak{R}}_{3}+\lambda\hat{\mathfrak{R}}_{4}\right)\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)ds.

(5.173)

Then if we define the new operators

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{T}}^{out}_{\lambda}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}^{out}_{\lambda}\left(\mathcal{T}^{in}_{\lambda}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right),\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{T}}^{ode}_{\lambda}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}^{ode}_{\lambda}\left(\mathcal{T}^{in}_{\lambda}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right),\alpha,\psi_{*}^{out},\lambda\tilde{\xi}\right),

we see that the system consisting of (
5.165
), (
5.166
), and (
5.172
) on the deformed open ball
\mathscr{O}
defined in (
5.163
) is equivalent to the fixed point problem

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}=\tilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}\left(\mathfrak{P}\right),\quad\mathfrak{P}\in\mathscr{O},

(5.174)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}\left(\mathfrak{P}\right)=\left(\mathcal{T}^{in}_{\lambda}\left(\mathfrak{P}\right),\tilde{\mathcal{T}}^{out}_{\lambda}\left(\mathfrak{P}\right),\tilde{\mathcal{T}}^{ode}_{\lambda}\left(\mathfrak{P}\right)\right).

(5.175)

Lemma 5.15
.

The operator
\tilde{\mathcal{T}}\colon\mathscr{O}\times\left[0,1\right]\rightarrow\mathfrak{X}
defined by
\tilde{\mathcal{T}}\left(\cdot,\lambda\right)\coloneqq\tilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}
in (
5.175
) is compact, and for all
\lambda\in\left[0,1\right]
, is a degree
1
operator.

Proof.

Since we are trying to construct a solution to (
5.174
) on a finite time interval
\left[T_{0},T\right]
, the proof follows is analogous to the proof given in
[
10
]
. The extra detail is due to the fact that we must obtain a solution for
\lambda\tilde{\xi}
instead of
\tilde{\xi}
. However, the form of the right hand side in (
5.173
) means we have compactness of the operator as well as the desired information on the degree for all
\lambda\in\left[0,1\right]
.
∎

The degree of the operator at
\lambda=1
exactly means we have a unique solution in
\mathscr{O}
to (
5.14
), (
5.31
), (
5.32
), (
5.34
) at
\lambda=1
, and therefore to (
1.1
) with the desired estimates in
\varepsilon
and
t
on
\left[T_{0},T\right]
, for all
T
large enough, as desired.

6
Proof of Theorem
1.1

We have a sequence of solutions to (
1.1
) that is identified with the sequence of solutions with estimates coming from (
5.163
) on
\left[T_{0},T\right]
,
\mathfrak{P}^{T}=\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T},\alpha^{T},\psi_{*}^{out,T},\tilde{\xi}^{T}\right)
to (
5.14
), (
5.31
), (
5.32
), (
5.34
) at
\lambda=1
that we constructed in Section
5.8
. These solutions satisfy
\mathfrak{P}^{T}\left(T\right)=0
.

To obtain the solution on
\left[T_{0},\infty\right)
with the properties claimed in Theorem
1.1
, we want to apply Arzelà–Ascoli. We begin with
\alpha^{T}
and
\tilde{\xi}^{T}
. For each
T
, we can extend
\alpha^{T}
and
\tilde{\xi}^{T}
to
\left[T_{0},\infty\right)
by extending as
0
for all
t>T
. We obtain a family of continuous functions with
L^{\infty}\left(\left[T_{0},\infty\right)\right)
derivatives
\dot{\alpha}^{T}
and
\dot{\tilde{\xi}}^{T}
by extending both
\dot{\alpha}^{T}
and
\dot{\tilde{\xi}}^{T}
by
0
for
t>T
.

Uniform boundedness for
(\alpha^{T},\tilde{\xi}^{T})
is clear due to the fact they are in
\mathscr{O}
defined in (
5.163
). Moreover, clearly the only the possible discontinuities for
\dot{\alpha}^{T}
and
\dot{\tilde{\xi}}^{T}
are at
T
, and so the absolute continuity of
\alpha^{T}
and
\tilde{\xi}^{T}
allows us to represent them on
\left[T_{0},\infty\right)
using Fundamental Theorem of Calculus. Since we also have uniform boundedness of their derivatives, we have uniform equicontiniuity. Thus Arzelà-Ascoli gives uniform convergence on compact subsets of
\left[T_{0},\infty\right)
. By a diagonal argument applied to the sequence of intervals
\left[T_{0},T_{0}+1\right]
,
\left[T_{0},T_{0}+2\right]\dots
, we obtain a sequence
(\alpha^{T_{n}},\tilde{\xi}^{T_{n}})\to(\alpha,\tilde{\xi})
that converges locally uniformly on
\left[T_{0},\infty\right)
, and satisfies bounds

\displaystyle\left\|t^{2}\alpha\right\|_{\left[T_{0},\infty\right)}\leq\varepsilon^{3-\sigma},\quad\left\|t\tilde{p}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},\infty\right)}\leq\varepsilon^{4-\sigma},\quad\left\|t^{2}\tilde{q}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},\infty\right)}\leq\varepsilon^{4-\sigma},

some small
\sigma>0
.

For equicontinuity in
z
for
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T}
for
t_{0}\in\left[T_{0},T_{0}+1\right]
, we first recall that
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T}
solves the equation

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T}\left(z,t\right)+\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q^{T}\right)+\mathfrak{a}_{R}^{T}\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T}-\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q^{T}\right)+\mathfrak{A}_{R}^{T}\right)\tilde{\psi}_{*R}^{T}\right)+\mathfrak{E}_{1}^{T}=0,

where
z
is the coordinate coming from (
5.19
) at
\lambda=1
,
\mathfrak{a}_{R}^{T}
is defined in (
5.25
) for
\lambda=1
,
\mathfrak{A}_{R}^{T}
is defined in (
5.158
) for
\lambda=1
,
\mathfrak{E}_{1}^{T}
is defined in (
5.162
) for
\lambda=1
, and
q^{T}=q_{0}+q_{1}+\tilde{q}^{T}
. For ease of notation, define

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{1}^{T}=-\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q^{T}\right)+\mathfrak{a}_{R}^{T}\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\mathscr{W}_{1}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q^{T}\right)+\mathfrak{A}_{R}^{T}\right)\tilde{\psi}_{*R}^{T}\right)+\mathfrak{E}_{1}^{T}.

Then as in (
5.88
), let
t\in\left[T_{0},T\right]
, and define characteristics
\bar{z}\left(\tau,t,z\right)
by the ODE problem

\displaystyle\frac{d\bar{z}^{T}}{d\tau}=\varepsilon^{-2}\nabla^{\perp}_{\bar{z}^{T}}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q^{T}\right)+\mathfrak{a}_{R}^{T}\right)\left(\bar{z}^{T}\left(\tau,t,z\right),\tau\right),\ \ \ \tau\in\left[t,T\right],

(6.1a)

\displaystyle\bar{z}\left(t,t,z\right)=z.

(6.1b)

We note that
\alpha^{T}
and
\xi^{T}
are both a uniformly bounded family of constant functions with respect to the space variables. Then Corollary (
5.12
) gives a Log-Lipschitz bound on
\nabla^{\perp}_{\bar{z}}\left(\mathscr{V}\left(q^{T}\right)+\mathfrak{a}_{R}^{T}\right)
, which along with (
6.1a
)–(
6.1b
), gives

\displaystyle\left|\bar{z}_{1}^{T}\left(\tau,t,z\right)-\bar{z}_{2}^{T}\left(\tau,t,z\right)\right|\leq C\varepsilon^{-2}e^{e^{\tau}}\left|z_{1}-z_{2}\right|,\quad\tau\in\left[t,T\right],

(6.2)

some constant
C>0
. We stress here that for the purposes of passage to the limit
T\to\infty
,
\varepsilon>0
is fixed, and can be considered a constant when checking equicontinuity of these terms. We will obtain the bounds in terms of
\varepsilon
claimed in Theorem
1.1
as the a priori bounds shown in Section
5
will be preserved by passage to the limit.

With
T_{n}
defined in the sequence
\left(\alpha^{T_{n}},\tilde{\xi}^{T_{n}}\right)
above, we define for each pair of points
z_{1},z_{2}\in B_{3\rho}\left(0\right)
,
T_{*}\left(z_{1},z_{2}\right)
by the identity

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-4}e^{e^{T_{*}}}\left|z_{1}-z_{2}\right|=\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{T_{*}}\right)^{1-\sigma}.

(6.3)

Then suppose for
\delta>0
small,
\left|z_{1}-z_{2}\right|<\varepsilon^{5-\sigma}e^{-2e^{\delta^{-\frac{1}{1-\sigma}}}}
. If
T_{n}\leq T_{*}
, using (
6.2
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}\left(z_{1},t_{0}\right)-\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}\left(z_{2},t_{0}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-2}\int_{t_{0}}^{T_{n}}\left|\tilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{1}^{T_{n}}\left(\bar{z}_{1}^{T_{n}},\tau\right)-\tilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{1}^{T_{n}}\left(\bar{z}_{2}^{T_{n}},\tau\right)\right|d\tau\leq C\varepsilon^{-4}e^{e^{T_{n}}}\left|z_{1}-z_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{-4}e^{e^{T_{*}}}\left|z_{1}-z_{2}\right|.

(6.4)

If instead
T_{*}<T_{n}
,

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}\left(z_{1},t_{0}\right)-\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}\left(z_{2},t_{0}\right)\right|=\varepsilon^{-2}\int_{t_{0}}^{T_{n}}\left|\tilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{1}^{T_{n}}\left(\bar{z}_{1}^{T_{n}},\tau\right)-\tilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{1}^{T_{n}}\left(\bar{z}_{2}^{T_{n}},\tau\right)\right|d\tau

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-2}\int_{t_{0}}^{T_{*}}\left|\tilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{1}^{T_{n}}\left(\bar{z}_{1}^{T_{n}},\tau\right)-\tilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{1}^{T_{n}}\left(\bar{z}_{2}^{T_{n}},\tau\right)\right|d\tau+\varepsilon^{-2}\int_{T_{*}}^{T_{n}}\left|\tilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{1}^{T_{n}}\left(\bar{z}_{1}^{T_{n}},\tau\right)-\tilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{1}^{T_{n}}\left(\bar{z}_{2}^{T_{n}},\tau\right)\right|d\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\varepsilon^{-4}e^{e^{T_{*}}}\left|z_{1}-z_{2}\right|+\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{T_{*}}\right)^{1-\sigma}\right),

(6.5)

due to the fact that
\tilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{1}^{T_{n}}\left(t\right)=\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{3-\sigma}t^{-\left(2-\sigma\right)}\right)}
on
\left[T_{0},T_{n}\right]
,
\sigma>0
small. Then combining (
6.4
) and (
6.5
), for all
T_{n}\geq t_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}\left(z_{1},t_{0}\right)-\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}\left(z_{2},t_{0}\right)\right|\leq C\left(\varepsilon^{-4}e^{e^{T_{*}}}\left|z_{1}-z_{2}\right|+\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{T_{*}}\right)^{1-\sigma}\right)=2C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{T_{*}}\right)^{1-\sigma}.

(6.6)

Next we note that the definition of
T_{*}
in (
6.3
) implies the bound

\displaystyle T_{*}\geq\log{\log{\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{5-\sigma}{2}}}{\left|z_{1}-z_{2}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)}}.

Applying this to (
6.6
), and using that
\left|z_{1}-z_{2}\right|<\varepsilon^{5-\sigma}e^{-2e^{\delta^{-\frac{1}{1-\sigma}}}}
, we get that

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}\left(z_{1},t_{0}\right)-\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}\left(z_{2},t_{0}\right)\right|\leq 2C\varepsilon^{1-\sigma}\delta,

which gives equicontinuity in
z
for
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}
at any
t_{0}\in\left[T_{0},T\right]
, as
\supp{\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}}\subset B_{3\rho}\left(0\right)
for any
T_{n}
.

Given
t_{1},t_{2}\in\left[T_{0},T_{0}+1\right]
, for all
T_{n}
large enough, we have the estimate

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}\left(t_{1}\right)-\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}\left(t_{2}\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}=\varepsilon^{-2}\|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{1}^{T_{n}}\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\leq C\varepsilon^{1-\sigma}\left|t_{1}-t_{2}\right|,

(6.7)

once again due to the fact that
\tilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{1}^{T_{n}}\left(t\right)=\mathcal{O}{\left(\varepsilon^{3-\sigma}t^{-\left(2-\sigma\right)}\right)}
on
\left[T_{0},T_{n}\right]
. This gives equicontinuity on
\left[T_{0},T_{0}+1\right]
for
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}
for fixed
z
.

Combining (
6.3
)–(
6.7
), we obtain uniform equicontinuity in
\left(z,t\right)
for
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}
. Uniform boundedness for this family is clear, so we obtain a subsequence, which we relabel as
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}
, such that
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}\to\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
uniformly on
B_{3\rho}\left(0\right)\times\left[T_{0},T_{0}+1\right]
. Applying the diagonal argument in
t
on the family of intervals
\left[T_{0},T_{0}+1\right]
,
\left[T_{0},T_{0}+2\right]\dots
, we obtain, upon relabeling once more, that
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}\to\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
uniformly in space and locally uniformly in time on
B_{3\rho}\left(0\right)\times\left[T_{0},\infty\right)
.

Note that due to Corollary
5.12
, as well as convergence of
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}\to\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
in
L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)
, we have the estimate

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{1-\sigma}}{t^{1-\sigma}}.

Next, once again due to their compact supports, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\phi}_{*R}-\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\leq C\left\|\tilde{\phi}_{*R}-\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)},

which gives convergence in
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)
. Along with Corollary
5.11
, this implies

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\phi}_{*R}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{H}_{-q^{\prime}}\right)}\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{3-\sigma}}{t^{2-\sigma}}.

The convergence
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}\to\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
uniformly in space and locally uniformly in time immediately implies local uniform convergence in
\left(z,t\right)
for
\tilde{\psi}_{*R}^{T_{n}}\to\tilde{\psi}_{*R}
. Along with local uniform convergence in time for
\left(\alpha^{T_{n}},\tilde{\xi}^{T_{n}}\right)\to\left(\alpha,\tilde{\xi}\right)
, we get local uniform convergence in
\left(x,t\right)
for
\psi_{*}^{out,T_{n}}\to\psi_{*}^{out}
due to the fact that it solves (
5.166
) for
\lambda=1
. The desired regularity and bounds for
\psi^{out}_{*}
and
\tilde{\psi}_{*R}
follow from the estimates for
\tilde{\phi}_{*R}
.

We now have that
\left(\dot{\alpha}^{T_{n}},\dot{\tilde{\xi}}^{T_{n}}\right)
satisfies the system (
5.167
)–(
5.170
) on
\left[T_{0},T_{n}\right]
with
\lambda=1
, with right hand sides depending on
\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}},\tilde{\psi}_{*R}^{T_{n}},\psi^{out,T_{n}}_{*},\alpha^{T_{n}},\tilde{\xi}^{T_{n}}\right)
. Then we set
\left(\dot{\alpha},\dot{\tilde{\xi}}\right)
as solving the same system on
\left[T_{0},\infty\right)
with right hand side instead depending on
\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\psi^{out}_{*},\alpha,\tilde{\xi}\right)
. Then the convergence

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R}^{T_{n}},\tilde{\psi}_{*R}^{T_{n}},\psi^{out,T_{n}}_{*},\alpha^{T_{n}},\tilde{\xi}^{T_{n}}\right)\to\left(\tilde{\phi}_{*R},\tilde{\psi}_{*R},\psi^{out}_{*},\alpha,\tilde{\xi}\right)

locally uniformly in time gives us
\left(\dot{\alpha}^{T_{n}},\dot{\tilde{\xi}}^{T_{n}}\right)\to\left(\dot{\alpha},\dot{\tilde{\xi}}\right)
locally uniformly in time, from which we get that
\left(\alpha,\tilde{\xi}\right)
is continuously differentiable, as well as obtaining the bounds

\displaystyle\left\|t^{2}\dot{\tilde{p}}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},\infty\right)}\leq\varepsilon^{4-\sigma},\quad\left\|t^{3}\dot{\tilde{q}}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},\infty\right)}\leq\varepsilon^{4-\sigma},\quad\left\|t^{3}\dot{\alpha}\right\|_{\left[T_{0},\infty\right)}\leq\varepsilon^{3-\sigma}.

Thus we have a solution to
(
1.1
)
with the properties stated in Theorem
1.1
, as desired.

Appendix A
Linearisation Around the Power Nonlinearity Vortex

Here we record some results about the linear operator attained from linearising (
1.6
) around the solution
\Gamma
defined in (
1.6
). The first is a non degeneracy result on the linearised operator. Throughout this appendix, let
\gamma>3
. Recall the definition of
\Gamma
from (
1.6
).

Lemma A.1
.

Suppose
\varphi\in L^{\infty}
is a solution to

\displaystyle\Delta\varphi+\gamma\Gamma^{\gamma-1}_{+}\varphi=0

on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Then
\varphi
lives in the span of
\partial_{1}\Gamma
and
\partial_{2}\Gamma
:

\displaystyle\varphi\in\left\langle\partial_{1}\Gamma,\partial_{2}\Gamma\right\rangle_{\mathbb{R}}.

The proof of this result is given in
[
9
]
. A slight modification of the proof given in
[
9
]
for the above lemma also gives the following result:

Lemma A.2
.

Suppose
\varphi\in L^{\infty}
is a solution to

\displaystyle\Delta\varphi+\gamma\Gamma^{\gamma-1}_{+}\varphi=\kappa\gamma\Gamma^{\gamma-1}_{+}\varphi

on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, with
\kappa\in(0,1)
a constant. Then
\varphi\equiv 0
.

Next, we describe the behaviour of solutions to the linear problem given by

\displaystyle\Delta\psi+\gamma\Gamma^{\gamma-1}_{+}\psi=\gamma\Gamma^{\gamma-1}_{+}g,

(A.1)

where
g
is a
C^{1}
function. We assume that
g
has no mode
0
term in its expansion, and look for solutions
\psi
that accordingly have no mode
0
terms either. Expanding
\psi
and
g
in Fourier modes, we have

\displaystyle\psi\left(r,\theta\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left(\uppsi_{c,k}\left(r\right)\cos{k\theta}+\uppsi_{s,k}\left(r\right)\sin{k\theta}\right),

\displaystyle g\left(r,\theta\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{c,k}\left(r\right)\cos{k\theta}+\mathfrak{g}_{s,k}\left(r\right)\sin{k\theta}\right).

Then we have the following lemma on how the coefficients
\uppsi_{c,k}
and
\uppsi_{s,k}
behave at the origin and as
r\to\infty
.

Lemma A.3
.

The coefficients
\uppsi_{c}\left(r\right)
and
\uppsi_{s}\left(r\right)
are in
L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)
and behave as

\displaystyle r^{k}\left(1+o\left(1\right)\right)

(A.2)

as
r\to 0
for
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
. Moreover
\uppsi_{c}\left(r\right)/r^{k}
and
\uppsi_{s}\left(r\right)/r^{k}
are both
C^{1}
.

Using the above results, we define
\varrho_{k}\left(r\right)
such that

\displaystyle\psi\left(r,\theta\right)=\varrho_{k}\left(r\right)\left(\cos{k\theta}+\sin{k\theta}\right)

(A.3)

is the solution to (
A.1
) with
g
given by

\displaystyle g\left(r,\theta\right)=r^{k}\left(\cos{k\theta}+\sin{k\theta}\right).

Appendix B
Spectral Theory of the Vortex Pair

In this appendix we record relevant facts related to the spectral theory of the linearised operator around the vortex pair solution exhibited in Theorem
2.1
. Recall that the vortex pair is a solution to the semilinear elliptic problem (
2.1
) in the
x
variable on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with odd symmetry in
x_{2}
and even symmetry in
x_{1}
. Thus, the linearized operator is given by
\Delta+f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}
. For our purposes it will be more convenient to analyse the eigenvalues and eigenfunctions of a different, but related linear operator.

Concretely, in
y=\varepsilon x
variables, let
\mathcal{H}\coloneqq\left\{h\in L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+},\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}dy\right):h\left(y_{1},0\right)=0\right\}
. Define the linear operator
\mathfrak{T}
by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}:\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle\mapsto\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}h\right).

As
\mathfrak{T}
is a compact self-adjoint operator on
\mathcal{H}
we get an eigenbasis
e_{j}
with eigenvalues
\mu_{j}\left(\varepsilon\right)
that, for fixed
\varepsilon
, are positive, and tend to
0
as
j\to\infty
:

\displaystyle\left(-\Delta\right)^{-1}\left(\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}e_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\mu_{j}\left(\varepsilon\right)e_{j},

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}e_{i}e_{j}

\displaystyle=\delta_{ij}.

(B.1)

We know the eigenfunction
e_{0}
is related to the mass and the corresponding largest eigenvalue has
\mu_{0}\left(\varepsilon\right)\to\infty
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle e_{1}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{1}\partial_{1}\Psi_{R},

(B.2)

\displaystyle e_{2}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{2}\partial_{q^{\prime}}\left(\Psi_{R}-c\varepsilon y_{2}-\left|\log{\varepsilon}\right|\Omega\right),

(B.3)

where
\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{2}
are normalization constants so that
e_{1}
and
e_{2}
satisfy (
B.1
). Then, since
e_{1}
and
e_{2}
are in the kernel of
\Delta+\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}
for all
\varepsilon
, we know that
\mu_{1}\left(\varepsilon\right)=\mu_{2}\left(\varepsilon\right)=1
for all
\varepsilon
.

For all eigenvalues
\mu_{j}
,
j\geq 3
, we have a uniform bound as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

Lemma B.1
.

There exists a fixed
\delta\in\left(0,1\right)
and
\varepsilon_{0}>0
small enough, such that for all
j\geq 3
and
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mu_{j}\left(\varepsilon\right)\in[0,\delta].

Proof.

Suppose first that there is a
j\geq 3
with a convergent subsequence
\mu_{j}\left(\varepsilon_{n}\right)\to\bar{\mu}_{j}\in[1,\infty)
. Then there is a corresponding subsequence
e_{j}\left(\varepsilon_{n}\right)
converging locally uniformly to some
\bar{e}_{j}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
solving

\displaystyle\Delta\bar{e}_{j}+\gamma\Gamma^{\gamma-1}_{+}\bar{e}_{j}=\kappa\gamma\Gamma^{\gamma-1}_{+}\bar{e}_{j},

(B.4)

for some
\kappa\in[0,1)
. By the boundary condition on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
that all elements of
\mathcal{H}
satisfy, which
\bar{e}_{j}
will inherit, we can upgrade this equality to
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. From (
B.1
) for
i=j
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\gamma\Gamma^{\gamma-1}_{+}\left|\bar{e}_{j}\right|^{2}=1.

(B.5)

Since
j\geq 3
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\left(f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right)^{+}e_{i}e_{j}

\displaystyle=0,\ \ i=1,2.

In the limit as
\varepsilon_{n}\to 0
, from (
B.2
)–(
B.3
), this implies that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\Gamma^{\gamma-1}_{+}\bar{e}_{j}\partial_{i}w=0,\ \ i=1,2.

In particular this implies that
w\notin\left\langle\partial_{1}\Gamma,\partial_{2}\Gamma\right\rangle_{\mathbb{R}}
, which along with Lemmas
A.1
and
A.2
and the fact that
\bar{e}_{j}
solves (
B.4
) with
\kappa\in[0,1)
, implies that
\bar{e}_{j}\equiv 0
. This directly contradicts (
B.5
).

Thus we are left to rule out the possibility that there is a subsequence
\mu_{j}\left(\varepsilon_{n}\right)\to\infty
, some
j\geq 3
. However, this would imply that there is a corresponding subsequence
e_{j}\left(\varepsilon_{n}\right)
converging locally uniformly to a constant, which is also true of
e_{0}
. Therefore, taking the limit
\varepsilon_{n}\to 0
in the orthogonality relation (
B.1
) for
i=0
gives

\displaystyle\kappa_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\Gamma^{\gamma-1}_{+}=0,

(B.6)

where
\kappa_{0}
is some constant, necessarily nonzero due to the fact that both
e_{0}
and
e_{j}
have norm
1
for all
\varepsilon
by (
B.1
). Then since (
B.6
) is also clearly a contradiction, we have the desired result.
∎
